# PART 4: RAW RESEARCH NOTES

Ten researcher note files. Each has a takeaway, cited findings with URLs, inferences, and gaps. Treat gaps sections as the verification to-do list.

### Note: federal_money_and_pacs.md

_Folder: Byron Donalds money and donor network_

### Byron Donalds: Federal Campaign Finance History and PAC Network (2020 through Oct 2026)

Scope note: This file covers the federal layer (Byron Donalds for Congress, his leadership PACs, joint fundraising committees, outside spending in FL-19 races, FEC enforcement). Where state committee figures (Friends of Byron Donalds PAC, the governor race) surfaced in the same sources, they are recorded in a separate section at the end so the tracker can be loaded, but a separate researcher owns that layer.

Access note: OpenSecrets pages were blocked by the fetch tool and the FEC API was blocked by the proxy, so cycle totals come from FEC.gov committee and candidate pages and ProPublica FEC Itemizer. Industry breakdowns per cycle from OpenSecrets could not be retrieved and are listed as a gap.

#### Committee map (FEC IDs, treasurer, registration dates)

##### Takeaway
Every federal Donalds committee is run by treasurer Bradley T. Crate of Red Curve Solutions, Beverly, MA. He controls one principal campaign committee, two leadership PACs (a second one registered December 2024), and one joint fundraising committee whose participants include the NRCC and the Republican Party of Florida.

##### Cited Findings
- Byron Donalds for Congress, FEC ID C00733329, principal campaign committee for candidate H2FL14186; Donalds filed his Statement of Candidacy for FL-19 on January 6, 2020, [FEC candidate page](https://www.fec.gov/data/candidate/H2FL14186/); [MUR 7783 First General Counsel's Report](https://www.fec.gov/files/legal/murs/7783/7783_13.pdf)
- Treasurer of Byron Donalds for Congress: Bradley T. Crate, [ProPublica Itemizer, 2024 cycle](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00733329/2024); [FEC MUR 7783 page](https://www.fec.gov/data/legal/matter-under-review/7783/)
- Proven Conservatives PAC, FEC ID C00755728, designated Leadership PAC, registered August 20, 2020, listed by ProPublica as "LEADERSHIP PAC - DONALDS, BYRON", treasurer Bradley T. Crate, [FEC committee page](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00755728/); [ProPublica Itemizer 2022](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00755728/2022)
- Proven Conservatives PAC - Federal, FEC ID C00894147, labeled Leadership PAC, Quarterly PAC Qualified, registered December 19, 2024, [FEC committee page](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00894147/)
- Byron Donalds Victory Fund, FEC ID C00760108, joint fundraising committee, original Form 1 filed October 1, 2020; amended Form 1 filed December 19, 2024 and again March 23, 2026; treasurer and custodian of records Bradley T. Crate, c/o Red Curve Solutions, 100 Cummings Center Ste 306-P, Beverly MA 01915, [FEC committee page](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00760108/); [FEC filings list](https://docquery.fec.gov/cgi-bin/forms/C00760108/); [Form 1, March 23, 2026](https://docquery.fec.gov/cgi-bin/forms/C00760108/1955929/)
- Byron Donalds Victory Fund participants per the Dec 19, 2024 and Mar 23, 2026 Form 1s: (1) Byron Donalds for Congress C00733329, (2) Proven Conservatives PAC C00755728, (3) Republican Party of Florida C00099259, (4) NRCC C00075820, (5) Proven Conservatives PAC - Federal C00894147, [Form 1 Dec 2024](https://docquery.fec.gov/cgi-bin/forms/C00760108/1860265/); [Form 1 Mar 2026](https://docquery.fec.gov/cgi-bin/forms/C00760108/1955929/)
- Mooney-Donalds JFC, FEC ID C00819615, joint fundraising committee with Rep. Alex Mooney, registered July 1, 2022, status Terminated; raised $39,445 (coverage July 1 to Oct 19, 2022), $38,945 from individuals, $500 from other committees; transferred $37,693.12 to participating committees and spent $1,751.88 on operations; ended with $0, [FEC committee page](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00819615/)
- Byron Donalds Victory Fund donation page exists on WinRed, [WinRed](https://secure.winred.com/byron-donalds-victory-fund/socal)

##### Inferences
- The "Proven Conservatives PAC - Federal" committee created Dec 2024 is likely a second leadership PAC vehicle set up alongside the governor run; FEC labels it a leadership PAC with $0 independent expenditures, so it is not a super PAC.
- Because the Victory Fund's participants include the NRCC and RPOF, large checks to the Victory Fund can be split across party committees at higher limits.

##### Gaps
- The Form 1 allocation formula for the Victory Fund was not extracted.
- Who funds Proven Conservatives PAC - Federal (only $15,000 in individual contributions through Sept 30, 2026) was not itemized.

#### Byron Donalds for Congress: cycle totals 2020 through 2026

##### Takeaway
Donalds raised about $3.25M (2020), $5.66M (2022), $6.60M (2024) and only $738K through mid 2026 for his House committee, which has spent heavily in 2025 and 2026 (including a $1.2M transfer to his state governor PAC) while raising little.

##### Cited Findings
- 2019-2020 cycle (coverage Jan 1 to Dec 31, 2020): total receipts $3,250,540.96; individual contributions $2,915,688.05 (itemized $1,816,900.47, unitemized $1,098,787.58); other committee (PAC) contributions $320,250.00; candidate loans $0; transfers in from other authorized committees $8,219.17; disbursements $2,769,646.89; ending cash $480,894.07, [FEC committee page, 2020](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00733329/?cycle=2020)
- 2020 race context: GOP primary rival Casey Askar raised $7,253,992 (largely self funded per campaignmoney.com listing of $3.5M raised) and spent $7,259,535; Democrat Cindy Banyai raised $214,750, [ProPublica race page 2020](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/races/2020/states/fl/house/19)
- 2021-2022 cycle: total receipts $5,657,617.12; individual contributions $4,621,136.22; other committee contributions $571,750.00; disbursements $5,400,519.91; ending cash $737,991.28, [FEC candidate page, 2022](https://www.fec.gov/data/candidate/H2FL14186/?cycle=2022&election_full=true)
- 2023-2024 cycle: total receipts $6,600,931.63; individual contributions $4,812,440.63; other committee contributions $682,634.15; disbursements $5,598,912.45; ending cash $1,740,010.46, [FEC candidate page, 2024](https://www.fec.gov/data/candidate/H2FL14186/?cycle=2024&election_full=true)
- 2024 cycle detail: between Aug 4 and Nov 2, 2024 the committee filed 22 "48 Hour Notice" reports ranging $1,000 to $19,200 (largest filed Oct 31, 2024, $19,200); 2024 year end report showed $5,258.14 in debts, [ProPublica Itemizer 2024](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00733329/2024)
- 2025-2026 cycle (coverage Jan 1, 2025 to June 30, 2026): total receipts $738,092.00; individual contributions $533,501.80; other committee contributions $56,800.00; disbursements $2,143,626.16; ending cash $334,476.30, [FEC candidate page](https://www.fec.gov/data/candidate/H2FL14186/)
- 2026 cycle per FundVoter (through Sept 30, 2026 reports): raised $738,092; individuals $533,502 (72%); transfers, loans and other $147,790 (20%); PACs $56,800 (8%); total spent $2,240,519; fundraising costs $164,521; mail and print $279,675; median active House incumbent raised $1,743,643 so Donalds is at about 0.4x median, [FundVoter](https://fundvoter.org/politicians/byron-donalds)
- Recent 2026 PAC receipts named by FundVoter: Rising Phoenix PAC $7,000 (July 23, 2026); CGCN PAC three contributions totaling $5,000 (May 27, 2026); Sysco $2,500 (Apr 29, 2026); Friends of Neal Dunn $1,000 (Feb 3, 2026); lobbyist contributions $113,800 across 38 entries including June 4, 17 and 18, 2026, [FundVoter](https://fundvoter.org/politicians/byron-donalds)
- FundVoter 2026 cycle industry tally (its "Trace the Influence" tool, only 5 of 11 industries shown): Finance/Banking $112K; Oil and Gas/Energy $51.5K; Real Estate $20K; Agriculture $18.5K; Tech $12K, [FundVoter](https://fundvoter.org/politicians/byron-donalds)
- FundVoter top 2026 PAC organizations: American Council of Engineering Companies PAC $15,000 (4 contributions); State Farm Federal PAC $14,000 (8); NextEra Energy PAC $12,000 (5), [FundVoter](https://fundvoter.org/politicians/byron-donalds)
- Donalds' House campaign transferred $1.2 million to the state committee Friends of Byron Donalds within two days of his Feb 25, 2025 governor announcement, [Florida Trident, Sept 8, 2025](https://floridatrident.org/byron-donalds-skirts-campaign-finance-laws/); [Florida Bulldog, Aug 24, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/)

##### Inferences
- The 2026 cycle disbursements ($2.14M) exceed receipts ($738K) by about $1.4M, consistent with the $1.2M transfer to the state PAC plus wind down spending from the $1.74M cash on hand at end of 2024.
- The FundVoter page's AIPAC figure ($59,864) exceeds its total 2026 PAC receipts ($56,800), so that AIPAC number is an all-cycle figure, not a 2026 one.

##### Gaps
- OpenSecrets per-cycle industry and top contributor tables (securities, real estate, insurance, oil and gas, health, crypto) could not be fetched (site blocked). The 2020 PAC list from Paradise Progressive (below) and FundVoter's 2026 partial tally are the only industry-type breakdowns obtained.
- No individual itemized donor list (names, employers) for 2020, 2022 or 2024 House cycles was obtained; the FEC receipt search pages are blocked to the fetch tool.

#### Corporate and trade PAC money to the House committee (what the PAC layer looks like)

##### Takeaway
The House committee's PAC money is a conventional Florida incumbent mix: sugar, utilities (NextEra), private prisons (GEO), banks, insurance, realtors, auto dealers, Koch, plus pro-Israel PACs, with AIPAC PAC the largest single ideological PAC at roughly $18K across 2022 and 2024.

##### Cited Findings
- 2020 cycle corporate and trade PAC contributions to Byron Donalds for Congress, as compiled from FEC by the Paradise Progressive (May 3, 2021): National Association of Realtors PAC $10,000; National Automobile Dealers Association PAC $10,000; NextEra Energy PAC $8,000 and $5,000; American Crystal Sugar PAC $5,000; US Sugar ESOP PAC $5,000; KochPAC $5,000; Bloomin' Brands PAC $5,000; Publix PAC $5,000; Deloitte PAC $5,000; GEO Group PAC $5,000; American Bankers Association BANKPAC $5,000 and $2,500; AFLAC PAC $3,500 and $2,500; LPL Financial PAC $3,500; AT&T/WarnerMedia PAC $3,000; KPMG PAC $3,000; Marathon Petroleum MPAC $2,500; ABC PAC $2,500; Chubb PAC $2,500; Comcast NBCUniversal PAC $2,500; JM Family PAC $2,500; Regions Financial PAC $2,500; UPS PAC $2,500; Wells Fargo PAC $2,500; PwC PAC $2,000; Protective Life PAC $2,000 and $1,000; ExxonMobil PAC $1,500; NRECA ACRE $1,500; Mosaic PAC $1,000; Reynolds American PAC $1,000; Akerman PAC $1,000; Discover PAC $1,000; GrayRobinson PAC $1,000; Jackson National PAC $1,000; Liberty Mutual PAC $1,000; Marsh & McLennan PAC $1,000; Rock Holdings PAC $1,000; TECO Energy PAC $1,000, [Paradise Progressive, "The Donalds Dossier"](https://www.theparadiseprogressive.com/blog-the-paradise-progressive/2021/05/03/the-donalds-dossier-a-deep-dive-into-the-pac-pool) (blog compiled from FEC; treat amounts as reported, not independently verified)
- AIPAC PAC (C00797670) to Byron Donalds for Congress, 12 entries totaling $18,415 net: Apr 14, 2022 $2,900; July 13, 2022 $5,000 and $2,100; Sept 23, 2022 refunds of -$5,000 and -$2,100; Sept 28, 2022 $5,000; Oct 11, 2023 $5; Oct 18, 2023 $500; Apr 17, 2024 $1; Apr 24, 2024 $9; July 10, 2024 $5,000; Sept 24, 2024 $5,000, [ProPublica Itemizer](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00733329/pac-donations/C00797670)
- Republican Jewish Coalition PAC (C00345132) to Byron Donalds for Congress: 12 small earmarked style entries Sept 4 to Nov 3, 2024 totaling $488, [ProPublica Itemizer](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00733329/pac-donations/C00345132)
- Pro-Israel PAC totals across all cycles per AIPAC Tracker: $74,692 total; AIPAC PAC and United Democracy Project combined $59,864; RJC PAC $5,827; American Principles $5,500; US Israel PAC $3,500; most recent transaction March 31, 2025; the site says there is no UDP independent expenditure on record for him, [whofundsmyrep.com](https://whofundsmyrep.com/rep/byron-donalds) (aggregator; its $59,864 figure far exceeds the $18,415 in ProPublica's AIPAC PAC itemization and likely includes AIPAC-earmarked individual contributions; flag as unreconciled)
- Bank of America Federal PAC to Byron Donalds for Congress: $1,000 Oct 27, 2023; $1,500 July 10, 2024, [ProPublica Itemizer](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00733329/pac-donations/C00364778)
- Federal PACs named by Legis1 as House-era supporters (no amounts): CGCN PAC, Freedom Caucus Fund, Asian American Hotel Owners Association PAC, Deloitte PAC, Google LLC NETPAC, NextEra Energy PAC, Republican Jewish Coalition PAC, [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/florida-governor-race-donors-follow-the-money-by)
- Crypto and AI industry individuals to Byron Donalds for Congress and Byron Donalds Victory Fund, 2024 cycle (Follow the Crypto): Christopher Dixon (a16z) $13,200; Ben Horowitz $13,200; Marc Andreessen $6,600; Laura Andreessen $6,600; total $39,600; no crypto PAC independent expenditures in FL-19 in 2024, [Follow the Crypto, FL-19 2024](https://influence.citationneeded.news/2024/elections/FL-H-19)
- 2026 cycle (Follow the Crypto): Andreessen Horowitz individuals $20,500 (Christopher Dixon $12,000; Ben Horowitz $8,500) and Google NETPAC $5,000, total $25,500 to FL-19; no crypto or AI PAC independent expenditures in the race; page notes Donalds "declined to run", [Follow the Crypto, FL-19 2026](https://influence.citationneeded.news/2026/elections/FL-H-19); [Follow the Crypto Florida 2026](https://influence.citationneeded.news/2026/states/florida)
- Byron Donalds Victory Fund PAC receipts: Growing Georgia $5,800 (Nov 11, 2021); GEO Group PAC $2,500 (May 12, 2022); WinRed $3,300 (Dec 14, 2023); GEO Group PAC $2,500 (Mar 26, 2024); WinRed -$500 (Sept 11, 2024), [ProPublica 2022](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00760108/2022/pac-donations); [ProPublica 2024](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00760108/2024/pac-donations)

##### Inferences
- The a16z founders (Andreessen, Horowitz, Dixon) are the one consistent crypto/AI donor bloc across 2024 and 2026 on the federal side, and the same network (Leading the Future, funded by a16z and Greg Brockman) later backed his governor run through American Mission Florida.

##### Gaps
- The 2022 and 2024 full PAC donor lists for Byron Donalds for Congress were not retrieved (ProPublica aggregate page errored).

#### Leadership PACs: Proven Conservatives PAC and Proven Conservatives PAC - Federal

##### Takeaway
Proven Conservatives PAC is small: about $42K to $257K per cycle, funded by corporate and trade PACs (Koch, Realtors, UBS, Microsoft, Comcast, NextEra, CPAs, Robinhood), and it gave $119,500 to other committees in the 2024 cycle, mostly $1,000 to $5,000 checks to GOP House candidates and Freedom PAC.

##### Cited Findings
- Proven Conservatives PAC cycle totals: 2021-2022 receipts $148,943.71, disbursements $116,300.25, contributions to other committees $57,679.00, cash $5,751.81 to $38,395.27, [FEC, 2022](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00755728/?cycle=2022)
- 2023-2024: receipts $257,208.95, disbursements $186,123.93, contributions to other committees $119,500.00, ending cash $109,480.29, [FEC, 2024](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00755728/?cycle=2024) (ProPublica shows lower aggregate receipts of $171,080 for the same cycle, likely a different summation method, [ProPublica](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00755728/2024))
- 2025-2026 (through June 30, 2026): receipts $42,500.00, disbursements $45,139.58, contributions to other committees $5,000.00, ending cash $106,840.71, [FEC](https://www.fec.gov/data/committee/C00755728/)
- PAC contributions received by Proven Conservatives PAC, 2024 cycle: National Association of Realtors PAC $5,000 (Apr 17, 2023) and $5,000 (June 12, 2024); Automotive Free International Trade PAC $5,000 (June 9, 2023); Robinhood Markets PAC $1,000 (June 22, 2023); ACA International PAC $1,000 (Sept 20, 2023) and $5,000 (Dec 2, 2024); Council of Insurance Agents & Brokers PAC $2,500 (Nov 15, 2023) and $2,500 (Dec 11, 2023); KochPAC $5,000 (Dec 11, 2023) and $5,000 (July 17, 2024); Small Business Investor Alliance PAC $5,000 (June 18, 2024); NextEra Energy PAC $2,500 (Aug 16, 2024); Fry for Congress $1,000 (Aug 26, 2024); Lucas for Congress $2,000 (Sept 4, 2024); American Institute of CPAs PAC $5,000 (Sept 10, 2024); Comcast NBCUniversal PAC $4,000 (Sept 26, 2024); Constellation Energy PAC $2,500 (Oct 9, 2024) and $1,000 (Dec 16, 2024); UBS Americas PAC $5,000 (Dec 16, 2024); Microsoft MSVPAC $3,000 (Dec 18, 2024); National Electrical Contractors Association PAC $2,500 (Dec 4, 2024) and $2,500 (Dec 30, 2024); PwC PAC $2,000 (Dec 31, 2024), [ProPublica Itemizer](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00755728/2024/pac-donations)

#### Outside spending in Donalds' House races (2020, 2022, 2024) and who funded it

##### Takeaway
The only large outside money in Donalds' House career was the 2020 primary, where Club for Growth Action spent about $2.5 million for him and against Casey Askar; Club for Growth Action's 2020 cycle was funded mainly by Richard Uihlein ($26.5M) with Jeff Yass at $1.7M. His 2022 and 2024 races drew only trivial outside spending, mostly Protect Freedom PAC (Yass-funded, Rand Paul aligned) at $20K to $30K and $10K respectively. No crypto super PAC (Fairshake network), UDP, America PAC, MAGA Inc, Turning Point Action or Kennedy-aligned spending was found on his House races.

##### Cited Findings
- 2020 FL-19 outside spending totals: $4,149,905 total; Club for Growth Action $2,504,727 (all primary, as of Aug 12, 2020); Conservative Outsider PAC $476,839; Concerned Conservatives Inc $424,667; Americans for Prosperity Action $251,328; Honesty America Inc $188,408, [ProPublica race page 2020](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/races/2020/states/fl/house/19)
- Club for Growth PAC itself states it spent $2.5 million supporting Donalds in the 2020 House primary, [Club for Growth, Mar 11, 2025](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/cfg-pac-endorses-rep-byron-donalds-in-fl-gov-race/)
- Club for Growth Action July 2020 itemized IEs: July 21 $28,584 anti-Askar literature; July 23 $485,916 pro-Donalds ads; July 23 $434,347 TV and mail opposing Askar; July 24 $103,385 pro-Donalds including $90,000 door to door; July 24 $29,084 anti-Askar mail, [Florida Politics, July 2020](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/352963-club-for-growth-spends-big-boosting-byron-donalds-bashing-casey-askar/)
- Other 2020 pro-Donalds IEs (OpenSecrets figures as reproduced by Paradise Progressive): Club for Growth Action $1,383,647 (support side only); Americans for Prosperity Action $203,613; Honesty America $138,131; Concerned Conservatives $85,706; Protect Freedom PAC $80,187; Trusted Conservatives $46,138; American Liberty Fund $37,553; New Journey PAC $32,230; Conservative Outsider PAC $17,769; Club for Growth $9,272; Guardian Fund $6,941; Friends of Mia Love PAC $6,045; NRA PVF $4,451; National Right to Life Victory Fund $3,396; FreedomWorks for America $2,500; House Freedom Fund $1,486; NRA ILA $1,184, [Paradise Progressive](https://www.theparadiseprogressive.com/blog-the-paradise-progressive/2021/05/03/the-donalds-dossier-a-deep-dive-into-the-pac-pool)
- 2020 itemized: Protect Freedom PAC $55,188 (Oct 1, 2020) and $25,000 (Oct 2, 2020) supporting Donalds; NRA PVF $4,452 (Oct 16, 2020); New Journey PAC $8,790 (Sept 26) and $2,960 (Oct 28); Guardian Fund four entries Oct 13 to 23 totaling $6,942; National Right to Life Victory Fund $3,241 (Oct 9), [ProPublica IE page 2020](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/independent_expenditures/2020/fl/house/19)
- Honesty America Inc was formed by Askar supporters and spent $24,500 on July 25, 2020 on mailers calling Donalds a "Never-Trumper"; Concerned Conservatives (pro Dane Eagle) spent $13,363 opposing Donalds, [Florida Politics](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/352963-club-for-growth-spends-big-boosting-byron-donalds-bashing-casey-askar/)
- Club for Growth Action 2020 cycle funders: Richard Uihlein $26.5 million (nearly half); Virginia James $2 million; Jeff Yass $1.7 million (as of Jan 2020), [FactCheck.org, Jan 2020](https://www.factcheck.org/2020/01/club-for-growth-action-5)
- Club for Growth Action 2024 cycle funders (as of Feb 2024): Jeff Yass about $16 million; Richard Uihlein about $8.8 million; Thomas Klingenstein $2.5 million; Virginia James over $1 million; the super PAC spent about $89 million in 2022 including $13.5 million in contributions to other committees, [FactCheck.org, Apr 2024](https://www.factcheck.org/2024/04/club-for-growth-action-7)
- 2022 FL-19 outside spending: total $110,553, all supporting Donalds; Elbert Guillory's America $88,423 (mostly direct mail dated May 15 to Dec 13, 2021 and $20,400 media Sept 27, 2022); Protect Freedom PAC $20,000 digital (Oct 13, 2022) plus $10,000 text messages (Nov 7, 2022); House Freedom Fund $1,228; Guardian Fund $902 (Oct 5, 2022); New Journey PAC $2,500 (Nov 5, 2022) and $1,000 (Nov 7, 2022), [ProPublica IE page 2022](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/independent_expenditures/2022/fl/house/19)
- 2024 FL-19 outside spending: total $12,748 supporting; Protect Freedom PAC $10,000 (Oct 9, 2024); House Freedom Fund $2,748 (Sept 27, 2023 to Dec 16, 2024); Gun Rights America $48 (Oct 17, 2024); Must Win. Can Win. $21,000 opposing Donalds (Oct 6 and Oct 8, 2024), [ProPublica IE page 2024](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/independent_expenditures/2024/fl/house/19)
- 2026 FL-19 outside spending (FundVoter): $56,535 supporting Donalds, of which Latinos for Conservative Values $56,350 and Freedom Caucus Fund $185; $0 opposing; race wide $5,849,122 support and $1,336,656 oppose across the open seat GOP field, [FundVoter](https://fundvoter.org/politicians/byron-donalds)
- Protect Freedom PAC funding: Jeff Yass gave $8 million on March 31, 2024 and about $30 million total since the PAC's 2017 founding, roughly 80% of its receipts; founded by former Rand Paul campaign staff, [Kentucky Lantern, Apr 11, 2024](https://kentuckylantern.com/2024/04/11/billionaire-tiktok-investor-charter-school-advocate-puts-8-million-into-paul-affiliated-pac/)
- School Freedom Fund (C00794396, Club for Growth affiliate) 2022 independent expenditures show no Florida or FL-19 entries in the visible table, [ProPublica](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00794396/2022/independent-expenditures)
- Fairshake / Defend American Jobs: 2025 Florida special election spending went to Randy Fine ($1.2M) and Jimmy Patronis ($345K); Donalds not mentioned, [Axios, Mar 21, 2025](https://www.axios.com/2025/03/21/fairshake-defend-american-jobs-florida-crypto); Fairshake's 2026 House slate reporting names no Florida candidate and does not mention Donalds, [Blockonomi, Oct 2025](https://blockonomi.com/crypto-pac-fairshake-backs-32-house-candidates-before-2026-midterm-elections/)
- America PAC (Musk) Florida spending: about $20,000 in texting for Patronis and Fine in the April 1, 2025 specials; Donalds not mentioned, [Yahoo/Miami Herald](https://www.yahoo.com/news/florida-republicans-cash-infusion-elon-201225499.html)
- Club for Growth PAC endorsed Donalds for FL-19 in 2020 (endorsement release) and for governor on March 11, 2025, [Club for Growth 2020](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/club-for-growth-pac-endorses-byron-donalds-fl-19/); [Club for Growth 2025](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/cfg-pac-endorses-rep-byron-donalds-in-fl-gov-race/)

##### Inferences
- The Yass-to-Donalds relationship on the federal side is indirect: Yass is the dominant funder of Protect Freedom PAC (the only outside group active for Donalds in all three House cycles) and a top-three funder of Club for Growth Action (his 2020 primary rescuer). Direct Yass checks appear only at the state level (see below).
- ProPublica's $2.5M Club for Growth Action figure for 2020 counts both support and oppose spending; the $1.38M OpenSecrets figure is support-only.

##### Gaps
- No source found for UDP, RJC Victory Fund, Turning Point Action, MAGA Inc or Kennedy-aligned group spending on Donalds' House races; absence in ProPublica IE tables is strong evidence none occurred but is not a positive confirmation.
- Elbert Guillory's America (2021-2022) funders not researched.

#### Jeff Yass and Susquehanna-linked money

##### Takeaway
No direct Yass contribution to Byron Donalds for Congress, Proven Conservatives PAC or the Victory Fund was documented in any source; the federal limit would cap such a gift at $3,300 per election. Yass's documented Donalds money is state-level: $5 million by March 31, 2025, rising to $7.5 million by mid 2026, to Friends of Byron Donalds PAC, making him Donalds' largest donor until Ken Griffin's $10 million in July 2026.

##### Cited Findings
- Jeff Yass $5 million to Friends of Byron Donalds PAC in the period through March 31, 2025 (filings published April 2025), [News4Jax, Apr 14, 2025](https://www.news4jax.com/news/local/2025/04/14/byron-donalds-secures-6-million-from-influential-gop-donors-in-early-fundraising-for-florida-governors-race/); [Florida Politics, Apr 2025](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/729731-a-month-in-byron-donalds-touts-12m-raised-for-governor-campaign/)
- Yass total $7.5 million to Friends of Byron Donalds PAC, the largest single contribution, per Miami New Times (2026, no calendar dates given); Yass also gave $16 million to MAGA Inc in the first half of 2025, [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- AOL/Miami Herald (July 24, 2026) describes Yass's $7.5 million as "given in March" and as the second largest donor after Griffin, [AOL](https://www.aol.com/articles/billionaire-ken-griffin-pours-money-192303000.html) (conflicts with April 2025 reporting of $5M through March 2025; most likely $5M in March 2025 plus $2.5M later, but the split and date of the second tranche are unverified)
- Yass is a top funder of Club for Growth Action ($1.7M in 2020 cycle; about $16M in 2024 cycle as of Feb 2024) and gave more than $15 million to the Club's super PAC before it backed Trump, [FactCheck 2020](https://www.factcheck.org/2020/01/club-for-growth-action-5); [FactCheck 2024](https://www.factcheck.org/2024/04/club-for-growth-action-7); [NBC Chicago / CNBC](https://www.nbcchicago.com/news/business/money-report/republican-megadonor-jeff-yass-inner-circle-give-millions-to-shape-schools-courts/3405754/)
- Yass gave $8 million to Protect Freedom PAC on March 31, 2024 and about $30 million lifetime (80% of its receipts); Protect Freedom PAC spent for Donalds in 2020 ($80,188), 2022 ($20,000 to $30,000) and 2024 ($10,000), [Kentucky Lantern](https://kentuckylantern.com/2024/04/11/billionaire-tiktok-investor-charter-school-advocate-puts-8-million-into-paul-affiliated-pac/); ProPublica IE pages cited above
- Club for Growth Action (super PAC whose largest funders are Yass and Uihlein) gave $975,000 to Friends of Byron Donalds per state records cited in Aug 2025, [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/); Miami New Times later reported Club for Growth PAC at $3.65 million, the PAC's second largest contribution, [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- A Substack author (Steve Farley) asserts Yass gave >$11M to School Freedom Fund, $3.5M to AFC Victory Fund and $8M to Protect Freedom PAC in 2024, and that Uihlein gave $2M to School Freedom Fund (2023) and $1M to AFC Victory Fund (2024); the fetch found several of these unsourced or mis-cited, [Farley Substack](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/how-the-donalds-network-the-school) (low reliability blog; use only as leads)

##### Inferences
- The TikTok angle: Yass's Susquehanna holds a large ByteDance stake and he reportedly lobbied against forced divestiture in 2024; Donalds sits on House Financial Services. No source documented a Donalds vote tied to Yass money.

##### Gaps
- No Janine Yass or Susquehanna International Group entity contribution to any Donalds committee was found in any source. FEC individual contribution search was blocked, so a $0 result cannot be positively confirmed.
- Exact dates of the Yass tranches to Friends of Byron Donalds must be pulled from the Florida Division of Elections database (state researcher's layer).

#### Other megadonors: confirmed, reported, or not found

##### Takeaway
Of the user's list, only Ken Griffin, Thomas Peterffy and Dick Uihlein are documented backers, all at the state level. Steve Wynn, the Seminole Tribe, GEO Group, NextEra, the Winklevoss twins and Kalshi are also documented state donors. No source tied Singer, Marcus, Bigelow, Dunn, Wilks, Perlmutter, Adelson, Loeb, DeVos, Mercer, Warren, Crow, Schwab or Witkoff to any Donalds committee, federal or state.

##### Cited Findings
- Ken Griffin (Citadel): $10 million to the political committee supporting Donalds for governor, reported July 24, 2026 (NBC first), largest single contribution in the race; Griffin also gave $2.5 million to Sen. Ashley Moody, [Tampa Bay Times, July 24, 2026](https://www.tampabay.com/news/florida-politics/elections/2026/07/24/ken-griffin-byron-donalds-governor-donation-campaign-finance/); [Bloomberg Government](https://news.bgov.com/bloomberg-government-news/ken-griffin-gives-10-million-to-donalds-in-florida-governor-bid); [AOL/Miami Herald](https://www.aol.com/articles/billionaire-ken-griffin-pours-money-192303000.html); Donalds did not dispute the $10M figure in an Aug 4, 2026 WUSF interview, [WUSF](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-08-04/rep-byron-donalds-discusses-campaign-finance-data-centers-the-environment-and-more)
- Richard Uihlein (Uline): $1 million by March 31, 2025; second $1 million check on June 6, 2025; $2 million total to Friends of Byron Donalds, [News4Jax](https://www.news4jax.com/news/local/2025/04/14/byron-donalds-secures-6-million-from-influential-gop-donors-in-early-fundraising-for-florida-governors-race/); [Florida Politics, July 2025](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/745791-byron-donalds-adds-to-haul-for-governors-race-bringing-total-to-22m/); [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- Thomas Peterffy (Interactive Brokers): $1 million per state records as of Aug 2025; $1.2 million per Miami New Times 2026, [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/); [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- Cameron and Tyler Winklevoss (Gemini): $500,000 each, $1 million combined, state records as of Aug 2025, [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/)
- Seminole Tribe of Florida $3 million; GEO Group $1.5 million in two donations; Steve Wynn $1 million; NextEra Energy $1 million (including $500K in Q4 2025 per News4Jax as cited by Farley); Kalshi $25,000 (2025), [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/); [Tampa Bay Times, June 15, 2026](https://tampabay.com/news/florida-politics/elections/2026/06/15/byron-donalds-kalshi-campaign-donations-sports-election/)
- AI network: Leading the Future (national super PAC funded by Andreessen Horowitz $25M, Marc Andreessen $12.5M, Ben Horowitz $12.5M, Greg and Anna Brockman $25M; about $140M raised since Aug 2025 founding) gave $3 million on March 4, 2026 to American Mission Florida, a state committee chaired by Sarasota Property Appraiser Bill Furst registered Feb 2026, which spent $2 million on pro-Donalds ads; Leading the Future pledged $5 million for Donalds (pledge reported Feb 12, 2026 per Farley citing NBC/NOTUS), [WGCU / Suncoast Searchlight, Apr 17, 2026](https://www.wgcu.org/top-story/2026-04-17/sarasotas-property-appraiser-is-moving-millions-from-ai-moguls-to-support-byron-donalds); [Yahoo/NBC](https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/super-pac-backed-ai-titans-130040489.html)
- Donalds' own account of his funding (Aug 4, 2026): "I'm not bought and paid for by anybody"; credits transplants to Florida; said his 2012 House run raised $80,000, half in-kind office space, [WUSF](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-08-04/rep-byron-donalds-discusses-campaign-finance-data-centers-the-environment-and-more)
- Aggregate state totals for context: Friends of Byron Donalds $71.8M and candidate account $9.2M as of end of May 2026, 35,292 donors, $81M+; $103M+ by late July 2026; $111M+ with about $58M remaining heading into Aug 18, 2026 primary; public matching funds nearly $823,000 by Aug 17, 2026, [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/florida-governor-race-donors-follow-the-money-by); [CBS Miami, Aug 17, 2026](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/florida-primary-byron-donalds-david-jolly-campaign-funds/)

##### Inferences
- The donor pattern behind Donalds is financial markets (Yass, Griffin, Peterffy, Winklevoss, Kalshi, Robinhood PAC), gaming (Seminole, Wynn), private prisons (GEO), utilities (NextEra), Club for Growth's school choice and tax network (Yass, Uihlein) and the a16z / OpenAI AI bloc. Pro-Israel PAC money is present but small ($18K to $75K depending on counting).

##### Gaps
- Paul Singer, Bernie Marcus or Marcus family, Robert Bigelow, Tim Dunn, Farris Wilks, Isaac Perlmutter, Miriam Adelson, Dan Loeb, Betsy DeVos family, Rebekah Mercer, Kelcy Warren, Harlan Crow, Charles Schwab, Steve Witkoff: no source found connecting any of them to a Donalds committee. A Real Deal (Oct 8, 2026) real estate donor story and a Bradenton Times "billionaire cash from outside the state" story could not be fetched and may contain additional names. Absence here is "not found," not "ruled out."

#### FEC complaints, enforcement, and irregularities

##### Takeaway
Two documented matters: MUR 7783 (CLC soft money complaint over a $107,456 state PAC to super PAC chain in 2020) deadlocked 3-3 at the FEC and was closed April 26, 2022 with no finding; and an August 5, 2025 FEC Reports Analysis Division letter telling the campaign that about $400,000 in post-announcement contributions must be refunded, which the treasurer rebuffed by asserting Donalds remained a House candidate. Separately, an opinion column documented $133,450 in campaign payments to aide Larry Wilcoxson.

##### Cited Findings
- MUR 7783, complaint filed Aug 18, 2020 by Campaign Legal Center (Brendan Fischer); respondents Byron Donalds, Byron Donalds for Congress and treasurer Bradley T. Crate, Friends of Byron Donalds (state PAC) and treasurer Noreen Fenner, Conservatives for Effective Government and treasurer David Ramba; allegations under 52 USC 30125(e) (soft money) and 30122 (contributions in the name of another), [FEC MUR 7783](https://www.fec.gov/data/legal/matter-under-review/7783/); [CLC](https://campaignlegal.org/document/clc-complaint-against-byron-donalds-friends-byron-donalds-and-conservatives-effective)
- Facts per OGC: Friends of Byron Donalds (state committee formed Aug 22, 2019, Donalds as chair) raised $98,707 Sept 2019 to Jan 2020; gave $87,000 to Conservatives for Effective Government on Jan 3, 2020, the day Donalds resigned as chair; Donalds filed for Congress Jan 6, 2020; Trusted Conservatives (federal super PAC) organized Jan 24, 2020; CFEG sent $107,038.66 back to FBD (reported May 28 or June 28, 2020) labeled a "refund"; FBD told Florida it ceased operations June 4, 2020; FBD gave $107,456 to Trusted Conservatives on June 22, 2020, never disclosed to Florida; Trusted Conservatives made $82,025 in direct mail IEs supporting Donalds or opposing rivals and terminated Nov 17, 2020, [First General Counsel's Report](https://www.fec.gov/files/legal/murs/7783/7783_13.pdf)
- OGC recommended reason to believe findings against FBD, Fenner and Donalds; the Commission split 3-3 on reason to believe, split 3-3 on no reason to believe, then voted 6-0 to close the file; disposition "Dismissed-Other," no penalty; file closed April 26, 2022, closure letter May 5, 2022; Statement of Reasons by Dickerson, Cooksey and Trainor dated May 31, 2022, [FEC MUR 7783](https://www.fec.gov/data/legal/matter-under-review/7783/); [closure letter](https://www.fec.gov/files/legal/murs/7783/7783_15.pdf)
- Aug 5, 2025: FEC RAD analyst Bradley Austin wrote that contributions to Byron Donalds for Congress received after the Feb 25, 2025 governor announcement "must be refunded, except to the extent there were net debts outstanding"; roughly $400,000 came in after the announcement; Aug 6, 2025 Form 99 from Crate asserts Donalds "has not ended his Congressional re-election campaign" so "no primary election contributions are required to be refunded at this time", [FEC Form 99, Aug 6, 2025](https://docquery.fec.gov/pdf/494/202508139789465494/202508139789465494.pdf); [Florida Trident](https://floridatrident.org/byron-donalds-skirts-campaign-finance-laws/); [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/)
- Florida law issue: Fla. Stat. bars qualifying for more than one office with overlapping terms; Donalds filed FEC paperwork for re-election in Nov 2024 and state paperwork for governor in Feb 2025; Crate formed the state PAC Friends of Byron Donalds on Feb 26, 2025; experts (Ian Vandewalker, Brennan Center; Jeff Swartz, Cooley Law) said he may violate the spirit but not the letter and that FEC enforcement is unlikely, [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/); [Florida Trident](https://floridatrident.org/byron-donalds-skirts-campaign-finance-laws/)
- Campaign payments to Larry Wilcoxson and Wilcoxson Consulting from Byron Donalds for Congress: $133,450 across 160+ disbursements July 7, 2020 to March 24, 2025, including payroll $75,250, payroll taxes $18,750, consulting $22,500, travel $8,450; more than $86,000 after May 2024, [The Blaze opinion column, May 30, 2025](https://www.theblaze.com/columns/opinion/byron-donalds-pays-body-man-with-a-rap-sheet-using-campaign-cash) (opinion piece using FEC data; the Farley Substack separately claims $419,000 to Wilcoxson citing OpenSecrets, unverified)
- Mooney-Donalds JFC and Byron Donalds for Congress had two amended April Quarterly reports (filed May 2, 2023 and May 29, 2024), [ProPublica](https://projects.propublica.org/itemizer/committee/C00733329/2024)

##### Inferences
- The 2020 chain (state PAC to CFEG to state PAC to federal super PAC) and the 2025 federal-to-state $1.2M transfer are the two episodes most likely to be cited by opponents as pattern evidence of moving money across the federal/state line.

##### Gaps
- No OCE or House Ethics complaint about Donalds' campaign finances was found.
- Whether any of the ~$400,000 was later refunded (post Aug 2025 filings) was not verified.

#### State layer figures captured incidentally (for the tracker; the state researcher owns verification)

##### Takeaway
Reported donors to Friends of Byron Donalds PAC (state) with amounts and the earliest date each figure was reported.

- Ken Griffin $10,000,000, reported July 24, 2026, [Tampa Bay Times](https://www.tampabay.com/news/florida-politics/elections/2026/07/24/ken-griffin-byron-donalds-governor-donation-campaign-finance/)

- Club for Growth PAC / Action $975,000 by Aug 2025; $3,650,000 cumulative by 2026, [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/); [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- Seminole Tribe of Florida $3,000,000, [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- Richard Uihlein $1,000,000 by Mar 31, 2025 plus $1,000,000 on June 6, 2025, [Florida Politics](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/745791-byron-donalds-adds-to-haul-for-governors-race-bringing-total-to-22m/)
- GEO Group $1,500,000 (two gifts); Thomas Peterffy $1,200,000; Steve Wynn $1,000,000; NextEra Energy $1,000,000; Kalshi $25,000, [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- Cameron Winklevoss $500,000 and Tyler Winklevoss $500,000 by Aug 2025, [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/)
- Byron Donalds for Congress to Friends of Byron Donalds PAC $1,200,000, Feb 26 or 27, 2025, [Florida Trident](https://floridatrident.org/byron-donalds-skirts-campaign-finance-laws/)
- Leading the Future to American Mission Florida $3,000,000 on March 4, 2026, [WGCU](https://www.wgcu.org/top-story/2026-04-17/sarasotas-property-appraiser-is-moving-millions-from-ai-moguls-to-support-byron-donalds)

- None beyond those above.

### Note: governor_campaign_money.md

_Folder: Byron Donalds money and donor network_

### Byron Donalds 2026 Florida Governor Campaign Money (as of Oct. 22, 2026)

Scope note: Florida Politics (floridapolitics.com), the Florida Division of Elections (dos.elections.myflorida.com) query endpoints, Transparency USA, and Tampa Bay Times full text were all unreachable from this research environment (client errors / proxy denials). Findings below are drawn from syndications of those stories (Yahoo, KDH News, Miami Times, WLRN/WUSF/WGCU News Service of Florida, Floridian Press, Hoodline, Bonus.com, Gambling Insider, Legis1, VoteWater, Bradenton Journal substack, Seeking Rents, Florida Phoenix) and from the FEC. Every figure is labeled by source type; "news report of filing" means a journalist cited a Division of Elections filing. Nothing here was pulled directly from the raw state database, so load-bearing numbers should be re-verified there before publication.

#### Key Question 1: Exact names and totals of Donalds' state accounts and committee(s)

##### Takeaway
Donalds runs two state vehicles: the candidate account "Byron Donalds for Governor" (capped at $3,000 per donor) and the unlimited affiliated committee "Friends of Byron Donalds PAC" (Division of Elections account #89043; chair Ryan Smith, treasurer Bradley T. Crate c/o Red Curve Solutions). Through the Oct. 3 to 9, 2026 reporting week the PAC alone had raised roughly $130.4 million; combined totals reported in news coverage climbed from $131 million (Sept. 18) to $134.4 million (Sept. 25) to "nearly $149 million" raised and "more than $110 million" spent (reported Oct. 20).

##### Cited Findings
- Friends of Byron Donalds PAC, FL DOE account 89043. Chairperson: Ryan Smith, 1555 Cristobal Drive, Tallahassee, FL 32303. Treasurer: Bradley T. Crate, c/o Red Curve Solutions, 138 Conant Street, Suite 401, Beverly, MA 01915. Committee address: 6017 Pine Ridge Road, #361, Naples, FL 34119, [FL Division of Elections committee detail](https://dos.elections.myflorida.com/committees/ComDetail.asp?account=89043)
- Ryan Smith is also described as "Chief Strategist for the Byron Donalds Campaign" and "Chairman of Friends of Byron Donalds PAC", [Floridian Press, Apr. 1, 2026](https://floridianpress.com/2026/04/byron-donalds-adds-record-22-million-to-gubernatorial-war-chest/); [FL Voice News, June 25, 2026](https://flvoicenews.com/donalds-pac-announces-20-million-statewide-ad-campaign-ahead-florida-gop-primary/)
- Candidate account name "Byron Donalds for Governor"; through May 31, 2026 it had raised $9.2 million vs. $71.8 million for Friends of Byron Donalds PAC, total $81.1 million, 35,292 individual donors; April to May 2026 haul was $13.8 million ($11.7M PAC, $2.13M candidate account, 8,812 donors), [Floridian Press, June 2, 2026](https://floridianpress.com/2026/06/byron-donalds-adds-13-8-million-to-gubernatorial-war-chest-brings-total-to-over-81-million/)
- Timeline of cumulative totals (all news reports of filings or campaign announcements):
  - Feb. 26, 2025: Donalds files for governor and transfers $1.2 million from his House campaign (FEC C00733329) to Friends of Byron Donalds, [WLRN, Aug. 20, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-20/byron-donalds-kept-house-re-election-campaign-alive-during-run-for-florida-governor)
  - Through Mar. 31, 2025: PAC over $11 million; campaign account over $500,000, [News4Jax, Apr. 14, 2025](https://www.news4jax.com/news/local/2025/04/14/byron-donalds-secures-6-million-from-influential-gop-donors-in-early-fundraising-for-florida-governors-race/)
  - $12 million raised in first month after Feb. 26, 2025 launch; $22 million total by July 1, 2025, [Axios, July 1, 2025](https://www.axios.com/2025/07/01/byron-donalds-florida-governor-fundraising)
  - Calendar 2025 total: $45 million; Q4 2025: $13.1 million from about 8,000 donors, [Floridian Press, Jan. 2026](https://floridianpress.com/2026/01/byron-donalds-hauls-45m-in-2025-over-13m-in-q4-for-gubernatorial-bid/)
  - Q1 2026: $22.44 million ($19.8M PAC, $2.64M campaign), cumulative "north of $67 million", [Highlands News-Sun / Mid Florida Newspapers](https://www.midfloridanewspapers.com/highlands_news-sun/news/donalds-dominates-fundraising-in-governor-race/article_70925d94-73c0-4f61-9737-e3805c0a1d70.html); [Miami Times, Apr. 2026](https://www.miamitimesonline.com/news/florida/byron-donalds-posts-record-22m-quarter-to-kick-off-2026-governor-s-race/article_ec4a377b-76a8-4b72-9aa5-c6bbdaf1711f.html)
  - Apr. 1 to May 31, 2026: raised $14 million; $65.8 million cash on hand across both accounts as of June 1, 2026, [WLRN / News Service of Florida, June 12, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-06-12/byron-donalds-continues-fundraising-domination-in-florida-governors-race)
  - Late July 2026: more than $103 million; heading into Aug. 18 primary more than $111 million raised with roughly $58 million retained, [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/florida-governor-race-donors-follow-the-money-by)
  - As of Aug. 17, 2026: total more than $111 million; Friends of Byron Donalds more than $99 million raised, more than $51 million cash on hand; campaign account $6.8 million cash on hand, [WLRN, Aug. 17, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-17/will-byron-donalds-dominance-in-fundraising-result-in-a-gop-victory)
  - As of Sept. 7, 2026: Friends of Byron Donalds more than $100 million, "the largest affiliated PAC in Florida", [WUSF, Sept. 9, 2026](https://wusf.org/text/politics-issues/2026-09-09/what-to-know-about-pacs-your-florida-breaks-it-down)
  - As of Sept. 18, 2026: $131 million raised (Tampa Bay Times analysis), [KDH News syndication of Tampa Bay Times, Oct. 2, 2026](https://kdhnews.com/news/politics/18-billionaires-have-funded-the-florida-governor-race-17-gave-to-donalds/article_f4a68240-1521-537e-b8cb-9006156ebc14.html)
  - As of Sept. 25, 2026: raised $134.4 million, spent $91.7 million, campaign account plus committee combined, [CBS Miami](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/byron-donalds-new-ads-florida-governor-race-david-jolly/)
  - Sept. 28, 2026: more than $115 million via political committee; more than $75 million spent on TV ads (Jolly campaign framing), [FOX 13, Sept. 28, 2026](https://www.fox13news.com/news/david-jolly-addresses-campaign-cash-gap-florida-governor-race)
  - Oct. 3 to 9, 2026 week: Friends of Byron Donalds raised more than $5.1 million; cumulative PAC haul "nearly $130.4 million", [Bradenton Journal (Substack), Oct. 2026](https://thebradentonjournal.substack.com/p/donations-from-neal-beruff-add-to)
  - Oct. 20, 2026: "nearly $149 million" raised, "more than $110 million" spent; cash on hand about $33 million more than Jolly per OpenSecrets, [Florida Phoenix, Oct. 20, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/10/20/democratic-governors-association-investing-1-million-to-support-david-jolly/)
- Public financing: Donalds qualified for $662,350.93 in state matching funds in July 2026, [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/florida-governor-race-donors-follow-the-money-by); an additional distribution of almost $21,000 to Donalds (vs. nearly $94,000 to Jolly) reported Sept. 8, 2026, [MySuncoast, Sept. 8, 2026](https://www.mysuncoast.com/2026/09/08/jolly-campaigns-venice-governors-race-matching-funds-grow/)
- Federal House account (Byron Donalds for Congress, FEC C00733329, treasurer Bradley Crate): kept alive through Aug. 2026; since start of 2025 received $738,092 (incl. $30,000 from PACs), spent more than $2.1 million including the $1.2M transfer, $136,561.47 "security services," $36,000 to an unnamed polling/research firm; told FEC Aug. 5/8, 2026 it would stop raising, [WLRN, Aug. 20, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-20/byron-donalds-kept-house-re-election-campaign-alive-during-run-for-florida-governor)

##### Inferences
- The Oct. 20 "nearly $149M raised / $110M spent" figures imply roughly $38 to 39 million cash on hand combined, consistent with the OpenSecrets-cited $33 million advantage over Jolly, but no outlet reported an exact combined cash figure for October.
- Roughly 90 percent or more of Donalds' money flows through the unlimited PAC, not the capped candidate account.

##### Gaps
- Exact October cash on hand per account; exact dates of the Griffin, Yass, Seminole, and other top gifts (the state database was unreachable).
- Florida Politics articles with the week-by-week October numbers (archives 824491, 825466, 825491) could not be fetched.

#### Key Question 2: Top donors by amount and their industries

##### Takeaway
The top of the donor list is dominated by out-of-state billionaires (Griffin $10M, Yass $7.5M direct plus $3.5 to 3.75M via Club for Growth Action Florida, Uihlein $2M, Peterffy $1.2M, Wynn $1M, DeVos family about $1M), the Seminole Tribe ($5.5M), Club for Growth ($3.65M), GEO Group ($2.5M), and Florida utilities, homebuilders, and sports betting operators. Per the Tampa Bay Times, about two thirds of Donalds' money came from corporations, political committees, and billionaires giving $10,000 or more; 17 billionaires gave $23.3 million (18 percent), and about $39 million (30 percent) came from outside Florida.
