- VoteWater (advocacy group) as of ~2025: nearly $3 million from developers/construction/housing finance; says Big Sugar gave nothing directly, only via AIF PAC and Florida PC, [VoteWater](https://votewater.org/dirty-money-talks-what-does-it-say-about-byron-donalds/)

##### Inferences
- Combined Yass money ($11M) plus Griffin ($10M) is roughly $21M, about 14 percent of the $149M total.
- The flows from state contractors owed money under Operation Vigil Sentry (GEO, CDR, IRG, Lemoine, Critical Response) total more than $3.3 million and represent a distinct "vendor seeking payment" donor cluster.

##### Gaps
- No single verified top-50 ranked list exists in accessible sources; ranks above are assembled from multiple articles with different as-of dates and may double count or miss gifts. Verify against the DOE contribution export.
- No accessible source quantified Publix/Jenkins family, Disney, Florida Crystals/Fanjul, U.S. Sugar, Citizens, or Florida Chamber direct gifts to Donalds' PAC (see Q4).

#### Key Question 3: Super PACs and outside groups for and against Donalds, and their funders

##### Takeaway
The main outside vehicle is American Mission Florida, a state PC chaired by Sarasota Property Appraiser Bill Furst and funded entirely by the AI-industry federal super PAC Leading the Future ($3M on Mar. 4, 2026; $5M pledged); Club for Growth Action Florida routed Yass money; AFP Action and Florida Chamber endorsed without disclosed spending. Against Donalds, Conservatives for Ethical Leadership (Lincoln Project-affiliated, funded by Affordable Florida) and the DGA ($1M for Jolly, Oct. 20, 2026) are the only reported spenders.

##### Cited Findings
- Leading the Future (federal super PAC, FEC C00916114; Andreessen Horowitz $25M, Marc Andreessen $12.5M, Ben Horowitz $12.5M, Greg and Anna Brockman $25M) pledged $5M to boost Donalds; gave American Mission Florida $3M on Mar. 4, 2026; American Mission Florida put about $2M into pro-Donalds ads by April; registered with the state in February 2026 listing a Tallahassee Staples store as address; chaired by Bill Furst, [WGCU, Apr. 17, 2026](https://news.wgcu.org/text/top-story/2026-04-17/sarasotas-property-appraiser-is-moving-millions-from-ai-moguls-to-support-byron-donalds); [NBC News](https://nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/super-pac-backed-ai-titans-pledges-5-million-boost-byron-donalds-run-f-rcna258612)
- American Mission spent at least $500,000 on TV for Donalds in spring 2026 (about 1,700 placements in Tampa Bay and Tallahassee); six-figure disbursements to Summit Ridge Media Group LLC (Reno mailbox) in March and June 2026; Strategic Media Placement (alias of The Strategy Group Co., Ohio) named as placement firm; Campaign Legal Center filed FEC complaint in May 2026 alleging shell LLCs (Summit Ridge Media, Lantern Production Consultants) hid spending, [Hoodline, July 2, 2026](https://hoodline.com/2026/07/secretive-pro-ai-cash-fuels-byron-donalds-tv-blitz-across-florida/); [Steve Farley Substack](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/no-primary-no-debate-how-byron-donalds)
- Club for Growth Action Florida: received $3.5M from Yass, gave $3.75M to Friends of Byron Donalds, [WUSF, Sept. 9, 2026](https://wusf.org/text/politics-issues/2026-09-09/what-to-know-about-pacs-your-florida-breaks-it-down); Club for Growth endorsed Donalds Mar. 11, 2025, [Steve Farley Substack](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/no-primary-no-debate-how-byron-donalds)
- Americans for Prosperity Action endorsed Donalds Mar. 4, 2026, pledging grassroots mobilization; no dollar figure, [The Capitolist, Mar. 4, 2026](https://thecapitolist.com/americans-for-prosperity-action-backs-byron-donalds-for-florida-governor/)
- Endorsing trade groups (no amounts disclosed): Florida Chamber of Commerce (June 23, 2026), Florida Retail Federation (July 21, 2026), Associated Industries of Florida (Jan. 27, 2026), Florida Restaurant and Lodging Association (Feb. 4, 2026), Associated Builders and Contractors of Florida (Apr. 14, 2026), Gun Owners of America (June 11, 2026), [Hoodline, July 2026](https://hoodline.com/2026/07/florida-retail-heavyweight-backs-byron-donalds-in-governor-showdown/); [Steve Farley Substack](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/no-primary-no-debate-how-byron-donalds)
- Against Donalds: Conservatives for Ethical Leadership (chair J.C. Planas; Lincoln Project-affiliated; created June 2026; primarily funded by PC "Affordable Florida") ran cable in Tallahassee market, digital in 17 counties Donalds lost in primary, and The Villages, week of Oct. 6, 2026; Affordable Florida ran an earlier summer digital ad on AI data centers, [Florida Phoenix, Oct. 6, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/10/06/new-ad-targets-byron-donalds-in-the-counties-he-lost-in-the-gop-primary/)
- Democratic Governors Association announced $1,000,000 for Jolly on Oct. 20, 2026 (spokesperson Kevin Donohoe), [Florida Phoenix, Oct. 20, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/10/20/democratic-governors-association-investing-1-million-to-support-david-jolly/)
- Pro-Collins (primary): Florida Fighters (political nonprofit, donors undisclosed) funded nearly $1.1M in TV ads on Collins' Green Beret service (mid-2025), [NBC News](https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/ron-desantis-lobbying-megadonor-ken-griffin-stays-sidelines-florida-go-rcna244801)
- Historical: 2020 FEC MUR 7783 (CLC complaint) alleged Friends of Byron Donalds (then treasurer Noreen Fenner) and Conservatives for Effective Government (treasurer David Ramba) moved soft money to a super PAC backing his House run; FEC deadlocked 3-3 and closed file 6-0, "Dismissed-Other", [FEC MUR 7783](https://www.fec.gov/data/legal/matter-under-review/7783/)
- AdImpact projects nearly $60 million in ad spending tied to the governor race, [WUSF, Sept. 9, 2026](https://wusf.org/text/politics-issues/2026-09-09/what-to-know-about-pacs-your-florida-breaks-it-down)

##### Inferences
- Because Florida allows unlimited gifts to the candidate's affiliated PC, most "outside" money (Yass, Griffin, Club for Growth) flows directly into Friends of Byron Donalds rather than independent super PACs, so federal-style IE reporting understates the picture.

##### Gaps
- No evidence found of MAGA Inc., Fairshake, Defend American Jobs, or Preserve America spending in the governor race; Yass gave $16M to MAGA Inc. in H1 2025 but no transfer to Donalds was reported.
- Affordable Florida's funders were not identified in accessible sources.
- Total American Mission Florida spending through October is unknown.

#### Key Question 4: Florida state-level interests (Chamber, AIF, FPL/NextEra, insurance, sugar, Disney, Seminole, homebuilders, Publix)

##### Takeaway
Confirmed givers: NextEra/FPL ($1M+), Duke ($500K), TECO affiliate ($125K), Seminole Tribe ($5.5M), AIF PAC ($525K), Heritage P&C ($50K), and a large homebuilder bloc (Hosseini $500K, Neal $640K+, Hutson $350K, Davis family $300K, Beruff $100K, Dream Finders $50K). No accessible source documented direct gifts from U.S. Sugar, Florida Crystals, Disney, Publix, Citizens, or the Florida Chamber to Donalds' committee.

##### Cited Findings
- NextEra Energy $1,000,000; Duke Energy $500,000; TECO-associated company $125,000 (see Q2 citations)
- Seminole Tribe $5,500,000 through Sept. 18, 2026, [Bonus.com](https://www.bonus.com/news/gaming-money-flows-into-florida-governor-race-with-seminole-tribe-draftkings-and-fanduel-b/)
- Associated Industries of Florida PAC $525,000 and Florida PC $237,000+ (both described as sugar-funded conduits); VoteWater states no direct U.S. Sugar or Florida Crystals gifts, [VoteWater](https://votewater.org/dirty-money-talks-what-does-it-say-about-byron-donalds/)
- Florida Crystals $600,000 and U.S. Sugar $1,000,000 went to Florida Chamber committees Sept. to Oct. 2024 (not to Donalds directly; blog aggregation), [Steve Farley Substack](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/no-primary-no-debate-how-byron-donalds)
- Heritage P&C Insurance $50,000 (Aug. 2026), [Miami Times](https://www.miamitimesonline.com/news/florida/daniel-perez-political-committees-give-1m-boost-to-byron-donalds-campaign-for-governor/article_8eab53e4-a074-412b-80fa-c2d4cc22cd7d.html)
- Homebuilders: MHK of Volusia $500,000, Hutson $350,000, DDI/PARC/Big Creek Timber $300,000, Dream Finders $50,000, Neal $640,000+, Beruff $100,000; none gave to Jolly, [Seeking Rents](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/in-the-race-for-florida-governor); [Bradenton Journal](https://thebradentonjournal.substack.com/p/donations-from-neal-beruff-add-to)
- Florida Chamber, Florida Retail Federation, AIF, FRLA, ABC Florida endorsed (no direct amounts found), [WLRN, Aug. 17, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-17/will-byron-donalds-dominance-in-fundraising-result-in-a-gop-victory)
- Publix: gave $100,000 to Perez's Conservatives for a Better Florida in Q4 2023, which later gave $500,000 to Donalds (indirect only), [WMNF](https://wmnf.org/publix-gives-100000-political-committee-future-florida-house-speaker-daniel-perez)
- Mori Hosseini attended a June 2025 DeSantis dinner about finding a Donalds challenger, then gave $500,000 to Donalds Sept. 15, 2026, [NBC News](https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/ron-desantis-lobbying-megadonor-ken-griffin-stays-sidelines-florida-go-rcna244801); [Seeking Rents](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/in-the-race-for-florida-governor)

##### Inferences
- The Sept. 15, 2026 cluster of homebuilder gifts (Hosseini, Hutson, Davis, Zalupski, $1M in one day) looks like a coordinated post-primary consolidation by the Florida development industry around the nominee.

##### Gaps
- Disney, Publix direct, Citizens/insurer trade groups, Florida Chamber PAC direct amounts, Florida Crystals/U.S. Sugar direct: not found; needs DOE database query.

#### Key Question 5: Republican primary result and funding of primary rivals

##### Takeaway
Donalds won the Aug. 18, 2026 GOP primary with 47.78 percent (810,916 votes) over Jay Collins 25.16 percent (426,891), James Fishback 10.47 percent (177,723), and Paul Renner; Casey DeSantis did not run. Collins' Quiet Professionals FL PC raised just under $10M including a single $5M gift from Propel Florida LLC on May 29, 2026; Renner's PC raised $6.2M; Fishback's Florida First PAC about $1.2M.

##### Cited Findings
- Primary results (as of Aug. 25, 2026): Donalds 810,916 (47.78%), Collins 426,891 (25.16%), Fishback 177,723 (10.47%), [Decision Desk HQ](https://votes.decisiondeskhq.com/races/2026-08-18/florida-governor-republican-primary); Donalds 47.8% with 99% in, Jolly won Dem primary with 61%, [Spectrum News 13, Aug. 19, 2026](https://mynews13.com/fl/orlando/news/2026/08/19/florida-governor-primary-results)
- Donalds lost 17 counties to Collins, [Florida Phoenix, Oct. 6, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/10/06/new-ad-targets-byron-donalds-in-the-counties-he-lost-in-the-gop-primary/)
- Rivals as of Aug. 17, 2026: Collins Quiet Professionals FL a little under $10M raised, $220,000 cash; Collins campaign $562,000 raised, $203,000 cash. Renner PC $6.2M raised, $164,000 cash; campaign $490,000 raised, $210,000 cash. Fishback Florida First PAC $1.2M+; campaign $705,000 incl. $100,000 self-loan Aug. 13, $2,371 cash, [WLRN, Aug. 17, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-17/will-byron-donalds-dominance-in-fundraising-result-in-a-gop-victory)
- Propel Florida LLC gave $5,000,000 to Quiet Professionals on May 29, 2026, nearly all of its $5.12M April to May haul; described as a group formed to combat Big Tech, [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/florida-governor-race-donors-follow-the-money-by); [WLRN, June 12, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-06-12/byron-donalds-continues-fundraising-domination-in-florida-governors-race)
- Q1 2026: Collins raised $1.74M, spent ~$640K, $2.7M cash, $245K in-kind from RPOF; Renner $800K raised, $1.88M spent ($1.5M ads), $3.7M cash, $302K in-kind from RPOF; Fishback $276K raised, $255K spent, $27K cash, [Highlands News-Sun](https://www.midfloridanewspapers.com/highlands_news-sun/news/donalds-dominates-fundraising-in-governor-race/article_70925d94-73c0-4f61-9737-e3805c0a1d70.html)
- Collins received $118,463.22 in state matching funds, [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/florida-governor-race-donors-follow-the-money-by)
- DeSantis sought a challenger in mid-2025 (Miami dinner with Cason Carter of Citadel, Mori Hosseini, Manny Kadre); Florida Fighters nonprofit spent ~$1.1M on Collins-themed ads, [NBC News](https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/ron-desantis-lobbying-megadonor-ken-griffin-stays-sidelines-florida-go-rcna244801)
- Donalds' primary ad blitz: Friends of Byron Donalds PAC $20M statewide buy announced June 25, 2026, launching June 30 through Aug. 18 (cable, broadcast, streaming, radio), [FL Voice News](https://flvoicenews.com/donalds-pac-announces-20-million-statewide-ad-campaign-ahead-florida-gop-primary/)
- Donalds had spent $3,119,572 of $7,104,287 raised in the candidate account as of Apr. 1, 2026 (Wikipedia table, candidate account only), [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Florida_gubernatorial_election)

##### Inferences
- Propel Florida LLC is the only major pro-Collins funder identified and its principals were not disclosed in accessible sources.

##### Gaps
- Renner vote count and percentage not captured; Propel Florida's backers unknown; Casey DeSantis never entered.

#### Key Question 6: Consultants, media vendors, fundraising firms, and Trump-world ties

##### Takeaway
Accessible sources name very few vendors. Confirmed: treasurer Bradley Crate / Red Curve Solutions (Beverly, MA; also treasurer for Trump-aligned committees), chief strategist and PAC chair Ryan Smith, communications director Gates McGavick, and a $20M June to August 2026 media buy plus a seven-figure "Agenda" buy in August. Itemized payees were not retrievable because the state expenditure database and Transparency USA were unreachable.

##### Cited Findings
- Bradley T. Crate, c/o Red Curve Solutions, Beverly, MA is treasurer of both Friends of Byron Donalds PAC and Byron Donalds for Congress, [FL DOE #89043](https://dos.elections.myflorida.com/committees/ComDetail.asp?account=89043); [WLRN, Aug. 20, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-20/byron-donalds-kept-house-re-election-campaign-alive-during-run-for-florida-governor)
- Ryan Smith: chief strategist and PAC chairman, [Floridian Press, Jan. 2026](https://floridianpress.com/2026/01/byron-donalds-hauls-45m-in-2025-over-13m-in-q4-for-gubernatorial-bid/); [FL Voice News](https://flvoicenews.com/donalds-pac-announces-20-million-statewide-ad-campaign-ahead-florida-gop-primary/)
- Gates McGavick: communications director / spokesman, [Floridian Press, June 2, 2026](https://floridianpress.com/2026/06/byron-donalds-adds-13-8-million-to-gubernatorial-war-chest-brings-total-to-over-81-million/); [Hoodline](https://hoodline.com/2026/07/wall-street-whale-ken-griffin-drops-10-million-bomb-on-florida-governor-race-6970828/)
- Friends of Byron Donalds PAC $20,000,000 statewide buy, June 30 to Aug. 18, 2026, [FL Voice News](https://flvoicenews.com/donalds-pac-announces-20-million-statewide-ad-campaign-ahead-florida-gop-primary/); seven-figure "Agenda" ad vs. Jolly announced Aug. 10, 2026, [AOL / Daily Caller](https://www.aol.com/articles/byron-donalds-pac-launches-seven-131903000.html)
- Donalds and PAC spent $91.7M through Sept. 25, 2026, [CBS Miami](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/byron-donalds-new-ads-florida-governor-race-david-jolly/); Jolly claimed more than $75M on TV, [FOX 13](https://www.fox13news.com/news/david-jolly-addresses-campaign-cash-gap-florida-governor-race)
- House account paid $136,561.47 for security services and $36,000 to an unnamed polling/research firm in 2025, [WLRN, Aug. 20, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-20/byron-donalds-kept-house-re-election-campaign-alive-during-run-for-florida-governor)
- Outside vendor chain (American Mission Florida, not the campaign): Summit Ridge Media Group LLC, Strategic Media Placement / The Strategy Group Co. (Ben Yoho), Lantern Production Consultants, [Hoodline, July 2, 2026](https://hoodline.com/2026/07/secretive-pro-ai-cash-fuels-byron-donalds-tv-blitz-across-florida/)
- Primary-era poll cited by campaign allies: Ryan Tyson poll (late Feb. 2026), [Highlands News-Sun](https://www.midfloridanewspapers.com/highlands_news-sun/news/donalds-dominates-fundraising-in-governor-race/article_70925d94-73c0-4f61-9737-e3805c0a1d70.html)
- Unverified blog claims (Steve Farley Substack, treat as leads only): aide Larry Wilcoxson received ~$419,000 from Donalds committees 2020 to 2025; Susie Wiles / James Blair / Silver Bullet Strategies / September Group named in Donalds orbit, [Steve Farley Substack](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/no-primary-no-debate-how-byron-donalds)

##### Inferences
- Red Curve Solutions (Crate) is the compliance shop used by Trump's campaign and MAGA Inc., so the treasurer is the clearest documented Trump-world vendor link.

##### Gaps
- Itemized Friends of Byron Donalds payees (media buyer, mail, digital, fundraising consultants, polling) were not retrievable; the Florida Politics July 2026 "spends big" article (archive 808342) and the DOE expenditure export are the sources to pull.

#### Comparison to David Jolly (for context)

##### Takeaway
Jolly's candidate account plus PC "Florida 2026" had raised under $22 million by Oct. 20, 2026 against Donalds' ~$149 million; his largest donors are Donald Sussman ($600K), Charles Wall ($525K), Barbara Stiefel ($100K), and J.B. Pritzker ($50K), with a $3M+ Bill Clinton Miami event in October.

##### Cited Findings
- Jolly PC is named "Florida 2026"; raised nearly $6M as of Sept. 7, 2026; Sussman $600,000; Charles Wall (ex-Altria GC) $525,000, [WUSF, Sept. 9, 2026](https://wusf.org/text/politics-issues/2026-09-09/what-to-know-about-pacs-your-florida-breaks-it-down)
- Aug. 17, 2026: campaign $6.3M raised, $2.9M cash; PC $3.9M raised, $864,937 cash, [WLRN, Aug. 17, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-17/will-byron-donalds-dominance-in-fundraising-result-in-a-gop-victory)
- $15.8M (Sept. 25), $16M (Sept. 18), $16.7M raised / $11.2M spent (Sept. 25 CBS), under $17M (Oct. 6), under $22M (Oct. 20); Clinton Miami fundraiser $3M+, [Florida Phoenix, Sept. 25](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/09/25/jolly-to-national-democrats-show-me-some-money/); [CBS Miami](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/byron-donalds-new-ads-florida-governor-race-david-jolly/); [Florida Phoenix, Oct. 20](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/10/20/democratic-governors-association-investing-1-million-to-support-david-jolly/)
- J.B. Pritzker $50,000, Jolly's only billionaire donor; 19 percent of Jolly money from out of state; Jolly has more unique donors and about twice as many Florida donors as Donalds, [KDH / Tampa Bay Times](https://kdhnews.com/news/politics/18-billionaires-have-funded-the-florida-governor-race-17-gave-to-donalds/article_f4a68240-1521-537e-b8cb-9006156ebc14.html)
- Barbara Stiefel $100,000 and Sussman $100,000 (2025), [Yahoo/Herald](https://www.yahoo.com/news/byron-donalds-dwarfs-david-jolly-163544449.html)

##### Gaps
- Jolly October cash on hand not reported in accessible sources.

### Note: personal_finances_and_erika.md

_Folder: Byron Donalds money and donor network_

### Byron Donalds personal finances, business interests, and Erika Donalds' education ventures

Research date: October 22, 2026. All figures dated to the filing or article that reports them. House disclosures report assets in ranges, so "net worth" figures are range midpoints computed by third parties, not official numbers.

#### Key Question 1: House financial disclosures 2020 through 2025: assets, income, liabilities, net worth, and stock/crypto trades

##### Takeaway
Donalds entered Congress reporting assets of roughly $69,000 to $985,000 and a Moran Wealth partnership stake; his most recent annual report (2025 filing year, filed Aug. 13, 2026) shows $1.6M to $7M in assets, driven almost entirely by his wife's holding company Onesto LLC and its Optima subsidiaries, plus a spouse bitcoin IRA of $50,001 to $100,000, offset by a $500,001 to $1M mortgage and a $250,001 to $500,000 federal student loan. He and his wife trade individual stocks through a Moran Wealth IRA, failed to file any periodic transaction reports for 2022 and 2023 (108 trades, up to $1.6M, per a Campaign Legal Center OCE complaint), and bought bitcoin the same day he signed a letter asking the IRS to loosen crypto staking tax rules while sitting on the Financial Services Digital Assets subcommittee.

##### Cited Findings

**2020 candidate filing (amended, filed Feb. 9, 2021, Filing ID 10039521, covering Jan. 1, 2019 to June 30, 2020)**
- Earned income: State of Florida salary $26,389.80 (current period) and $26,624.76 (prior year); Moran Wealth Management LLC salary $89,537.65 and partnership income $82,465.00 (current period); Wells Fargo Clearing Services salary $66,824.76 (prior year); Moran Edwards Asset Management LLC salary $7,916.66 (prior year), [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- Asset: Moran Wealth Management LLC (Naples wealth management firm) ownership stake valued $100,001 to $250,000, capital gains income $15,001 to $50,000 in the current period, [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- Moran Wealth IRA held roughly 29 individual stocks each valued $1,001 to $15,000 (including Alibaba, AMETEK, Anthem, AT&T, Biogen, Bristol-Myers, Honeywell, Mastercard, UnitedHealth, Verizon, Zebra, Zoetis); Moran Wealth 401k mutual funds; Florida Retirement System funds, [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- Spouse assets: Boston Private Financial Holdings 401(k); DGHM Profit Sharing Plan 401K (Dalton, Greiner, Hartman, Maher & Co.); FRS 2045 Retirement Fund, [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- Spouse income sources listed with amounts "N/A": Educator Solutions (salary), Onesto LLC (partnership income), Boston Private Financial Holdings (partnership income), Dalton Greiner Hartman Maher & Co. (salary and partnership income), Vestcor Family Foundation Inc. (1099 income), School District of Collier County (salary), [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- Liabilities: Nelnet 2002 student loan $10,000 to $15,000 (filer); Nelnet 2002 student loan $15,001 to $50,000 (spouse); Chase credit card incurred Dec. 2019 $15,001 to $50,000; Boston Private 401(k) loan from Aug. 2012 $10,000 to $15,000 (spouse), [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- Positions: Partner and Employee, Moran Wealth Management LLC; Employee, Moran Edwards Asset Management LLC; Employee, Wells Fargo Advisors; Representative, State of Florida; Member, Collier County Republican Executive Committee, [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- Schedule J: compensation over $5,000 from confidential financial advisory clients in Naples, names withheld under a confidentiality agreement, [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- First congressional disclosure (2021) reported assets between $69,044 and $984,998, [Florida Phoenix, Apr. 20, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/04/20/jay-collins-says-byron-donalds-is-not-a-risk-that-we-can-afford-to-take/)

**2023 annual report, amended (Filing ID 10071798, filed Aug. 13, 2025)**
- Spouse holds 100% of Onesto, LLC (holding company, spouse sole member), which holds Optima Management Services, LLC (back-office services) and OptimaEd, LLC (curriculum development), each valued $1,000,001 to $5,000,000, income type "None", [House Clerk 2023 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2023/10071798.pdf)
- Spouse retirement: Inspira Financial IRA $50,001 to $100,000; FRS 2045 fund $15,001 to $50,000, [House Clerk 2023 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2023/10071798.pdf)
- Moran Wealth IRA held roughly 45 stock positions of $1,001 to $15,000 each (Alphabet, Bristol-Myers, Cadence, Chemed, Dynatrace, FactSet, Gartner, GM, Howmet, Humana, Ingersoll Rand, Intuit, JPMorgan, Lockheed, Mastercard, Merck, Meta, Microsoft, Molina, Monolithic Power, Monster, MSCI, Novo Nordisk, NVIDIA, Parker-Hannifin, Regeneron, RLI, Sony, Trane, Veeva, Vertex, Zoetis), [House Clerk 2023 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2023/10071798.pdf)
- 2023 transactions (all $1,001 to $15,000): Feb. 24, 2023 bought Gartner, Humana, MSCI, STMicro, sold Fortinet, Waters, Zebra, Intuitive Surgical; July 5, 2023 bought Microsoft, RLI, Meta, sold Arch Resources, Revvity, WaFd; Oct. 19, 2023 sold Elevance, QuidelOrtho, Edwards Lifesciences, STMicro, bought Dynatrace, JPMorgan, Alphabet, NVIDIA, [House Clerk 2023 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2023/10071798.pdf)
- Spouse earned income: Educator Solutions salary N/A; Optima Management Services salary N/A; Optima Management Services LLC business income N/A; Onesto LLC honorarium $6,000, [House Clerk 2023 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2023/10071798.pdf)
- Liability: Chase credit card (joint), incurred Jan. 2021, $15,001 to $50,000, [House Clerk 2023 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2023/10071798.pdf)
- Travel: Club for Growth sponsored trip Mar. 2 to 5, 2023, Washington DC to Palm Beach FL, [House Clerk 2023 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2023/10071798.pdf)
- The original 2023 report had valued OptimaEd as "over $1,000,000" and did not disclose the spouse's stake in Optima Management Services at all; the amendment came two weeks after a June 2025 Florida Bulldog story, [Florida Bulldog, Sept. 7, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/09/disclosures-deepend-mystery-rep-donaldss-wifes-charter-school-business/)

**2024 annual report (filed Aug. 13, 2025)**
- Net worth estimate $4.3M; 68 assets totaling $1.6M to $7M: LLC/partnership $3.8M (3 entries), stocks $344K (44 entries), retirement $228K (21 entries), [Congress Stock Tracker](https://www.congressstock.com/politicians/byron-donalds/net-worth)
- Optima Management Services LLC $1,000,001 to $5,000,000; OptimaEd LLC $500,001 to $1,000,000 (down from $1M to $5M in amended 2023); Onesto LLC 100% N/A; Inspira Financial IRA $50,001 to $100,000; FRS 2045 $15,001 to $50,000; one liability, Chase credit card $15,001 to $50,000, [Congress Stock Tracker](https://www.congressstock.com/politicians/byron-donalds/net-worth); OptimaEd drop also reported by [Florida Bulldog, Sept. 7, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/09/disclosures-deepend-mystery-rep-donaldss-wifes-charter-school-business/)
- 2024 disclosure shows Erika no longer receives a salary from Educator Solutions, [Florida Bulldog, Sept. 7, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/09/disclosures-deepend-mystery-rep-donaldss-wifes-charter-school-business/)
- Net worth $1.6M to $7M per most recent disclosure (August 2025), [Florida Phoenix, Apr. 20, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/04/20/jay-collins-says-byron-donalds-is-not-a-risk-that-we-can-afford-to-take/)

**2025 annual report (Filing ID 10075306, filed Aug. 13, 2026)**
- Alto IRA (spouse): Bitcoin $50,001 to $100,000, IRA transferred from Inspira Financial in Dec. 2025, [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)
- Optima Ed, Inc 401k (spouse): Nuveen Lifecycle Index 2045 Fund $100,001 to $250,000, [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)
- Onesto LLC (100%, spouse sole member) holds: Optima Management Services LLC $250,001 to $500,000 (down from $1M to $5M); OptimaEd, Inc. $500,001 to $1,000,000, converted to a C-Corp in Nov. 2025; Dewalt Impact Group, LLC (Lewes, DE, held through Optima Management Services) $250,001 to $500,000 with business income $100,001 to $1,000,000, [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)
- Moran Wealth IRA (filer and spouse) roughly 30 stocks at $1,001 to $15,000 each including Alphabet, Amazon, Amphenol, Arista, Autodesk, Broadcom, Brown & Brown, Cadence, Carnival, Chipotle, Comfort Systems, Fortinet, Howmet, IDEXX, Intuit, Lam Research, Mastercard, McKesson, Meta, Microsoft, Monolithic Power, NVIDIA, Parker-Hannifin ($15,001 to $50,000), PayPal, Progressive, Trade Desk, Tradeweb, Trane, Visa, Zoetis, [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)

Amazon, Abbott, Chipotle, Progressive, PayPal, Fiserv, Broadcom; 11 sells incl. First Citizens, MSCI, Gartner, Robinhood, JPMorgan, Novo Nordisk, Equifax, Vertex); Apr. 10 (Amphenol, Visa buys; Regeneron, Nu Holdings sells); Aug. 7 (Lam Research buy; Deckers, Howmet sells); Sept. 4 (SPY buy $15,001 to $50,000, IDEXX buy; 8 sells); Oct. 9 (SPY sell $15,001 to $50,000; 7 buys); Oct. 23 and 24; Dec. 4 (Comfort Systems buy, Abbott sell); Dec. 18 Bitcoin purchase (spouse, Alto IRA) $50,001 to $100,000, [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)
- Spouse earned income: OptimaEd, Inc. salary N/A; Dewalt Impact Group LLC business income N/A; X Corp. 1099 income N/A. No filer earned income beyond salary, [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)

- Schedules E, F, G, H, I: none disclosed (no positions, agreements, gifts, or sponsored travel), [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)

**Third-party net worth estimates**
- Naples Daily News estimated net worth in Feb. 2025 at $630,000 to $2.6M; Quiver Quantitative estimates $4.63M, [Florida Phoenix, Feb. 4, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/02/04/paul-renner-says-byron-donalds-has-enriched-himself-in-office/)
- Opinion piece claims: 2020 official range $69,044 to $984,998 (Quiver ~$81,000); 2023 official $1,527,064 to $2,966,000 (Quiver ~$1.27M); latest $1.6M to $7M (Quiver $4.37M to $4.63M). The outlet is a local site and the piece is first-person advocacy, not straight news, [Lakeland Gazette, Aug. 5, 2026](https://lakelandgazette.info/news/2026/08/05/expose-how-did-byron-donalds-get-so-rich-in-congress/)

**Stock trading and STOCK Act compliance**
- Sept. 5, 2024: Campaign Legal Center filed an OCE complaint alleging Donalds and his spouse made 108 trades worth up to $1.6M in 2022 and 2023 without filing any periodic transaction reports, [Campaign Legal Center press release](https://campaignlegal.org/press-releases/rep-byron-donalds-florida-fails-disclose-16-million-stock-trades-campaign-legal); [CLC complaint PDF](https://thecapitolist.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/CLC-OCE-Complaint_Rep.-Donalds.pdf)
- Trades involved more than three dozen companies (Alcoa, Alphabet, Zebra, Zions Bancorp among them); CLC focused on 12 transactions in 2023 in six companies under House Financial Services jurisdiction: Elevance Health, Humana, JPMorgan Chase, MSCI, RLI Corp., WaFd. Elevance gave $2,500 and JPMorgan PAC $3,000 to his campaign in 2023; JPMorgan lobbied on five bills he co-sponsored. CLC said he was the only member it found who "completely failed to report any PTRs for two years." Late PTR penalty is $200; knowing and willful violations carry up to $61,585 civil penalty and up to one year in prison, [Florida Bulldog, Sept. 2024](https://floridabulldog.org/2024/09/republican-rep-byron-donalds-failed-timely-report-stock-trades)
- On Mar. 7, 2022 TV appearance Donalds said STOCK Act violators should face sanctions and that he had "traded securities for a living", [Florida Bulldog, Sept. 2024](https://floridabulldog.org/2024/09/republican-rep-byron-donalds-failed-timely-report-stock-trades)
- Capitol Trades profile: 204 total trades, 66 issuers, 1.70M volume (past three years), [Capitol Trades buzz, Jan. 9, 2026](https://www.capitoltrades.com/buzz/byron-donalds-makes-first-bitcoin-buy-at-six-month-low-2026-01-09)
- LegiStorm filing index: 2024 annual plus 2 PTRs (Nov. 7 and 8, 2024); 2025 annual plus 7 PTRs; 2026 annual plus 8 PTRs (Jan. 7, Jan. 9, Feb. 12, Mar. 6, Apr. 8, May 13, Jul. 15, Sept. 15, 2026); 2022 and 2023 show annual reports only, no PTRs, [LegiStorm](https://www.legistorm.com/pfd_office/member/Byron_Donalds/3395.html)
- Apr. 10, 2025 trades (Visa, Nu Holdings, Amphenol, Regeneron, $8,008 to $120,000 combined) came about a week after the "Liberation Day" tariff announcement, [NOTUS, Jan. 9, 2026](https://www.notus.org/congress/byron-donalds-crypto-rules-bitcoin)

**Crypto**
- Dec. 18, 2025: Donalds and 18 other members sent a letter to IRS acting commissioner Scott Bessent asking the IRS to drop treatment of crypto staking rewards as taxable income on receipt. The same day, $50,001 to $100,000 of bitcoin was purchased; his office said wife Erika made the purchase. Spokesman Daniel First: "Byron's wife, who is a business and finance professional, purchased the Bitcoin" and the letter "had no bearing whatsoever on the spot price of Bitcoin." Donalds had not reported owning bitcoin in his prior annual disclosure. In May 2025 he introduced a bill to codify the Strategic Bitcoin Reserve. Former Rep. Reid Ribble: "The timing of it really, really looks bad.", [NOTUS, Jan. 9, 2026](https://www.notus.org/congress/byron-donalds-crypto-rules-bitcoin)
- PTR filed Feb. 12, 2026 (Filing ID 20033981): Jan. 8, 2026 spouse bitcoin purchase $1,001 to $15,000; Jan. 8, 2026 sales of Howmet (partial), Parker-Hannifin (partial), Trade Desk (filer and spouse) in Moran Wealth IRA, each $1,001 to $15,000, [House Clerk PTR 20033981](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/ptr-pdfs/2026/20033981.pdf)
- Benzinga noted the bitcoin buy was disclosed alongside an Abbott sale and Comfort Systems buy, and that he sits on the Financial Services Subcommittee on Digital Assets, Financial Technology and AI, [Benzinga via Finviz, Jan. 9, 2026](https://finviz.com/news/272536/trumps-pick-for-next-governor-of-florida-buys-bitcoin-is-congressional-committee-a-conflict)
- Committee assignments (119th Congress): Financial Services (Subcommittees on Digital Assets, Financial Technology and AI; Financial Institutions); Oversight and Government Reform, [Wikipedia, Byron Donalds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byron_Donalds)

##### Inferences
- The jump in reported assets between the 2021 and 2024 filings is almost entirely the valuation of Erika's Onesto LLC subsidiaries (Optima Management Services and OptimaEd) rather than any asset Byron personally accumulated; the Moran Wealth IRA positions have stayed in the $1,001 to $15,000 per-position band throughout.
- The 2025 filing shows the Optima valuations falling (OMS from $1M to $5M down to $250K to $500K) at the same time a new entity, Dewalt Impact Group LLC, appears with business income of $100,001 to $1M, suggesting a restructuring of how Erika's income is routed. No reporting on Dewalt Impact Group was found.
- The large new liabilities (mortgage July 2024, federal student loan Sept. 2021 at $250K to $500K) mean the headline $1.6M to $7M asset figure overstates net worth substantially.

##### Gaps
- Could not fetch the official 2024 annual report PDF directly (House Clerk returned an error); figures come from Congress Stock Tracker and Florida Bulldog summaries of it.
- OpenSecrets personal finances page is blocked from this environment.
- No OCE or House Ethics Committee public action on the CLC complaint was found; status appears unresolved as of October 2026.
- No Florida Form 6 (state full and public disclosure) for Donalds as a gubernatorial candidate was located; the only Form 6 search hit was DeSantis'. Candidates qualifying in June 2026 must file one, so it should exist on disclosure.floridaethics.gov.
- The 2021 and 2022 annual reports were not fetched directly; LegiStorm confirms they exist.
- Washington Examiner Feb. 22, 2026 article on crypto divestment was cited secondhand but could not be located.

#### Key Question 2: Career background in finance and arrest history

##### Takeaway
Donalds spent his pre-political career in Southwest Florida banking and wealth management (TIB Bank, CMG Surety, Wells Fargo Advisors, Moran Wealth Management) and chaired the Florida House Insurance and Banking Subcommittee; his 1997 marijuana charge was dismissed through pretrial diversion and a 2000 felony charge (described variously as theft or bribery) ended in a no contest plea, probation, and later sealing and expungement.

##### Cited Findings
- Born Oct. 28, 1978, NYC; B.A. finance and marketing, Florida State University, 2002. 2003 credit analyst at TIB Bank (senior credit analyst 2004); 2007 portfolio manager at CMG Surety LLC; 2015 financial advisor at Wells Fargo Advisors. Florida House District 80, Nov. 8, 2016 to Nov. 3, 2020; chaired Insurance and Banking Subcommittee, [Wikipedia, Byron Donalds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byron_Donalds)
- 2020 disclosure confirms employment at Wells Fargo Advisors ($66,824.76 salary in 2019), Moran Edwards Asset Management LLC, and partner/employee at Moran Wealth Management LLC with a $100,001 to $250,000 equity stake, [House Clerk 2020 amended filing](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2020/10039521.pdf)
- 1997: at 19, notice to appear on misdemeanor marijuana possession in Leon County; pretrial diversion, charge dismissed, $150 fee. PolitiFact found the record in court files and no evidence of expungement. 2000: arrested and charged with felony theft, pleaded no contest, one year probation per spokesman Mark Harris; campaign said records later sealed and expunged; case does not appear in Leon County records. In 2014 Donalds said a woman offered him money for his debit card and PIN and $7,000 in bad checks were cashed on his account. He checked "no" to arrest questions on a 2014 application for the Edison State College board. PolitiFact rated a Casey Askar ad calling him a convicted felon "Mostly False", [PolitiFact, Oct. 2025 republish of 2020 check](https://politifact.com/factchecks/2025/oct/25/casey-askar/florida-gop-candidates-ad-misleads-about-rivals-cr/)
- July 2020 Florida Commission on Ethics complaint by Kelly Lichter alleged he concealed a 1997 marijuana distribution arrest and 2000 bribery arrest on applications for the FSW State College board, a Mason Classical Academy board position, and a FINRA securities license; the Commission dismissed it for lack of legal sufficiency and jurisdiction questions (reported Oct. 6, 2020). Donalds said the bribery record was expunged after a no contest plea and two years' probation, [Florida Political Review, Oct. 2020](https://www.floridapoliticalreview.com/ethics-complaint-against-byron-donalds-dismissed)
- WUSF describes the 2000 charge as bribery, which Donalds attributed to depositing a bad check, [WUSF, Feb. 26, 2025](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2025-02-26/things-know-about-byron-donalds-filed-paperwork-run-governor)
- Jay Collins' April 2026 opposition document repeated the 2000 bribery arrest and two years' probation claim and said Donalds acknowledged to CBS Miami that he sold marijuana as a youth, [Florida Phoenix, Apr. 20, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/04/20/jay-collins-says-byron-donalds-is-not-a-risk-that-we-can-afford-to-take/)

##### Inferences
- Sources conflict on whether the 2000 charge was "bribery" or "felony theft" and on probation length (one vs. two years); the record is expunged so no primary document is available. Both labels appear in reputable outlets and should be reported as such.

##### Gaps
- No FINRA BrokerCheck record was pulled; whether he still holds any securities licenses is unverified.
- Details of his role and tenure at Moran Wealth Management (joining date, exit, whether the equity stake was sold) were not found beyond the 2020 filing.

#### Key Question 3: Erika Donalds' companies, nonprofits, and public money flows

##### Takeaway
Erika Donalds built a network of Hillsdale-affiliated classical charter schools through the nonprofit Optima Foundation (renamed Education Freedom Foundation in March 2025) and then shifted the management business to two for-profit firms she owns through Onesto LLC, OptimaEd and Optima Management Services; auditor filings show the Foundation received roughly $15.1M in management fees from three schools, the for-profits took in $5.84M in 2023 alone, CBS put the total at about $35M (30% of government funding) from 2020 to 2023, and all four opened schools have since cut ties, with one suing.

##### Cited Findings

**Biography and public roles**
- Born 1980 as Erika Brynne Lees; B.S. accounting FSU 2002; M.S. accounting FAU 2006; worked at Dalton, Greiner, Hartman, Maher & Co. (DGHM) 2002 to 2018; married Byron Mar. 15, 2003; Constitution Revision Commission 2017 to 2018 (appointed by Speaker Richard Corcoran); FGCU Board of Trustees appointed by DeSantis Mar. 2022; leads education policy at America First Policy Institute (Politico, June 30, 2025); advisory boards of Classical Learning Test, Moms for Liberty, Independent Women's Forum Education Freedom Center; former Heritage Foundation visiting fellow; Oct. 2025 Leadership Institute campus tour, [Wikipedia, Erika Donalds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erika_Donalds)
- Won Collier County School Board seat 2014, did not seek re-election; chairs AFPI's Center for Education Opportunity (joined 2025), [WUSF, Feb. 26, 2025](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2025-02-26/things-know-about-byron-donalds-filed-paperwork-run-governor); [Florida Trib, July 30, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/07/30/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida/)
- Helped establish Mason Classical Academy (Hillsdale-affiliated, Naples) in 2014; she and Byron later fell out with its leadership, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)

**Entities (exact names and registrations)**
- Optima Foundation Inc., EIN 82-3745515, Naples FL, 501(c)(3) since March 2019; formed late 2017 with Shawn Frost and Louise Penta (Sunbiz N17000012066); renamed Education Freedom Foundation March 2025, [ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer](https://projects.propublica.org/nonprofits/organizations/823745515); [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- Onesto, LLC: anonymous Delaware entity formed late 2018; Erika sole member; holds OptimaEd and Optima Management Services, [Florida Bulldog, Sept. 7, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/09/disclosures-deepend-mystery-rep-donaldss-wifes-charter-school-business/)
- OptimaEd, LLC: formed 2020; Byron reported Erika owns 81%; converted to OptimaEd, Inc. (C-Corp) Nov. 2025, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/); [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)
- Optima Management Services, LLC: formed 2022, originally Delaware at the same address and incorporation service as Onesto; Florida Sunbiz doc M22000018873 lists Erika as founder and sole manager, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/); [Florida Bulldog, Sept. 7, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/09/disclosures-deepend-mystery-rep-donaldss-wifes-charter-school-business/)
- Dewalt Impact Group, LLC (Lewes, DE), held through Optima Management Services, first appears in the 2025 annual report, [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)
- Educator Solutions: fictitious name (Sunbiz G18000107544) of ESI Technical Inc., founded by state Rep. John Snyder (R-Stuart), [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- OptimaEd and Optima Management Services share an address on South Horseshoe Drive, Naples, in a building owned by Quest Educational Foundation (led by Tom Grady and Ed Morton), which runs the Freedom Institute of Collier County private school; Quest reports no rental income, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)

**Schools**
- Treasure Coast Classical Academy (Stuart, Martin County), approved April 2018, Hillsdale affiliate, first Optima charter, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- Jacksonville Classical Academy opened Aug. 2020 (John Rood, Vestcor Companies, helped launch); Naples Classical Academy opened 2021; Jacksonville Classical East opened 2022, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- Optima Classical Academy (virtual reality charter, also called Optima Academy Online) first year ended June 2023; accepts Florida taxpayer-funded scholarships, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/); [AOL repost of Miami Herald](https://www.aol.com/articles/byron-donalds-runs-governor-wife-150251717.html)
- Optima Classical Academy at Gladiolus, Fort Myers (Lee County): broke ground 2023, never opened; delayed from fall 2024 to 2025 to 2026; Aug. 2025 Erika sought to transfer the building to another operator, Lee County School Board approved Oct. 2025, [CBS via Ravitch, Dec. 4, 2025](https://dianeravitch.net/2025/12/04/florida-wife-of-gop-gubernatorial-candidate-builds-a-for-profit-charter-school-empire); [Fox 4, 2025](https://www.fox4now.com/news/local-news/abandoned-fort-myers-charter-school-becomes-flashpoint-in-florida-governor-race)

**Money flows (public funds to Donalds-linked entities)**
- Management fees paid to Optima Foundation per tax filings and auditor reports: Treasure Coast Classical $4,303,822; Naples Classical $8,424,572; Jacksonville Classical $2,398,221, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- Optima Foundation paid Erika $183,326 in 2021 (FY ending June 2022 990), the only salary on record; Byron did not report it in 2021 or 2022 disclosures, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/); [ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer](https://projects.propublica.org/nonprofits/organizations/823745515)
- Optima Foundation 990 financials: FY June 2020 revenue $905,605; June 2021 $1,782,383; June 2022 $4,156,778; June 2023 $3,488,835; Dec. 2023 (short year) $635,543; Dec. 2024 $1,429,852, expenses $842,151, net assets $2,832,995. Dec. 2024 COO Maddy Vigil paid $130,000; Erika Donalds (President) $0, [ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer](https://projects.propublica.org/nonprofits/organizations/823745515)
- Byron reported Erika earned more than $500,000 total salary from Educator Solutions 2020 to 2022; Optima-run schools paid Educator Solutions $6,930,584 in 2020 to 2022 and the Foundation paid it $2,783,216 for payroll services; Snyder's disclosures show nearly $700,000 from ESI since 2020, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- OptimaEd and Optima Management Services together took in $5,843,969 in 2023 for management, payroll, and IT; Foundation paid OMS $143,415 for 2023 payroll and ESI about $1.76M, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- Naples Classical: 12% of revenues management fee; $1.24M to Foundation in 2021-22; $1.56M to Optima Management Services in 2023-24, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- Optima Classical Academy (virtual) service fees to OptimaEd: $1,085,266 in 2023 and $2.1M in 2024, nearly 89% of the $3,588,660 in government funds it received over those two years. Contract signed Oct. 11, 2022, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/); [Lakeland Gazette](https://lakelandgazette.info/news/2026/08/05/expose-how-did-byron-donalds-get-so-rich-in-congress/)
- Optima Foundation and affiliated charters paid more than $10M to the two for-profit firms, [Florida Bulldog, Sept. 7, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/09/disclosures-deepend-mystery-rep-donaldss-wifes-charter-school-business/)
- CBS News (Dec. 2, 2025): between 2020 and 2023 the schools spent roughly 30% of government funding, about $35M, on outside firms tied to Erika Donalds; University of Miami charter expert found students at the four opened schools performed below average in math and reading, [CBS via Ravitch, Dec. 4, 2025](https://dianeravitch.net/2025/12/04/florida-wife-of-gop-gubernatorial-candidate-builds-a-for-profit-charter-school-empire)
- Summit Construction Management Group LLC (Jacksonville, co-founder Charles "Chip" Cordes) contracts: $14.4M Jacksonville Classical (2019), $20M Naples Classical campus (2020), $1M Jacksonville East renovation (2022), $11M TCCA contract (Apr. 18, 2023); Cordes gave $8,400 to Donalds' campaigns in 2020 (wife same), $2,000 in 2021, [Florida Bulldog, Jan. 4, 2026](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2026/01/charter-business-owned-rep-donalds-wife-cut-side-deals-construction-firm-suit-says/)

**Breakups, audits, litigation**
- TCCA terminated its Foundation contract Dec. 31, 2023; both Jacksonville academies ended ties in 2023, citing auditor "deficiencies" in debts, textbooks, revenues, vendor invoices, county funds, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- TCCA v. Optima Foundation (filed late 2024): alleges Optima subcontracted all TCCA employees to OptimaEd despite board veto, steered no-bid contracts to Summit, paid a $127,487.98 Summit invoice without board authorization, and failed to disclose Summit-related entities invested in Optima-related entities. Martin County judge dismissed May 2025 on venue grounds (contract requires Collier County); TCCA appealed, [Florida Bulldog, Jan. 4, 2026](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2026/01/charter-business-owned-rep-donalds-wife-cut-side-deals-construction-firm-suit-says/)
- Jan. 2023 TCCA independent audit found "outstanding" financial health but questioned class sizes as possibly violating state law, [CBS via Ravitch, Dec. 4, 2025](https://dianeravitch.net/2025/12/04/florida-wife-of-gop-gubernatorial-candidate-builds-a-for-profit-charter-school-empire)
- July 2026: Kelly Mason (Lichter), Collier School Board member, sued in Collier Circuit Court alleging Byron threatened to "crush" her in a 2022 grocery store confrontation over prior Mason Classical litigation; campaign called it "baseless, politically motivated", [Florida Phoenix, July 8, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/07/08/lawsuit-alleges-byron-donalds-threatened-collier-county-school-board-member-in-2022/)

**ESA / voucher context and Erika's statements**
- Education Freedom Foundation offers a free Start-A-School Toolkit plus fee-based consulting, mentorship and membership, works primarily in ESA states; Erika says ESAs are about $9,000 per student and over 500,000 Florida parents use scholarships, [Philanthropy Roundtable, Apr. 29, 2025](https://www.philanthropyroundtable.org/the-educational-freedom-foundation-shaping-the-future-of-education-with-erika-donalds/)
- Florida taxpayer-funded school choice is a $4.5B per year enterprise (2026 FEFP); over 500,000 voucher students at about $8,000 each; FEA sued May 2026, [Florida Trib, July 30, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/07/30/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida/)
- Erika on conflicts: "I believe that people should be compensated for their services, and I don't think I should be treated any differently"; "Profit is not going to be a bad word when it comes to the education industry" (March 2026 Doral event), [Miami Herald via KDH News, 2026](https://kdhnews.com/news/politics/profit-is-not-going-to-be-a-bad-word-florida-gubernatorial-candidate-byron-donalds-wants/article_aa9e4d6f-9338-5211-b6af-4e73acdb8b25.html); [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-billionaire-trying-to-reinvent-florida-schools-has-a-7-5-million-bet-on-byron-donalds-40565603/)
- Erika: "I have nothing to hide. I am the most open book ever"; says she does not do business with the state of Florida and there is "no conflict", [AOL repost](https://www.aol.com/articles/byron-donalds-runs-governor-wife-150251717.html); [Florida Trib, July 30, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/07/30/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida/)
- Dec. 2023 Erika tweeted endorsing Freedom Institute of Collier County as an ESA option without disclosing her companies share its landlord's building, [Florida Bulldog, June 29, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)

##### Inferences
- Summing documented flows: roughly $15.1M in Foundation management fees (three schools), $9.7M to Educator Solutions (schools plus Foundation, 2020 to 2022), $5.84M to the two for-profits in 2023, and $3.2M from the virtual academy to OptimaEd in 2023 to 2024. These overlap in part and are not additive, but they are consistent with CBS's $35M figure.
- "Optima Domi" and "Classical Academies of Florida network" did not surface in any source; the virtual school is referred to as Optima Classical Academy or Optima Academy Online.

##### Gaps
- No ethics complaint or formal investigation specifically about Erika's contracts was found; criticism comes from reporters (Florida Bulldog, CBS, Miami Herald) and rival candidates.
- No source quantified ESA/voucher dollars flowing to OptimaEd or Optima Academy Online, beyond the virtual charter's state funding ($3.59M over 2023 to 2024).
- Optima Domi, Hillsdale Barney Charter School Initiative formal status, and FDOE school records were not directly retrieved.
- Erika's AFPI compensation is unknown; Byron's disclosures list X Corp. 1099 income for her (2025) with no amount.

#### Key Question 4: Byron's own ties to the charter and education industry

##### Takeaway
Byron's documented ties run through his wife's companies and through major school-choice donors (Jeff Yass $7.5M, Ken Griffin $10M) to his governor committee; no direct contributions from Charter Schools USA, Academica, or Stride were found in this pass.

##### Cited Findings
- Byron was an early champion of the 2017 Schools of Hope law and co-sponsored the original charter co-location law; 2018 to 2019 chaired the Florida House PreK-12 Quality Subcommittee, [AOL repost of Miami Herald](https://www.aol.com/articles/byron-donalds-runs-governor-wife-150251717.html); [Lakeland Gazette](https://lakelandgazette.info/news/2026/08/05/expose-how-did-byron-donalds-get-so-rich-in-congress/)
- Jeff Yass (Susquehanna) gave $7.5M to Friends of Byron Donalds; Erika met the Yasses after OptimaEd was a 2023 Yass Prize semifinalist; Yass spokesperson: "Byron Donalds is a school choice warrior", [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-billionaire-trying-to-reinvent-florida-schools-has-a-7-5-million-bet-on-byron-donalds-40565603/)
- Ken Griffin (Citadel) gave $10M to the PAC supporting Donalds; Yass' latest $2.5M check came three days after the March 2026 Doral education event; Donalds has raised over $100M; Byron said donors "do not set my agenda", [Florida Trib, July 30, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/07/30/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida/)
- Summit Construction co-founder Charles Cordes gave $8,400 (2020), wife $8,400 (2020), $2,000 (2021) to Donalds' congressional campaigns, [Florida Bulldog, Jan. 4, 2026](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2026/01/charter-business-owned-rep-donalds-wife-cut-side-deals-construction-firm-suit-says/)
- Erika served on a Mason Classical Academy board; Byron applied for a Mason Classical board position (per the 2020 Lichter complaint), [Florida Political Review](https://www.floridapoliticalreview.com/ethics-complaint-against-byron-donalds-dismissed)

##### Inferences
- The Florida Bulldog piece states it found no reporting tying specific subcommittee votes to Optima's benefit, and the Lakeland Gazette opinion piece concedes the same.

##### Gaps
- No search of FEC or Florida Division of Elections records for Charter Schools USA, Jonathan Hage, Academica, or Stride/K12 contributions was completed; this belongs with the donor-network researcher.

#### Key Question 5: Book deals, speaking, media income, LLCs, real estate, sponsored travel

##### Takeaway
Donalds' House filings show no book agreement, no honoraria, and no outside positions since taking office; his sponsored travel is limited to conservative movement events (Conservative Partnership Institute, Heritage, Club for Growth), with staff trips sponsored by agricultural and biofuel groups; the only real estate indicator is a $500,001 to $1M mortgage from July 2024.

##### Cited Findings
- 2023 amended and 2025 annual reports list no agreements (Schedule F), no gifts, no positions, and no Schedule I compensation; 2025 lists no travel, [House Clerk 2023 amended](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2023/10071798.pdf); [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)
- Wikipedia lists no book authored by Donalds, [Wikipedia, Byron Donalds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byron_Donalds)
- Sponsored travel 2021 to 2023 (12 trips, 39 days): Byron personally: Conservative Partnership Institute, Miami, Feb. 12 to 13, 2021; Heritage Foundation, Sea Island GA, Jan. 7 to 9, 2022; CPI, Amelia Island FL, Feb. 10 to 12, 2022; Club for Growth, West Palm Beach, Mar. 3 to 4, 2023. Staff: South Florida Agricultural Foundation (Apr. 2021, Apr. 2022), Congressional Institute (June 2021, May 2023), Louisiana Sugar Cane Foundation (Nov. 2021), Iowa Renewable Fuels Association / Biodiesel Coalition of Missouri / Clean Fuels Alliance (Aug. 2022), CPI Cambridge MD (Oct. 2022), Stanford University (Aug. 2023). Costs not shown, [Howard Center Congressional Travel Explorer](https://cnsmaryland.org/interactives/fall-2024/congressional_travel_explorer/member/D000032.html)
- No AIPAC/AIEF Israel trip for Donalds appears in the Howard Center / Politico database article, [CNS Maryland, Nov. 1, 2024](https://cnsmaryland.org/2024/11/01/members-of-congress-have-taken-hundreds-of-aipac-funded-trips-to-israel-in-the-past-decade)
- Cross Country Mortgage on personal residence, incurred July 2024, $500,001 to $1,000,000, [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)
- Spouse 1099 income from X Corp. (2025, amount N/A), [House Clerk 2025 annual](https://disclosures-clerk.house.gov/public_disc/financial-pdfs/2025/10075306.pdf)

##### Inferences
- The July 2024 mortgage suggests a home purchase or refinance in 2024; no property records were retrieved.

##### Gaps
- Sponsored travel for 2024 through 2026 was not found (the Howard Center dataset ends 2023; LegiStorm travel page requires login; the 2025 annual shows none).
- No book deal, paid speaking, or media contract was found in any source; absence in Schedule F is the only evidence.

#### Key Question 6: Ethics complaints, OCE referrals, and investigative reporting

##### Takeaway
The live items are the Campaign Legal Center's Sept. 5, 2024 OCE complaint on 108 unreported trades (no public resolution found), a dismissed 2020 Florida Ethics Commission complaint, and a series of Florida Bulldog, CBS News, NOTUS, and Miami Herald investigations into the Optima money flows and the bitcoin purchase, which GOP rivals Renner, Collins and Fishback turned into primary attacks.

##### Cited Findings
- CLC OCE complaint Sept. 5, 2024, [Campaign Legal Center](https://campaignlegal.org/press-releases/rep-byron-donalds-florida-fails-disclose-16-million-stock-trades-campaign-legal)
- Florida Commission on Ethics dismissed Lichter complaint, reported Oct. 6, 2020, [Florida Political Review](https://www.floridapoliticalreview.com/ethics-complaint-against-byron-donalds-dismissed)
- Feb. 4, 2026: Paul Renner said Donalds had a "tenfold increase in his net worth" and proposed a "Florida First" package requiring 3-day stock trade disclosure for officials and spouses and banning first-time government contracts to officials and spouses; campaign strategist Ryan Smith dismissed Renner as an "anti-Trump RINO", [Florida Phoenix, Feb. 4, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/02/04/paul-renner-says-byron-donalds-has-enriched-himself-in-office/)
- Apr. 20, 2026: Jay Collins released a 13-page "liabilities" document citing the CLC complaint, the CBS $35M figure, and the unopened Fort Myers school; Donalds had raised $67M by then, [Florida Phoenix, Apr. 20, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/04/20/jay-collins-says-byron-donalds-is-not-a-risk-that-we-can-afford-to-take/)
- James Fishback held a press event at the unopened Optima Classical Academy in Fort Myers; Donalds campaign said the project used only private money and no tax dollars to date and "remains in full compliance" with its Lee County charter, [Fox 4](https://www.fox4now.com/news/local-news/abandoned-fort-myers-charter-school-becomes-flashpoint-in-florida-governor-race)
- Donalds supports Rep. Anna Paulina Luna's bill to bar members and some family from trading individual stocks, [Florida Phoenix, Feb. 4, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/02/04/paul-renner-says-byron-donalds-has-enriched-himself-in-office/)
- Aug. 18, 2026: Donalds won the GOP primary with about 47.8%; faces Democrat David Jolly Nov. 3, 2026, [Wikipedia, Byron Donalds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byron_Donalds)
- Florida Bulldog also published a piece arguing Donalds' simultaneous governor and House campaigns appear to violate state law (date not shown), [Florida Bulldog tag page](https://www.floridabulldog.org/tag/byron-donalds/)

##### Inferences
- The pattern across filings (missing 2021 to 2022 spouse salary, missing OMS stake until 2025, late bitcoin attribution to spouse) is one of under-disclosure corrected after press reports rather than adjudicated violations.

##### Gaps
- Florida Politics article "Byron Donalds says his investments are above board" could not be fetched; its date and quotes are unknown.
- No OCE board action, House Ethics referral, or fine was found for the 2024 complaint.
- Washington Examiner Feb. 22, 2026 crypto divestment story not located directly.

### Note: policy_and_interest_alignment.md

_Folder: Byron Donalds money and donor network_

### Byron Donalds: policy record, endorsements, governor agenda, and the interests that fund him (through Oct 22, 2026)

Scope note: This file maps "who stands to benefit" from a Donalds governorship by pairing his documented record and stated agenda with the specific donors, PACs and industry groups that have a stake. Dollar figures come from state (Florida Division of Elections) and federal (FEC) records as reported by the cited outlets. Unless noted, amounts to "Donalds" mean the state political committee Friends of Byron Donalds PAC. Headline totals: PAC had received $119.9M and spent $82.5M through Sept 25, 2026 per Florida Bulldog; campaign plus committee combined raised $134.4M through Sept 25 per CBS Miami; "nearly $149 million" raised and more than $110M spent per OpenSecrets via Florida Phoenix on Oct 20, 2026. Jolly: $16.7M (Sept 25) to under $22M (Oct 20). Several Florida Politics, Tampa Bay Times and CNN pages could not be fetched (site errors); those are flagged in Gaps.

#### Key Question 1: Key House votes and bills, and which funders cared

##### Takeaway
Donalds' House record is consistently pro-crypto, anti-CBDC, anti-Ukraine aid, pro-Israel aid, anti-debt-ceiling deal, pro-TikTok divestiture (despite his largest donor's ByteDance stake), and pro-One Big Beautiful Bill. The clearest documented overlap between a vote or official act and a financial interest is the Dec 18, 2025 IRS crypto letter paired with a same-day $50K to $100K bitcoin purchase by his wife (NOTUS), plus the Campaign Legal Center's 2024 STOCK Act complaint.

##### Cited Findings

Crypto and financial regulation
- Roll Call 226, May 22, 2024: Donalds voted Aye on passage of H.R. 4763, the Financial Innovation and Technology for the 21st Century Act (FIT21), which passed 279 to 136., [House Clerk](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2024226)
- Same day he voted Aye on the rule (H.Res. 1243, passed 204 to 203) providing for consideration of FIT21, H.R. 5403 (CBDC Anti-Surveillance State Act) and H.R. 192; voted No on the Casar amendment to FIT21; Aye on the Norman amendment., [House Clerk RC 222](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2024222); [RC 223](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2024223); [RC 224](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2024224)
- Roll Call 227, May 23, 2024: Donalds voted Aye on an amendment to H.R. 5403 (the CBDC bill), agreed to 204 to 176., [House Clerk](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2024227)
- July 17, 2025 (per his July 18, 2025 release): voted for H.R. 3633 CLARITY Act (passed 294 to 134), S. 1582 GENIUS Act (passed House 308 to 122, signed July 18, 2025), and H.R. 1919 Anti-CBDC Surveillance State Act (passed 219 to 210). Quote: "Central Bank Digital Currency would give unelected bureaucrats in our federal government absolute control over your money.", [Donalds House release](https://donalds.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=1923)
- In May 2025 he introduced a bill to codify Trump's Strategic Bitcoin Reserve and U.S. Digital Asset Stockpile (created by March 2025 executive order); it has not received a vote. He sits on the House Financial Services Committee., [NOTUS via Washington Sun, Jan 9, 2026](https://www.washingtonsun.com/congress/byron-donalds-crypto-rules-bitcoin?redirected=notus_org)
- Dec 18, 2025: Donalds and 18 other members sent a letter to IRS acting commissioner Scott Bessent asking the IRS to drop its rule treating crypto staking rewards as taxable income when received ("New property is never taxable income in the hands of its first owner"). The same day a congressional financial document shows a $50,001 to $100,000 bitcoin purchase. His office said wife Erika Donalds made the purchase and that he would amend his disclosure; no amendment was public as of the article. Spokesman Daniel First: "Importantly, the letter referenced had no bearing whatsoever on the spot price of Bitcoin." Former GOP Rep. Reid Ribble: "The timing of it really, really looks bad." Donalds had not previously reported owning bitcoin., [NOTUS via Washington Sun, Jan 9, 2026](https://www.washingtonsun.com/congress/byron-donalds-crypto-rules-bitcoin?redirected=notus_org)
- Stand With Crypto rates Donalds "very pro-cryptocurrencies.", [Stand With Crypto](https://www.standwithcrypto.org/politicians/person/byron---donalds)
- CFPB: On May 7, 2025 the House Financial Services Committee (on which Donalds sits) approved, 30 to 22 on party lines, a reconciliation provision cutting by almost 60 percent the funding the CFPB can draw from the Federal Reserve. The source does not list Donalds' individual committee vote., [AFSA](https://afsaonline.org/2025/05/07/house-gives-cfpb-a-haircut/)
- Roll Call 190, July 3, 2025: Donalds voted Aye on concurring in the Senate amendment to H.R. 1, the One Big Beautiful Bill Act (passed 218 to 214)., [House Clerk](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2025190). His Oct 2026 "Never" ad cites this vote as cutting taxes on overtime, tips and Social Security., [CBS Miami, Oct 6, 2026](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/byron-donalds-new-ads-florida-governor-race-david-jolly/)
- Federal-side crypto money: NOTUS reported Fairshake spent $172M in the 2024 cycle (Coinbase about $70M of it) and ran a $2M buy against Rep. Jamaal Bowman three weeks after the FIT21 vote; the article quotes Donalds as a pro-crypto member of the digital assets subcommittee but reports no Fairshake spending on him., [NOTUS via Washington Sun, Nov 26, 2024](https://www.washingtonsun.com/congress/crypto-congress-election?redirected=notus_org)
- Follow the Crypto's 2026 Florida page lists no crypto PAC spending in the governor's race; it lists only $25,500 to Donalds' House account from Andreessen Horowitz and Google executives., [Follow the Crypto / Citation Needed](https://influence.citationneeded.news/2026/states/florida)
- State-level crypto-adjacent donors to his governor PAC: Robinhood $200,000, Kalshi $75,000 (Gambling Insider; Miami New Times reported $25,000 from Kalshi), Jeff Yass (Susquehanna, described as a Kalshi investor and Robinhood co-owner of Rothera) $7.5M direct plus $3.5M to Club for Growth Action Florida., [Gambling Insider, Aug 12, 2026](https://www.gamblinginsider.com/news/185823/gambling-prediction-markets-florida-elections-byron-donalds); [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)

TikTok, Ukraine, Israel, debt ceiling
- Roll Call 86, March 13, 2024: Donalds voted Yea on H.R. 7521, Protecting Americans from Foreign Adversary Controlled Applications Act (the TikTok divestiture bill), passed 352 to 65., [House Clerk](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/202486)
- Roll Call 145, April 20, 2024: Yea on H.R. 8038, 21st Century Peace through Strength Act (the package carrying the TikTok divest-or-ban provision), passed 360 to 58., [House Clerk](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2024145)
- Roll Call 151, April 20, 2024: Nay on H.R. 8035, Ukraine Security Supplemental Appropriations Act., [House Clerk](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2024151)
- Roll Call 152, April 20, 2024: Yea on H.R. 8034, Israel Security Supplemental Appropriations Act., [House Clerk](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2024152)
- Roll Call 243, May 31, 2023: No on H.R. 3746, Fiscal Responsibility Act (the debt ceiling deal), which passed 314 to 117., [House Clerk](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2023243)
- Yass context: Jeff Yass holds a ByteDance stake worth billions and lobbied Republicans in 2024 against TikTok divestiture or ban legislation; he gave $16M to MAGA Inc. in H1 2025 and is a major Club for Growth supporter., [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/). Donalds nonetheless voted for both TikTok bills (above). Yass's first $5M to the Donalds PAC was reported in filings through March 31, 2025, after those votes., [News4Jax, Apr 14, 2025](https://gmg-wjxt-prod.cdn.arcpublishing.com/news/local/2025/04/14/byron-donalds-secures-6-million-from-influential-gop-donors-in-early-fundraising-for-florida-governors-race)

School choice in Congress
- Donalds co-sponsored a law allowing charter schools to co-locate inside traditional public schools ("schools of hope"); Ken Griffin's firm helped push an expansion of that program., [Florida Trib via FlaglerLive, Aug 5, 2026](https://flaglerlive.com/gospel-of-school-choice/)
- AFP Action credits him with legislation that created Florida's Hope Scholarship (from his state House tenure)., [AFP Action, Mar 4, 2026](https://afpaction.com/afp-action-endorses-byron-donalds-for-governor/)

Immigration bills (ties to detention contractors)
- Co-sponsored H.R. 2, Secure the Border Act of 2023; primary sponsor of H.R. 8430, Reshape Alternatives to Detention Act of 2024. GEO Group spent $340,000 (July to Sept 2024) and $350,000 (Oct to Dec 2024) lobbying for Donalds' two bills and $690,000 lobbying for H.R. 2 earlier in 2024; GEO spent nearly $2M lobbying DHS and federal lawmakers including Donalds in 2024., [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)

Tobacco
- Donalds introduced H.J.Res. 99 on Nov 9, 2023 to exempt premium cigars from FDA tobacco rules; Swisher PAC gave $5,000 in spring 2023 and Premium Cigar Association PAC $5,000 across 2023. (Partisan source, figures from FEC.), [Paradise Progressive](https://www.theparadiseprogressive.com/blog-the-paradise-progressive/the-donalds-dossier-insurance-water-and-the-power-of-pacs)

Federal PAC profile (2024 cycle, partisan compilation of FEC data)
- 2023: 24 financial services PACs gave $70,000; 18 banking PACs $67,000; 16 insurance PACs $94,000; seven energy PACs $25,500 in the 2024 cycle (Exxon, Valero, Marathon, NextEra, Duke); seven sugar PACs $11,000; Koch Industries PAC $2,000 (2023) and $4,000 (2024). Total PAC receipts $371,134 through Q1 2024., [Paradise Progressive](https://www.theparadiseprogressive.com/blog-the-paradise-progressive/the-donalds-dossier-insurance-water-and-the-power-of-pacs)
- 2026 cycle House account (FEC through Sept 30, 2026): Finance/Banking $112K from 22 orgs; Oil and Gas $51.5K; Real Estate $20K; Agriculture $18.5K; Tech $12K. Named PACs: American Council of Engineering Companies $15,000, State Farm Federal PAC $14,000, NextEra Energy PAC $12,000., [FundVoter](https://fundvoter.org/politicians/byron-donalds)

##### Inferences
- The FIT21, GENIUS, CLARITY and anti-CBDC votes are the baseline for the industry's affection, but the state-level money (Yass, Robinhood, Kalshi) is more plausibly about Florida prediction-market policy than federal crypto bills (see KQ5 gambling section).
- His TikTok votes are a counter-example to a simple "votes follow Yass" story; Yass's money arrived afterward.

##### Gaps
- No source found giving Donalds' individual committee vote on the May 2025 CFPB funding cut, or roll calls on bank capital (Basel III endgame) or SEC oversight bills.
- OpenSecrets was blocked; career industry totals for the House account could not be pulled directly.
- Could not fetch the Washington Post April 20, 2024 vote tracker; used House Clerk roll calls instead.

#### Key Question 2: Who has endorsed him for governor and what those groups want

##### Takeaway
Donalds has assembled nearly the entire Florida Republican institutional coalition: Trump (Feb 20, 2025), Club for Growth (Mar 11, 2025), Rick Scott (Aug 2, 2025), Elon Musk (Nov 13, 2025), Turning Point Action (Jan 13, 2026), AIF (Jan 27, 2026), AFP Action (Mar 4, 2026), ABC of Florida (Apr 2026), Florida Chamber (Jun 23, 2026), Police Chiefs (Jul 2026), SBA Pro-Life America (Jul 20, 2026), Farm Bureau FarmPAC (c. June 2026), Florida Realtors PAC (Aug 21, 2026), FOP and PBA (Sept 2026), DeSantis (Sept 1, 2026), Everglades Trust (Oct 6, 2026) and 40 rabbis (Oct 16, 2026). The business groups' stated asks are tort reform preservation, regulatory relief, school choice, property rights and a business-friendly posture on data centers and growth.

##### Cited Findings
- Trump: Truth Social post Thursday Feb 20, 2025: "Donalds would be a truly great and powerful governor for Florida... should he decide to run, will have my complete and total endorsement. Run, Byron, Run!", [WUSF, Feb 21, 2025](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2025-02-21/2026-florida-governor-race-trump-endorses-byron-donalds)
- Club for Growth PAC: endorsed Mar 11, 2025; cites Donalds' 100% lifetime CfG Foundation rating; PAC spent $2.5M in his 2020 nine-way primary which he won by 777 votes; priorities: pro-growth economics, "education freedom," limited government, tax cuts. First 2025-cycle CfG move aligned with Trump after McIntosh and Trump reconciled., [Club for Growth](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/cfg-pac-endorses-rep-byron-donalds-in-fl-gov-race/). CfG money to the Donalds PAC: $2.5M in Q1 2026 ([Highlands News-Sun](https://www.midfloridanewspapers.com/highlands_news-sun/news/donalds-dominates-fundraising-in-governor-race/article_70925d94-73c0-4f61-9737-e3805c0a1d70.html)); $975,000 from the Florida branch in Q2 2025 ([Yahoo/News Service of Florida](https://www.yahoo.com/news/byron-donalds-dwarfs-david-jolly-163544449.html)); Club for Growth Action Florida gave the PAC $3.75M total, funded in part by $3.5M from Yass ([WUSF, Sept 9, 2026](https://www.wgcu.org/government-politics/2026-09-09/what-to-know-about-pacs-your-florida-breaks-it-down)); Miami New Times put the CfG PAC total at $3.65M.
- Sen. Rick Scott: Aug 2, 2025: "I am proud to stand with President Trump and endorse Byron Donalds.", [Fox 35](https://www.fox35orlando.com/news/sen-rick-scott-former-florida-governor-endorses-trump-backed-byron-donalds)
- Elon Musk: Nov 13, 2025 on X: "Congratulations, Byron! You certainly have my full support.", [Florida's Voice](https://flvoicenews.com/elon-musk-endorses-byron-donalds/)
- Turning Point Action: Jan 13, 2026; Donalds cited Charlie Kirk's earlier endorsement; the group emphasizes school choice, parental rights, economic conservatism, youth turnout., [Florida's Voice](https://flvoicenews.com/turning-point-action-endorses-byron-donalds-in-florida-governors-race/)
- Associated Industries of Florida: Jan 27, 2026; CEO Brewster Bevis cites "reducing regulatory burdens" and a "pro-jobs environment.", [AIF release](https://www.aif.com/information/2026/pr260127.html). AIF's June 11, 2026 poll release calls Donalds the "AIF-endorsed candidate" and touts his "$81-million-dollar war chest.", [AIF](https://aif.com/information/2026/pr260611.html). AIF PAC gave the Donalds PAC $525,000 and "The Florida PC" committee more than $237,000 as of March 2026., [VoteWater, Mar 26, 2026](https://votewater.org/dirty-money-talks-what-does-it-say-about-byron-donalds/). Seeking Rents: AIF and Chamber committees that took more than $6M from data center interests gave Donalds more than $3.5M., [Seeking Rents, Oct 15, 2026](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/data-center-interests-have-put-30). AIF's committees were funded 2019 to 2022 by Florida Crystals $6.7M, HCA $4.9M, U.S. Sugar $4.5M, FPL $2.3M, Disney $2M, Florida Blue $1.3M., [Seeking Rents, Apr 3, 2022](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/the-voice-of-florida-business-is?open=false)
