- Miami New Times headline: "Jeff Yass, whose fortune is tied to TikTok's parent company, is Byron Donalds' largest donor.", [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- Miami New Times: "The billionaire trying to reinvent Florida schools has a $7.5 million bet on Byron Donalds," with Susquehanna spokesperson quote and Yass's praise of Donalds., [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-billionaire-trying-to-reinvent-florida-schools-has-a-7-5-million-bet-on-byron-donalds-40565603/)
- WUSF: Yass ~$11M via direct and Club for Growth Action Florida routes., [WUSF, Sept 9, 2026](https://wusf.org/text/politics-issues/2026-09-09/what-to-know-about-pacs-your-florida-breaks-it-down)
- Tampa Bay Times: 17 of 18 billionaires in the race gave to Donalds; Yass ~$11M., [Tampa Bay Times via KDH News, Oct 2, 2026](https://kdhnews.com/news/politics/18-billionaires-have-funded-the-florida-governor-race-17-gave-to-donalds/article_f4a68240-1521-537e-b8cb-9006156ebc14.html)
- Club for Growth PAC endorsement of Donalds for governor, March 11, 2025., [Fox News](https://www.foxnews.com/politics/first-fox-top-conservative-group-aligns-trump-makes-major-endorsement-high-profile-race.print); [Club for Growth release](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/cfg-pac-endorses-rep-byron-donalds-in-fl-gov-race/)
- The Yass-Trump meeting of March 1, 2024 was at a Club for Growth retreat in Palm Beach, Florida., [ABC News](https://abcnews.com/Politics/trumps-tiktok-ban-reversal-after-meeting-megadonor-stake/story?id=108013785)

##### Inferences
- Club for Growth Action Florida appears to be a state-registered super PAC conduit for Yass money into the governor race alongside his direct gifts; no fetched source describes its full donor list beyond Yass's $3.5M.

##### Gaps
- No reporting found of Yass attending a Donalds event or issuing a formal public endorsement beyond the praise quoted by Miami New Times.
- Club for Growth Action Florida's registration date, total receipts and other donors not found.

_Folder: Donalds second hop who gains_

### Second hop: billionaire, AI, crypto and ideological money behind Byron Donalds' 2026 Florida governor campaign

Research notes compiled Oct 22, 2026. Round one facts (Griffin $10M, Yass $7.5M direct plus $3.5M via a Club for Growth Florida vehicle, Uihlein $2M, Peterffy $1.2M, DeVos ~$1M, Fields $500K, Schmidt $500K, Winklevoss $1M, Club for Growth PAC $3.65M, Leading the Future $3M to American Mission Florida, Kohelet/Dantchik chain, Donalds TikTok vote) are treated as established and only re-cited where a source here independently confirms or refines them.

Research constraints encountered: the search budget was exhausted mid-task and several key paywalled or robots-blocked pages (Tampa Bay Times full text, Miami Herald, The Real Deal, Haaretz, NBC, floridapolitics.com, Transparency USA, ProPublica itemizer, OpenSecrets) could not be fetched. Gaps are flagged per section.

#### Key Question 1: Ken Griffin's Florida agenda and what he wants from a Florida governor

##### Takeaway
Griffin is Florida's largest individual political donor and, since moving Citadel to Miami in 2022, has paired a multibillion dollar Miami real estate and philanthropy build-out (Brickell HQ tower, $2B CMU Miami campus, $75M Baptist Health, $50M UM, $25M Nicklaus) with giving aimed at keeping Florida a low-tax, pro-business, "open for jobs" state and away from culture war fights. He has never stated an explicit ask of Donalds beyond "proud to support him as our next Governor," but his record shows what he values: pro-business policy, a Chinese-property-law carveout for Citadel, opposition to marijuana legalization, and a split from DeSantis over culture war politics.

##### Cited Findings

**The $10M gift and his statement**
- Griffin gave $10 million to Donalds' political committee (Friends of Byron Donalds), reported July 24, 2026; it was the campaign's largest reported donation to date. NBC first reported it. He told the Times/Herald he is "proud to support him as our next Governor" and praised Donalds' concern for Florida families. The campaign had roughly $92M in its war chest at the time; Griffin's and Yass's contributions together made up roughly a fifth of it., [Tampa Bay Times via AOL, July 24, 2026](https://www.aol.com/articles/billionaire-ken-griffin-pours-money-192303000.html)
- Griffin had dinner with DeSantis in Miami in April 2026 when Boris Johnson was in town; DeSantis had not endorsed a successor., [Tampa Bay Times via AOL](https://www.aol.com/articles/billionaire-ken-griffin-pours-money-192303000.html)
- Photo caption: Griffin has spent more than $700 million acquiring real estate in Miami's financial district., [Tampa Bay Times, July 24, 2026](https://www.tampabay.com/news/florida-politics/elections/2026/07/24/ken-griffin-byron-donalds-governor-donation-campaign-finance/)
- Legis1 confirms the $10M went to the Friends of Byron Donalds political committee and was "the largest reported contribution in the governor's race at the time"; Donalds' operation had raised more than $111M and held roughly $58M heading into the Aug. 18 primary., [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/florida-governor-race-donors-follow-the-money-by)

**DeSantis tried and failed to get Griffin to fund an anti-Donalds effort**
- At a June (2025) dinner in Miami DeSantis pitched Griffin on a financial strategy to challenge Donalds without a specific candidate; at a Fishers Island, NY fundraiser he urged backing Lt. Gov. Jay Collins. A person close to Griffin said "Ken has not made any decision regarding the Florida governor's race." Griffin was one of the largest donors to DeSantis' 2022 reelection but did not back his 2024 presidential bid, partly because he was put off by the culture war focus., [NBC News](https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/ron-desantis-lobbying-megadonor-ken-griffin-stays-sidelines-florida-go-rcna244801)
- Sept. 19, 2023: Griffin called DeSantis' Disney feud "pointless," said it "doesn't reflect well on the ethos of Florida" and conflicts with the state's pitch to business; said Florida should show it is "open to companies that want to create jobs"; praised the state's pandemic response., [Fortune, Sept. 19, 2023](https://www.fortune.com/2023/09/19/ken-griffin-ron-desantiss-pointless-fight-disney)

**2024-cycle Florida giving pattern (what he funds in Tallahassee)**
- Griffin spent more than $28 million on Florida state and local elections in the 2024 cycle, more than FPL, Duke Energy, U.S. Sugar, Florida Crystals and Publix combined. Breakdown: $12M to the DeSantis chief-of-staff-led group opposing the marijuana ballot initiative; $7M to the Republican Party of Florida in the closing days; $4M each to Republican leaders in the state House and Senate; $1M to Miami Mayor Francis Suarez; $500K to Miami-Dade Mayor Daniella Levine Cava; $70K to Palm Beach school board candidate Gloria Branch; $350K to losing candidates for Miami sheriff and Palm Beach state attorney. After Citadel moved to Miami it hired a Tallahassee lobbying firm run by one of DeSantis' top fundraisers, and per Bloomberg lobbyists persuaded Florida leaders to add a carveout helping Citadel to the law restricting Chinese citizens from buying property., [Seeking Rents (Jason Garcia)](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/a-billionaire-is-showering-cash-on)

**2026 cycle federal and Florida giving**
- $2.5 million to Stronger Safer Nation, a super PAC backing Sen. Ashley Moody, given in May 2026; the PAC registered March 2025, raised about $12M through June, and spent about $4.9M over two weeks (87% of race spending in the period), mostly opposing Democrat Angie Nixon. Griffin has given about $32.3M to federal races this cycle versus $74M in 2022 and $108.8M in 2024., [Hedgeweek citing Reuters/FEC, Oct. 2, 2026](https://hedgeweek.com/news/citadel-founder-backed-super-pac-spends-4-9m-in-florida-senate-race)
- Miami Herald (July 2026) framed the Moody $2.5M as part of a nationwide push to keep Senate control; Bloomberg reported $10M to the GOP's main Senate super PAC., [Florida Dems repost of Miami Herald/Bloomberg, July 21, 2026](https://floridadems.org/2026/07/icymi-miami-billionaire-ken-griffin-quietly-dropped-2-5m-into-floridas-senate-race-miami-herald/)
- Fortune (Oct. 21, 2026): Griffin's giving this cycle includes $10M to Senate Leadership Fund, $5M to Congressional Leadership Fund, and $10M supporting Donalds., [Fortune, Oct. 21, 2026](https://fortune.com/2026/10/21/ken-griffin-trump-tax-cuts-national-debt)

**Relationship with Trump and stated policy priorities (2025 to 2026)**
- Oct. 20 to 21, 2026 at the Future of Global Markets Conference: Griffin said Republicans erred by renewing the 2017 tax cuts ("We didn't have the fiscal space to renew the tax cuts"), called the tariff war "a fiasco," and said the country should invest in AI, data centers, nuclear energy, batteries and robotics and "grow the damn economy.", [Fortune, Oct. 21, 2026](https://fortune.com/2026/10/21/ken-griffin-trump-tax-cuts-national-debt)
- Sept. 25, 2025: Griffin criticized Trump's corporate equity deals, saying "all of us lose" when government picks winners., [CNBC (headline only; page not fetchable)](https://www.cnbc.com/2025/09/25/ken-griffin-trump-white-house-tariffs.html)
- 2022: Griffin said it was time to "move on" from Trump and backed DeSantis for 2024., [Fox News](https://www.foxnews.com/politics/republican-mega-donor-ken-griffin-says-time-move-from-trump-backs-desantis-2024)

**Miami real estate, Brickell tower, philanthropy**
- Sept. 30, 2026: $3 billion gift to Carnegie Mellon, the largest individual gift to a university: $1B to Pittsburgh ($500M to the renamed Kenneth C. Griffin School of Computer Science) and $2B for CMU Miami, a 35-acre Wynwood campus focused on human health, national security, energy and climate resilience, and advanced manufacturing; 3,500+ students, ~300 faculty; construction 2027, enrollment 2028 pending approvals. Griffin joins CMU's board. Citadel moved HQ from Chicago to Miami in 2022 and has unveiled plans to expand its future Brickell headquarters with a 300-unit apartment tower and parking garage. Quote: "What we're about to embark on in Miami will create one of the great stories in higher education of our lifetime.", [Fox Business, Sept. 30, 2026](https://www.foxbusiness.com/business-leaders/ken-griffin-doubles-down-florida-record-3b-carnegie-mellon-gift)
- Oct. 7, 2026: additional $25M to Baptist Health South Florida, bringing his total to $75M; institute renamed the Baptist Health Kenneth C. Griffin Neuroscience Institute; earlier $50M came in 2024; $25M to Nicklaus Children's Hospital in 2023., [Billionaires.Africa, Oct. 10, 2026](https://www.billionaires.africa/2026/10/10/american-billionaire-ken-griffin-raises-his-miami-hospital-gift-to-75-million/)
- Bloomberg (via Insurance Journal, Sept. 30, 2026): Griffin's Miami philanthropy includes $50M to Baptist Health Miami Neuroscience Institute, $10M to Perez Art Museum Miami, $50M to the University of Miami for a cancer research building, $25M to Nicklaus Children's, $5M for the Kenneth C. Griffin Urban Trail. He co-founded the Partnership for Miami with Orlando Bravo and Ana-Marie Codina and teamed with Stephen Ross on a $10M campaign promoting South Florida. Fortune exceeds $57B. Article notes DeSantis' education overhaul has alienated academics as context., [Insurance Journal / Bloomberg, Sept. 30, 2026](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/southeast/2026/09/30/887378.htm)
- Griffin unveiled plans for a 54-story Citadel HQ in Brickell., [Commercial Observer](https://commercialobserver.com/?p=486837); Miami-Dade cleared the path for the Brickell HQ., [BRG](https://www.brgintl.com/?p=66652); July 17, 2026: Griffin sought to buy a historic Miami house near the Citadel HQ., [The Real Deal](https://therealdeal.com/miami/2026/07/17/ken-griffin-wants-to-buy-historic-miami-house-near-citadel-hq/)
- 2024: Griffin gave $12M to fight the Florida marijuana initiative., [WLRN](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2024-08-05/billionaire-hedge-fund-manager-gives-12m-to-fight-pot-initiative)

##### Inferences
- Griffin's giving is best read as "keep Florida a stable, pro-business, low-drama place to anchor Citadel and a new tech/university cluster." His $4M each to legislative leadership in 2024 and the Citadel carveout in the Chinese property law show he works the Legislature directly; a Donalds governorship aligned with House leadership (which blocked DeSantis' AI bill) would be consistent with his interests. This is inference; no source states an explicit ask tied to the $10M.
- His simultaneous public criticism of Trump's tariffs and tax cuts and his funding of Trump-endorsed Donalds suggests he backs Donalds as the pro-business establishment Republican rather than as a Trump loyalist; the DeSantis-to-Donalds shift tracks his stated distaste for culture war governance.
- CMU Miami's focus on AI, national security and energy, plus his Oct. 2026 call to invest in "AI, data centers, nuclear," means he has a direct stake in Florida not enacting restrictive AI/data center rules.

##### Gaps
- No source found quoting Griffin on Florida property tax elimination, insurance reform, or the DeSantis AI Bill of Rights.
- Could not retrieve Griffin's itemized 2025 to 2026 Florida legislative or local giving (Florida Division of Elections data, Transparency USA page failed).
- Could not confirm whether the "Chinese property law carveout" was a Bloomberg finding or Seeking Rents' characterization beyond the article's attribution.
- CNBC Sept. 2025 article could not be fetched; only the headline is cited.

#### Key Question 2: Jeff Yass network beyond Donalds (Club for Growth's Florida slate, School Freedom Fund, Florida legislators, AI/TikTok stance, Trump relationship, Susquehanna in Florida)

##### Takeaway
Yass is the dominant funder of Club for Growth Action ($10M in Nov. 2025 alone; $15M in the 2026 cycle per Wikipedia; more than $50M lifetime per Sludge), which in Florida is targeting FL-22 (opposing Casey Askar), FL-14 (opposing Kathy Castor), and via Win It Back PAC FL-19 (opposing Chris Collins), while Club for Growth PAC endorses Donalds, AG James Uthmeier, Anna Paulina Luna and Mike Beltran. His 2025 to 2026 Trump relationship flipped from refusal to $16M to MAGA Inc., $5M to the White House ballroom, and a board seat for a Susquehanna affiliate in the new TikTok U.S. joint venture. Susquehanna's stated Florida interest beyond Donalds is school choice ("Donalds is a school choice warrior") and, via its Kalshi investment, prediction markets; no evidence found of Susquehanna business operations in Florida.

##### Cited Findings

**Club for Growth 2026 Florida slate and spending**
- Club for Growth PAC's 2026 Florida endorsements: Byron Donalds (Governor), James Uthmeier (Attorney General, unopposed in primary, faces José Javier Rodríguez), Anna Paulina Luna (FL-13), Mike Beltran (FL-14, newly drawn toss-up). No Florida Senate endorsement., [Club for Growth PAC endorsed candidates](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/elections/pac-endorsed-candidates/)
- Club for Growth PAC endorsed Donalds March 11, 2025; McIntosh: "Rep. Byron Donalds is a proven constitutional conservative" and Club PACs will "work closely with President Trump, his political team." The Club spent $2.5M helping Donalds win his 2020 congressional primary; he has a 100% lifetime Club rating., [Fox News, March 11, 2025](https://www.foxnews.com/politics/first-fox-top-conservative-group-aligns-trump-makes-major-endorsement-high-profile-race.print)
- Club for Growth's Donalds page notes state election law bars it from processing online donations for Donalds for Governor and directs supporters to his WinRed page., [Club for Growth](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/candidates/byron-donalds/)
- Club for Growth Action spending through Aug. 31, 2026 includes ~$704,806 opposing Casey Askar in the FL-22 GOP primary and ~$455,986 opposing Rep. Kathy Castor (FL-14, ~$241,556 TV plus digital). Receipts through Aug. 31: $50.39M ($23.44M from individuals, $25.81M from committees). Jeff Yass gave $10M in November 2025 plus other multimillion contributions; Restoration of America PAC (Uihlein) gave $2.5M in Oct. 2025 and $15M reported Aug. 27, 2026; Marsha for Senate gave $4.8M in May., [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/club-for-growth-action-2026-spent-957-million-1)
- FactCheck.org (Oct. 6, 2026): Yass is the source of much of Club for Growth Action's 2026 money; more than $50M raised as of Aug. 31; stated 2026 plan was $175M total ($75M Senate, $55M House, $20M governors, $20M issue advocacy); Win It Back PAC received more than $4.5M from Club for Growth Action; the only Florida race it cites is opposition to Casey Askar in FL-22., [FactCheck.org](https://www.factcheck.org/2026/10/club-for-growth-action-8/)
- July 27, 2026: Club for Growth's Win It Back PAC made a $1.1M buy in FL-19 (Donalds' open seat) attacking Chris Collins, a 2019 insider-trading convict pardoned by Trump in 2020, as soft on immigration ("Anybody but Chris"). Collins scored 49% on the Club scorecard. Ten Republicans ran including Madison Cawthorn, Jim Oberweis, Catalina Lauf., [Axios, July 27, 2026](https://www.axios.com/2026/07/27/republicans-florida-19-chris-collins-club-for-growth); [Club for Growth release](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/icymi-win-it-back-pac-hits-collins-with-1-1-million-buy-in-fl-19/)
- Florida state-level: a "Florida branch of Club for Growth" gave $975,000 to Donalds' political committee in Q2 2025., [Yahoo/Tampa Bay Times, 2025](https://www.yahoo.com/news/byron-donalds-dwarfs-david-jolly-163544449.html); Florida Bulldog (Aug. 24, 2025) lists "Club for Growth Action" at $975,000 among the nearly $10M from Yass ($5M), Uihlein ($2M), Peterffy ($1M), Winklevoss ($1M)., [Florida Bulldog, Aug. 24, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/)
- Gambling Insider (Aug. 12, 2026) describes Yass's Florida giving as "$7.5 million directly to Donalds, plus $3.5 million to a political committee that later supported him.", [Gambling Insider](https://www.gamblinginsider.com/news/185823/gambling-prediction-markets-florida-elections-byron-donalds)
- Miami New Times: Yass's $7.5M makes him the largest donor to Friends of Byron Donalds PAC (which had raised more than $71M), more than double the next largest, Club for Growth PAC at $3.65M. Yass did not respond., [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- A Susquehanna representative told the Miami New Times: "Donalds is a school choice warrior.", [Steve Farley Substack citing Miami New Times 2026](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/how-the-donalds-network-the-school)

**School choice vehicles**
- Yass gave $5M to School Freedom Fund in Nov. 2021; co-founded Students First PAC and gave it $41.7M 2010 to 2022., [Wikipedia: Jeff Yass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Yass)
- Per Farley (citing Hechinger Report 2025 and OpenSecrets 2024): Yass gave over $209M to school choice causes in a decade; vehicles include Club for Growth Action (over $39.5M, 2023 to 2024), School Freedom Fund (over $11M), AFC Victory Fund ($3.5M), Protect Freedom PAC ($8M in 2024). Uihlein gave $2M to School Freedom Fund in 2023 and $1M to AFC Victory Fund in 2024., [Farley Substack](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/how-the-donalds-network-the-school)
- Erika Donalds founded OptimaEd (2017), which manages classical charter schools in Florida; she is a Heritage visiting fellow, leads AFPI education policy, sits on advisory boards for Moms for Liberty and the Classical Learning Test; DeSantis appointed her to the FGCU board. CBS News (Dec. 2, 2025) reported on a Florida charter company "run by GOP rising star" leaving parents scrambling., [Wikipedia: Erika Donalds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erika_Donalds)
- Betsy DeVos and Donalds jointly urged Florida lawmakers to expand school choice., [Fox News](https://foxnews.com/media/betsy-devos-rep-byron-donalds-libs-of-tiktok-others-urge-fl-lawmakers-expand-school-choice.amp)

**Yass political totals 2024 to 2026**
- 2024 cycle: ~$100M to Republican groups; 2025: $16M to MAGA Inc. in H1 and $10M to Ramaswamy's super PAC; 2026: Yass and wife Janine over $89.3M including $20M to Ramaswamy's super PAC, $16M to MAGA Inc., $15M to Club for Growth Action. Club for Growth gifts: $3.8M (2018), $20.7M (2020). Yass and Trump met March 2024 at a Club for Growth event., [Wikipedia: Jeff Yass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Yass)
- Sludge (Aug. 1, 2025): $16M to MAGA Inc. ($1M on Jan. 17, 2025; $15M on March 6, 2025, his largest single contribution ever), his first-ever gifts to a Trump-affiliated group, made while TikTok's fate was negotiated in the White House; more than $50M lifetime to Club for Growth super PAC. SIG owns about 15% of ByteDance; Yass personally about 7%, worth up to $34B at a $400B internal valuation; SIG holds a ByteDance board seat; stake acquired 2012 at ~$5B valuation. Trump delayed the TikTok ban three times (Jan. 20, Apr. 4, June 19, 2025). Reuters reported Yass led discussions on an SIG bid for TikTok's U.S. operations., [Sludge, Aug. 1, 2025](https://readsludge.com/2025/08/01/tiktok-billionaire-donates-millions-to-trump-as-he-repeatedly-delays-ban/)

**TikTok deal outcome (Jan. 2026)**
- TikTok USDS joint venture announced Jan. 22 to 23, 2026, led by Silver Lake, Oracle and MGX; ByteDance retains 19.9%. Vastmere Strategic Investments LLC, an SIG affiliate, is an investor and gets a board seat held by SIG managing director Mark Dooley. Yass holds 51% of SIG, cofounded 1987; personal ByteDance stake ~7% (~$21B). Political spending: ~$100M in 2024, $34M on school choice, $16M to MAGA Inc. 2025, at least $2.5M to the White House ballroom., [Technical.ly, Jan. 23, 2026](https://technical.ly/entrepreneurship/susquehanna-international-group-jeff-yass-tiktok-board/)
- Keystone Newsroom (Jan. 27, 2026): Oracle, Silver Lake and MGX each 15%, ByteDance 19.9%, seven-member board with American majority; Yass retains his stake; a LittleSis analyst said his Trump investment "has paid off.", [Keystone Newsroom](https://keystonenewsroom.com/news/politics/yass-tiktok-deal-revealed/)
- Inquirer (Oct. 15, 2026): Yass committed $5M to the White House ballroom per a Washington Post donor list (an earlier Inquirer story, Nov. 3, 2025, had "at least $2.5M"); attended an Oct. 2025 White House dinner for $2.5M+ ballroom donors; Trump's 2025 domestic policy bill created a K-12 scholarship tax credit aligned with Yass's school choice agenda. Yass spokesperson declined comment., [Philadelphia Inquirer, Oct. 15, 2026](https://www.inquirer.com/politics/comcast-jeff-yass-trump-ballroom-donors-20261015.html); [Inquirer, Nov. 3, 2025](https://www.inquirer.com/politics/nation/jeffrey-yass-trump-ballroom-billionaire-donations-20251103.html)

**Kalshi / prediction markets (Yass link to Florida gaming money)**
- Gambling Insider identifies Yass as a Kalshi investor and groups his $7.5M + $3.5M with Kalshi ($268K total, $75K to Donalds) and Robinhood ($200K, all to Donalds) in Florida., [Gambling Insider, Aug. 12, 2026](https://www.gamblinginsider.com/news/185823/gambling-prediction-markets-florida-elections-byron-donalds)
- Tampa Bay Times (June 15, 2026) caption: Kalshi gave $25,000 to Donalds' political committee "last year"; headline notes Kalshi touts Donalds' lead in the governor race while donating to him; a money-in-politics researcher says prediction markets seek "friends in high places.", [Tampa Bay Times, June 15, 2026](https://www.tampabay.com/news/florida-politics/elections/2026/06/15/byron-donalds-kalshi-campaign-donations-sports-election/)

##### Inferences
- The "$3.5M via Club for Growth Action Florida" from round one is consistent with the $975K Q2 2025 figure plus later transfers; I could not independently verify the Florida committee's exact name or cumulative total (see Gaps).
- Yass's Florida interests, by his own representative's framing, are school choice first. Florida already has universal ESAs; the likely ask is protection and expansion of that regime plus a friendly environment for OptimaEd-type operators. This is inference.
- Yass's personal TikTok outcome was resolved by the Jan. 2026 federal deal; a Florida governor has little leverage on it. The Florida-relevant Yass business interest is prediction markets (Kalshi) and whether Florida (and the Seminole compact) challenges sports event contracts.

##### Gaps
- Could not retrieve the Florida Division of Elections record for the Club for Growth Florida committee (name, total raised, Yass share) or any Yass giving to individual Florida legislators.
- No source found on School Freedom Fund independent expenditures in Florida 2026 state legislative races.
- No source found on Susquehanna having offices, trading operations or portfolio companies in Florida.
- No source found on Yass stating a position on state AI regulation.
- NBC/Miami Herald reporting on Club for Growth's Florida legislative slate (if any) not located.

#### Key Question 3: Yass and Dantchik's Israel-related philanthropy beyond Kohelet (be precise about Yass vs Dantchik)

##### Takeaway
The documented Israel giving runs almost entirely through the Claws Foundation (EIN 20-1658710), whose funding is attributed by Shomrim to Dantchik alone but whose directors were Dantchik, Alan Dye and (2015 to 2022 per ProPublica) Yass. Claws' largest Israel grantee by far is the pluralist Shalom Hartman Institute ($25M+ 2010 to 2020; $3.3M in 2021; $31M per a spokesperson), followed by Jerusalem Online University ($7.9M, 2014 to 2019), Central Fund of Israel ($100K in 2014, then ~$12M via Silver Valley in 2021), American Friends of Hebrew University (~$500K, 2021), and the Taub Center. The only confirmed Yass-personal Israel-adjacent giving is $3M to Hadassah (2023) via the Susquehanna Foundation, and the QXZ Inc. pro-Israel/anti-Iran-deal grants ($1.5M Citizens for a Nuclear Free Iran, $250K Emergency Committee for Israel in 2015; $1.05M RJC 2018 to 2019), which Yass's spokesperson denies he directed. No source found linking either man to Tikvah, Shalem, Im Tirtzu or Regavim, and no source found on Susquehanna investing in Israeli defense tech.

##### Cited Findings

**Claws Foundation structure and finances**
- ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer (EIN 20-1658710, Reston, VA): directors Alan P. Dye (2012 to 2024), Arthur Dantchik (2012 to 2024), Jeff Yass (2015 to 2022, not listed 2023 to 2024). Charitable disbursements: 2020 $36.3M; 2021 $45.4M; 2022 $37.6M; 2023 $30.1M; 2024 $18.5M. Revenue 2020 $109.7M; 2023 $44.8M; 2024 $0.38M. Assets fell from $80.9M (2020) to $6.2M (2024)., [ProPublica Nonprofit Explorer](https://projects.propublica.org/nonprofits/organizations/201658710)
- Shomrim/TheMarker (Aug. 22, 2023, Uri Blau): Claws founded 2004; funding "attributed exclusively to Dantchik over the past decade"; managed by Sterling Foundation Management. Dantchik gave Claws about $220M 2013 to 2020 (about $110M in 2020 alone). In 2021 Claws transferred about $12M to Silver Valley Research Foundation (a months-old U.S. nonprofit describing itself as medical research, with Sterling employees on its board), most of which went to the Central Fund of Israel (director Jay Marcus, who refused to name donors). Kohelet received ~25M NIS (~$7.7M) from CFI in 2021 and ~29M NIS (~$8.5M) in 2022, with CFI listed as Kohelet's sole donor. Claws also gave ~$500K in 2021 to American Friends of the Hebrew University and has supported the Taub Center and Shalom Hartman Institute. "No direct Claws transfers to Kohelet have been identified." Yass is not mentioned., [Shomrim](https://www.shomrim.news/eng/danchik-kohelet)
- Inside Philanthropy: Claws is a low-profile vehicle administered by Sterling in Reston; Yass "is also a director"; $13.5M in grants in 2018. Dantchik grantees include Shalom Hartman Institute, Jerusalem Online University, Central Fund of Israel, PEF Israel Endowment Funds, American Friends of Old City Charities; libertarian grantees: Institute for Justice (board), Cato, CEI, FIRE, Ayn Rand Institute, Reason, Niskanen; also CHOP, Wistar, Franklin Institute, Kids Connect Charitable Fund., [Inside Philanthropy](https://www.insidephilanthropy.com/wall-street-donors/tag/Arthur+Dantchik)
- Wikipedia (Dantchik): Claws gave more than $25M to Shalom Hartman Institute 2010 to 2020; major donor to Institute for Justice, Cato, Students for Liberty. Dantchik owns ~19% of SIG (2022), oversees SIG private equity/VC/international, helped found SIG Asia Investments (2004), sits on ByteDance's board as SIG's representative., [Wikipedia: Arthur Dantchik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Dantchik)

**Dantchik's Kohelet withdrawal and the Yass denial**
- JPost/JTA (Aug. 5 to 6, 2023): Dantchik "stopped donating to think tanks in Israel, including the Kohelet Policy Forum"; he gave "millions," thought to be Kohelet's largest funder; Claws gave nearly $3.3M to Shalom Hartman in 2021. On Yass: anonymous sources told Calcalist Yass has not donated to Kohelet, matching NYT and WaPo reporting and contradicting the 2021 Haaretz investigation. Activists protested at Dantchik's home and office; a full-page Jewish Exponent ad told him to "get [his] hands off our democracy.", [Jerusalem Post, Aug. 5, 2023](https://www.jpost.com/israel-news/article-753745)
- Inquirer (Aug. 8, 2023): Dantchik, 65, cofounder of SIG, announced to Calcalist he would end support "for research institutes in Israel, including the Kohelet Forum"; ~30 Israelis protested for months outside his Gladwyne home (Haaretz). Yass mentioned only as business partner., [Philadelphia Inquirer](https://www.inquirer.com/business/susquehanna-international-group-sig-funding-controversy-20230808.html)
- Wikipedia (Kohelet): Haaretz March 2021 reported Yass and Dantchik as principal donors; "acquaintances of Yass disputed this, saying he has never been a donor to Kohelet." Dantchik's donations conjectured at 93% of Kohelet's budget. April 2024: Kohelet cut about half its staff., [Wikipedia: Kohelet Policy Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kohelet_Policy_Forum)
- Shomrim (July 21, 2025): Kohelet donations fell from 30M NIS (~$8.9M, 2022) to 24M NIS (~$7M, 2023) to 13.6M NIS (~$4M, 2024, down 43.2%); CFI still provided ~$3.5M of ~$4M 2024 income; Kohelet may have found a new chief benefactor (unconfirmed); it stopped funding other right-wing groups., [Shomrim, July 21, 2025](https://www.shomrim.news/eng/funding-crisis-forces-kohelet-to-cut-salaries-research-and-support-for-other-groups)
- Calcalist exclusive on Dantchik halting support., [Calcalist, Aug. 2023](https://www.calcalistech.com/ctechnews/article/hkora4ci3)

**Guardian / Responsible Statecraft "$16M" attribution and the Yass-vs-Dantchik problem**
- Daily Beast summarizing the Guardian/Responsible Statecraft investigation (April 2024): Yass "contributed about $16 million" to anti-Muslim and pro-Israel groups; Claws Foundation, "where Yass served as a director," routed $7.9M to Jerusalem Online University 2014 to 2019; $10K to Friends of the IDF (2011); $35K to Center for Security Policy; $250K to David Horowitz Freedom Center (2012 to 2013). Dantchik not mentioned., [Daily Beast](https://blogs.thedailybeast.com/gop-megadonor-jeff-yass-poured-dollar16m-into-anti-muslim-groups-report)
- Responsible Statecraft: Yass was one of three Claws directors with Dantchik and Alan Dye; Claws grants: Jerusalem Online University $7.9M (2014 to 2019); Imagination Productions (JOU parent) $3.48M from Kids Connect Charitable Fund (which Haaretz tied to both men's philanthropy, neither listed as director); Friends of IDF $10K (2011); Center for Security Policy $35K (2010 to 2011); David Horowitz Freedom Center $250K (2013 to 2014); Central Fund of Israel $100K (2014); Shalom Hartman Institute $31M per spokesperson. Separately, QXZ Inc. (linked to Yass via a Massachusetts ballot group) gave $1.5M to Citizens for a Nuclear Free Iran and $250K to Emergency Committee for Israel (2015) and $1.05M to the Republican Jewish Coalition (2018 to 2019). The piece "doesn't separate the two individually"; the spokesperson responded for "Yass and Dantchik" jointly, denied Yass directed QXZ's grants, and said Yass "never discussed foreign policy with Donald Trump" and generally opposes U.S. foreign involvement., [Responsible Statecraft](https://responsiblestatecraft.org/jeff-yass/)
- Haaretz (March 24, 2024) headline describes Yass as "invested in TikTok and Israel's judicial coup." Page could not be fetched., [Haaretz](https://www.haaretz.com/us-news/2024-03-24/ty-article/.premium/jeff-yass-billionaire-invested-in-tiktok-and-israels-judicial-coup-top-spender-in-2024/0000018e-70d5-d477-abbf-f7d531d20000)

**Susquehanna Foundation (corporate foundation; the clearer Yass vehicle)**
- InfluenceWatch: Susquehanna Foundation (EIN 23-2732477, formed 1994), Dantchik president, Yass vice president; Yass is a former Cato vice chair and former Claws board member. Grants include $3,000,000 to Hadassah, the Women's Zionist Organization of America (2023), the only clearly Jewish/Zionist recipient in the listed data; $3M/yr to Cato (2023, 2024); $500K/yr to Institute for Justice (2020 to 2024); $500K/yr to Institute for Free Speech (2022 to 2024); $63.4M to Fidelity Charitable and $12M to Vanguard Charitable (2024); large education-reform and CHOP grants. Assets $673.6M (2025). All-time grants $176.6M., [InfluenceWatch: Susquehanna Foundation](https://www.influencewatch.org/non-profit/susquehanna-foundation/)

**Tikvah / Shalem (no link found)**
- Tikvah Fund: Roger Hertog chairman 2011, Elliott Abrams chairman 2022, Eric Cohen CEO; gave $12.5M (2011) to establish Shalem College; received $10.5M from the NEH in Sept. 2025. Yass and Dantchik do not appear., [Wikipedia: Tikvah Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tikvah_Fund); [Times of Israel on Tikvah $16M to Shalem](https://www.timesofisrael.com/israels-first-liberal-arts-college-gets-16-million-boost-from-tikvah-fund/)

##### Inferences
- Attribution precision: by funding source, Claws is Dantchik's money (Shomrim: exclusively Dantchik for a decade). Yass's connection is governance only (a director seat 2015 to 2022). Any statement that "Yass funded Kohelet" rests on Haaretz 2021 and is contradicted by NYT, WaPo and Calcalist sourcing; "Yass sat on the board of the foundation that funded the conduit to Kohelet" is the defensible formulation. The Guardian/Daily Beast "$16M Yass" figure is largely Claws money and therefore mostly Dantchik's by the same logic; it is a weaker attribution.
- The ideological mix of Claws' Israel giving is not uniformly right-wing: Shalom Hartman ($25M to $31M) and Taub are pluralist/centrist, dwarfing the Horowitz Center, CSP and FIDF grants. The Kohelet chain is the outlier in scale ($16M+ over 2021 to 2022) and in routing (via Silver Valley and CFI).
- Yass's removal from the Claws board after 2022 (ProPublica) and Claws' collapse in assets and disbursements by 2024 are consistent with Dantchik's Aug. 2023 withdrawal from Israeli think tanks.

##### Gaps
- No 990 grant schedules were retrievable (ProPublica page showed summary financials only; grant recipient lists not extracted). Year-by-year Claws grants to Hartman, Taub, Hebrew U, CFI beyond those reported by Shomrim, Inside Philanthropy, JPost and Responsible Statecraft are missing.
- No source links Yass or Dantchik to Tikvah Fund, Shalem College, Im Tirtzu or Regavim. Absence of evidence, not evidence of absence; Kohelet itself reportedly funded other right-wing groups (Shomrim 2023), which could include such groups downstream, but no names were retrievable.
- No source found on Susquehanna (SIG) investments in Israeli tech or defense companies; the two Calcalist defense-tech-map articles did not mention SIG.
- Haaretz pages could not be fetched; the 2021 investigation's actual evidence on Yass could not be reviewed.

#### Key Question 4: Uihlein, Peterffy, DeVos, Clive Fields, Steve Wynn, Deborah Magowan

##### Takeaway
Uihlein ($2M, in two $1M installments, the second June 6, 2025) and Peterffy ($1M in Q2 2025, later reported as $1.2M) are confirmed; both are Trump-aligned, free-market donors, with Uihlein also the main funder of Restoration of America PAC which in turn gave $17.5M to Club for Growth Action, meaning Uihlein money reaches Donalds through two channels. Peterffy lives in Palm Beach near Mar-a-Lago and is described by one analyst as an "access buyer." Schmidt's $500K appears in Oct. 2026 filings. No retrievable source confirmed the DeVos, Fields, Wynn or Magowan contributions.

##### Cited Findings

**Uihlein**
- Richard Uihlein gave $1M to Friends of Byron Donalds PAC by March 31, 2025 (with Yass $5M)., [News4Jax, April 14, 2025](https://www.news4jax.com/news/local/2025/04/14/byron-donalds-secures-6-million-from-influential-gop-donors-in-early-fundraising-for-florida-governors-race/)
- Second $1M check to Friends of Byron Donalds June 6, 2025., [Florida Politics](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/745791-byron-donalds-adds-to-haul-for-governors-race-bringing-total-to-22m/)
- Florida Bulldog (Aug. 24, 2025): Uihlein $2M total., [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/)
- Uihlein ran Uline (CEO), ~7,000 employees (2020). As of Aug. 2026 the Uihleins had spent $69.5M supporting Republican candidates in 2026. Restoration PAC received $125M from Richard 2020 to Oct. 2024 and paid Brian Timpone's firm to produce the "Wisconsin Catholic Tribune." Agenda: anti-union, anti-tax, pro-deregulation; opposed gay/trans and abortion rights; backed a federal abortion ban effort; opposed recreational cannabis; funded efforts to end public-employee collective bargaining and make Ohio's constitution harder to amend; $4.3M to Tea Party Patriots 2015 to 2020 (Jan. 6 rally backer); $500K to Trump's 2017 inauguration. The couple owns multiple Florida properties; no Florida-specific giving described., [Wikipedia: Richard and Elizabeth Uihlein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_and_Elizabeth_Uihlein)
- Restoration of America PAC gave $2.5M (Oct. 2025) and $15M (reported Aug. 27, 2026) to Club for Growth Action., [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/club-for-growth-action-2026-spent-957-million-1); FactCheck profile of Restoration of America PAC., [FactCheck.org, Aug. 2026](https://www.factcheck.org/2026/08/restoration-of-america-pac/)
- Uihlein gave $2M to School Freedom Fund (2023) and $1M to AFC Victory Fund (2024)., [Farley Substack citing OpenSecrets](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/how-the-donalds-network-the-school)

**Peterffy**
- "Thomas Petterfy" [sic], Palm Beach billionaire, $1,000,000 to Donalds' political committee in Q2 2025., [Yahoo/Tampa Bay Times](https://www.yahoo.com/news/byron-donalds-dwarfs-david-jolly-163544449.html)
- Farley describes Peterffy's $1.2M as an "access buyer" gift, not ideological., [Farley Substack](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/how-the-donalds-network-the-school)
- Peterffy lives in Palm Beach near Mar-a-Lago and is a Mar-a-Lago Club member; said in Nov. 2022 Trump "can't get elected" and in Dec. 2023 he would not donate to Trump; then gave $500,000 (Aug. 5, 2024) and $344,660 (Aug. 12, 2024) to Trump 47 Committee; ~$7M to Republican causes before Aug. 2024; strongly free-market, anti-socialism., [Wikipedia: Thomas Peterffy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Peterffy)
- Interactive Brokers (Peterffy's firm) runs ForecastEx, a CFTC-regulated prediction market exchange offering political, economic and climate contracts; it appointed Philip Tetlock to its board Jan. 22, 2026., [Interactive Brokers press release, Jan. 22, 2026](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260122202508/en/ForecastEx-Appoints-Renowned-Forecaster-Dr.-Philip-Tetlock-to-Board-of-Directors)
- 2019: Peterffy was a repeat large donor to DeSantis' committee., [Florida Phoenix, Oct. 24, 2019](https://floridaphoenix.com/2019/10/24/pac-report-big-conservative-gop-donor-again-lavishes-cash-on-fl-gov-desantis/)

**Eric Schmidt**
- Eric Schmidt (former Google chairman/CEO) $500,000 to Friends of Byron Donalds, reported in the Oct. 3 to 9, 2026 filing window alongside GEO Group $1M and Fighting for Florida's Families $1M; the PAC raised $5.1M that week and ~$130.4M total., [Bradenton Journal Substack](https://thebradentonjournal.substack.com/p/donations-from-neal-beruff-add-to)
- No Schmidt-specific AI super PAC was identified in the fetched sources; Leading the Future's named funders are a16z, Brockman, Lonsdale, Conway, Perplexity., [Washington Sun/NOTUS, Jan. 30, 2026](https://www.washingtonsun.com/money/ai-super-pac-fundraising-midterms-democrats-republicans?redirected=notus_org)

**Wynn, Magowan, DeVos, Fields**
- Not found in any fetched source. The Real Deal (Oct. 8, 2026) "Who Real Estate's Big Money Backs in Florida Governor's Race" likely covers Wynn/Magowan but was robots-blocked., [The Real Deal](https://therealdeal.com/miami/2026/10/08/who-real-estates-big-money-backs-in-florida-governors-race/)
- Clive Fields is co-founder and former CMO of VillageMD., [VillageMD](https://www.villagemd.com/docs2docs/videos/why-i-started-villagemd)

**Other large Friends of Byron Donalds donors surfaced (for context)**
- GEO Group (Boca Raton private prison operator) $1M; Fighting for Florida's Families (Miami PC) $1M; Pat Neal ~$640K total; Carlos Beruff $100K (Oct. 8, 2026). Neal and Beruff are suing Manatee County over impact fees and lobbied against fixing SB 180., [Bradenton Journal](https://thebradentonjournal.substack.com/p/donations-from-neal-beruff-add-to)
- Associated Industries of Florida over $2M to Donalds' committee via linked PACs (Google $250K, Amazon $20K, Phillips Infrastructure $556K into AIF)., [Politico via NewsBreak, July 21, 2026](https://www.newsbreak.com/politico-560779/4782517602692-the-data-center-and-ai-donors-powering-donalds-bid-for-florida-governor)
- Suncoast Searchlight caption: $1M from Airbnb via an affiliated committee., [Suncoast Searchlight](https://suncoastsearchlight.org/?p=3923)

##### Inferences
- Uihlein's $2M direct plus $17.5M to Club for Growth Action (which spends in Florida and whose PAC endorsed Donalds) makes him a larger indirect Donalds backer than the direct figure suggests. Inference based on fungibility; no source traces Restoration dollars to Donalds-specific spending.
- Peterffy's ForecastEx gives him, like Yass (Kalshi) and Robinhood, a direct commercial stake in Florida not cracking down on prediction markets. No source states this as his motive.

##### Gaps
- DeVos ~$1M, Clive Fields $500K, Steve Wynn, Deborah Magowan: no retrievable source. The round-one figures stand unverified here.
- Peterffy's $1.2M (vs $1M) reconciliation not found; likely an additional $200K later in 2025 or 2026.
- No 2026 giving by Peterffy, Uihlein or DeVos to other Florida races located.

#### Key Question 5: Leading the Future, a16z, and state AI regulation; Eric Schmidt's vehicles

##### Takeaway
Leading the Future (founded Aug. 15, 2025; $140M+ network; a16z/Andreessen/Horowitz $50M, Brockmans $25M, Lonsdale, Conway, Perplexity) explicitly backs a "national regulatory framework for AI," which NOTUS describes as an industry euphemism for blocking state AI laws. Florida was its first state-level race: a $5M pledge (Feb. 2026), $3M to American Mission Florida (March 4, 2026), ~1,700 TV spots ($500K+) in Tampa and Tallahassee in March to April promoting Trump's endorsement and never mentioning AI. Its direct Florida interest was DeSantis' AI Bill of Rights (SB 482/HB 1395), which the House under Speaker Perez killed after White House contact, and the data center bill (SB 484), which passed in watered-down form and was signed May 7, 2026. Donalds has since pivoted rhetorically toward local control and ratepayer protection, prompting a rival AI PAC (Public First, Anthropic-linked) to run $1M+ in ads praising him for opposing federal preemption.

##### Cited Findings

**Leading the Future structure and goals**
- Formed Aug. 15, 2025. Donors: a16z (Andreessen and Horowitz) $50M in 2026; Greg and Anna Brockman $25M; Joe Lonsdale. Affiliates: American Mission PAC (supported Chris Gober, TX), Think Big PAC (spent $8M against Alex Bores, NY-12), Build American AI (501c4 led by Nathan Leamer, described by Axios as a dark money "offshoot"). Over $140M raised., [Wikipedia: Leading the Future](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leading_the_Future)
- NOTUS (Jan. 30, 2026): network raised $125M in H2 2025, $70M cash on hand entering 2026; LTF itself $50.3M raised, $39.3M cash; Brockman and wife $12.5M each, Andreessen and Horowitz $12.5M each personally; Lonsdale $250K to American Mission; Conway $500K to Think Big; LTF gave $5M each to Think Big, American Mission and Build American AI. Supports candidates "committed to a national regulatory framework for AI," which the article calls an industry euphemism for blocking state enforcement. Congress rejected limits on state AI laws twice in 2025., [Washington Sun/NOTUS](https://www.washingtonsun.com/money/ai-super-pac-fundraising-midterms-democrats-republicans?redirected=notus_org)
- Axios (Apr. 16, 2026): LTF raised $25M in Q1 2026, all from a16z and co-founders; ~$51M cash; routed $13M in Q1: $5M American Mission PAC, $5M Think Big, $3M American Mission Florida., [Axios](https://www.axios.com/2026/04/16/ai-influence-network-cash)
- Yahoo/Bloomberg-style piece on tech moguls: LTF advocates federal preemption of state AI laws; an a16z proposal would limit states mainly to consumer protection (child safety, insurance fraud); Trump signed an executive order threatening to sue states that pass AI laws; initial LTF focus states NY, CA, IL, OH; Build American AI launched a $10M campaign pressing Congress; Josh Vlasto co-head; DeSantis pushed back against Trump's moves., [Yahoo](https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/tech-moguls-close-trump-see-190246768.html)
- InfluenceWatch profile., [InfluenceWatch: Leading the Future](https://www.influencewatch.org/political-party/leading-the-future/)

**Florida activity**
- NBC (Feb. 2026): LTF to spend ~$5M for Donalds, its first state-level race and first gubernatorial candidate; part funds an AI "education" campaign; Zac Moffatt (LTF strategist) praised Donalds for recognizing a "magical era of technological advancement"; Ryan Smith (Donalds strategist) said Donalds backs "responsible innovation." DeSantis' package to regulate AI and limit data centers was blocked in the House. David Sacks and Taylor Budowich contacted Florida officials to oppose the DeSantis plan. White House initially distrusted LTF because its leaders were ex-Schumer staff; tensions eased and LTF says it works with Trump's outside advisers on the AI action plan., [NBC News](https://nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/super-pac-backed-ai-titans-pledges-5-million-boost-byron-donalds-run-f-rcna258612); [AOL mirror](https://www.aol.com/articles/super-pac-backed-ai-titans-130040320.html)
- American Mission Florida formed Feb. 2026, chaired by Sarasota County Property Appraiser Bill Furst, address a Tallahassee Staples; sole funding $3M from LTF on March 4, 2026; ~$2M toward pro-Donalds ads in March per Florida Politics; UCF's Aubrey Jewett: "In some ways it's a form of political money laundering.", [Suncoast Searchlight](https://suncoastsearchlight.org/?p=3923); [WUSF, Apr. 19, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-04-19/sarasota-property-appraiser-moving-millions-ai-moguls-support-byron-donalds)
- WUSF (July 20, 2026): FCC records show ~$63K to WFLA Tampa for 15-second spots March to April plus similar for 30-second spots; LTF did not answer what happened to the rest of the $5M pledge; Donalds ad: "In my Florida, if you don't protect our pocketbooks or our people, you won't build data centers, period.", [WUSF](https://wusf.org/text/politics-issues/2026-07-20/as-ai-grows-so-does-its-influence-on-florida-politics)
- Miami Herald via Florida Pundit / Hoodline (July 2026): at least $500K, ~1,700 spots in Tampa Bay and Tallahassee, March to April 2026; ads promoted Trump's endorsement ("He's hot as a pistol") and never mentioned AI; vendor Strategic Media Placement (alias of The Strategy Group Co.) and Summit Ridge Media Group LLC (Reno mailbox); Campaign Legal Center filed an FEC complaint in May 2026 alleging shell LLCs obscured vendors. Donalds has since "publicly shifted toward supporting state-level AI regulation as governor.", [Florida Pundit, July 5, 2026](https://thefloridapundit.com/2026/07/05/ai-pac-byron-donalds-florida-governor-race/); [Hoodline, July 2, 2026](https://hoodline.com/2026/07/secretive-pro-ai-cash-fuels-byron-donalds-tv-blitz-across-florida/)
- Tech Influence Watch: American Mission PAC (federal) has $22.75M in contributions and $16.27M in independent expenditures, 100% supporting Republicans; Florida federal spending: $194,627 for Mike Haridopolos (FL-8, Oct. 9, 2026) and $207,329 for Laurel Lee (FL-15 primary). Statewide crypto/AI PAC totals in Florida ~$7.3M: Protect Progress $2.21M, Defending Our Values $1.60M, American Mission $402K, Think Big $310K., [Tech Influence Watch: American Mission](https://influence.citationneeded.news/2026/committees/C00916692); [Florida page](https://influence.citationneeded.news/2026/states/florida)
- Newsweek (Aug. 17, 2026): LTF spent $800K backing Ty Masterson in the Kansas governor primary; endorsed Republican state legislative candidates in Florida as part of 18 endorsements across eight states; Public First Action ran ads backing Donalds for opposing federal preemption; Meta-funded PACs active in CA, IL, TX., [Newsweek](https://www.newsweek.com/ai-super-pac-spending-map-2026-elections-12333140)
- Politico (July 21, 2026): Public First (Anthropic CEO-backed) spent over $1M on a broadcast ad saying Donalds opposes federal preemption of state AI laws; a16z partners gave at least $36K to Donalds' federal committees in Feb. 2025; Brendan Steinhauser (Alliance for Secure AI): "Six months ago, he was sounding more like the industry, and now I think he sounds more like us." LTF spokesperson: "We need smart, responsible AI policy that will unlock the economic benefits that can power Florida's future and protect children and communities.", [Politico via NewsBreak](https://www.newsbreak.com/politico-560779/4782517602692-the-data-center-and-ai-donors-powering-donalds-bid-for-florida-governor)

**The Florida bills LTF cared about**
- Dec. 2025: DeSantis proposed an AI Bill of Rights: AI interaction notices, ban on AI therapy, parental controls, no state subsidies for data centers, limits on data center water draw, sound reviews., [Yahoo/NBC, Dec. 2025](https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/reject-every-fiber-being-desantis-190000224.html)
- March 6, 2026: SB 482 (Leek, AI Bill of Rights) passed the Senate 35-2; HB 1395 (House companion) got no hearing; SB 484 (data center guardrails) advanced; HB 1007 (House data center bill) moved late. Perez: "We do believe that the federal government should take care of AI," aligned with the White House., [WLRN, March 6, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-03-06/desantis-heightens-push-for-ai-rules-as-bill-falters)
- March 9, 2026: HB 527 (Cassel, human review of AI insurance claim denials) passed House 108-0; SB 202 companion got no Senate hearing., [Florida Bar News](https://www.floridabar.org/the-florida-bar-news/florida-lawmakers-split-on-ai-regulation/)
- April 20, 2026: DeSantis called a special session (Apr. 28 to May 1) for the AI Bill of Rights; House "refused to touch" it; White House contacted Perez to discourage it; Donalds got a $5M pledge from a pro-AI super PAC "last month.", [Florida Phoenix, Apr. 20, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/04/20/floridas-ai-bill-of-rights-what-happened-whats-in-it-and-whats-next/)
- May 7, 2026: DeSantis signed SB 484: data centers pay their own utility costs (PSC enforces); OPPAGA study due July 1, 2027; one-year confidentiality for expansion plans; local land-use authority preserved; does not ban NDAs. Senate passed it in the session's final hours after the House rewrote it following White House conversations with House members (per Florida Politics)., [Florida Phoenix, May 7, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/05/07/florida-has-a-new-law-regulating-ai-data-centers/)
- NBC headline: "Republicans in Florida again block Ron DeSantis' push to regulate AI" (page robots-blocked)., [NBC](https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2028-election/republicans-florida-block-ron-desantis-push-regulate-ai-rcna342489)

**Donalds' evolving AI/data center position**
- June 2025: "I think the future of the state, what's going to be important are data centers and power." Sept. 2026: "If local governments don't want them, they won't build them." Strongly supports SB 484, sponsored by his running mate Bryan Avila; no comment on Avila's SB 1118 (data center confidentiality, died on Senate floor). Voted May 2025 for the House OBBBA with a 10-year state AI regulation moratorium, now says he "wasn't probably the biggest fan of" it. July 2026 introduced the federal Protecting Ratepayers Act requiring data centers to source off-grid power and water. UNF July poll: 68% oppose a local AI data center; 19 Florida local governments have paused or banned data centers., [Florida Phoenix via Orlando Weekly, Sept. 16, 2026](https://www.orlandoweekly.com/news/florida-news/floridians-are-raising-the-alarms-over-data-centers-and-ai-where-do-byron-donalds-and-david-jolly-stand/)
- July 24, 2026: Donalds opposes a statewide data center moratorium (Jolly supports a one-year one), favors slowing construction, says a New York-style moratorium signals Florida is closed to capital., [WUSF, July 24, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-07-24/byron-donalds-opposes-statewide-moratorium-data-centers)
- Oct. 20, 2026: Democratic candidates (Victor Arias FL-19, Phillip Carter HD-76) rallied in Cape Coral denouncing Donalds' data center donors (Duke Energy, NextEra Energy Capital Holdings, Ferreira Construction, TECO Holdings); Donalds' office did not respond., [Lehigh Acres Citizen, Oct. 20, 2026](https://www.lehighacrescitizen.com/2026/10/20/democratic-candidates-denounce-donalds-for-data-center-contributions/)
- Anti-Donalds electioneering site "datacenterdonalds.com" is paid for by Conservatives for Ethical Leadership (Miami, Ch. 106 PC); claims NextEra gave $1M (source link actually about Airbnb; attribution unclear)., [datacenterdonalds.com](https://datacenterdonalds.com/)

**Eric Schmidt's AI policy vehicles**
- No fetched source tied Schmidt to Leading the Future, Public First or a Florida-specific AI vehicle; his only documented Donalds tie is the $500K to Friends of Byron Donalds (Oct. 2026 filing)., [Bradenton Journal](https://thebradentonjournal.substack.com/p/donations-from-neal-beruff-add-to)
- Earlier reporting: Schmidt funded dozens of federal science/tech policy staff positions via Schmidt Futures during the Biden administration., [Yahoo/Politico](https://news.yahoo.com/ex-google-boss-helps-fund-093000306.html?fr=sycsrp_catchall)

##### Inferences
- LTF's Florida spending was a bet on the governor who would succeed the one Republican governor openly fighting the industry on state preemption. Its $3M was deployed in March to April 2026, exactly when the House was sitting on SB 482; the timing supports the reading that the money was meant to signal to Tallahassee Republicans as much as to voters. This is inference; LTF's ads never mentioned AI.
- The fact that LTF's ads omitted AI, and that the rival Anthropic-aligned PAC then praised Donalds for the opposite position, suggests Donalds has become a contested figure between AI factions rather than a captured one; his actual record (OBBBA moratorium vote, SB 484 support, Protecting Ratepayers Act) is mixed.
- The remaining ~$2M of LTF's $5M pledge was not traced; it may sit in American Mission Florida or have gone to the federal American Mission PAC's Florida House spending (Haridopolos, Lee).

##### Gaps
- No LTF "model state bill" document located; the a16z preemption proposal is described only in summary.
- Names of the Florida state legislative candidates LTF endorsed (Newsweek) were not retrievable.
- NPR June 22, 2026 piece on OpenAI/Anthropic-linked PAC totals was robots-blocked.
- Floridian Press story on a PAC "funded by AI investor" targeting Florida Democrats could not be fetched after repeated rate limits.
- No source on Schmidt's 2026 AI super PAC giving (e.g., to Public First or LTF).

#### Key Question 6: Crypto (Winklevoss/Gemini, Fairshake in Florida, Florida bitcoin reserve bill, Kalshi and Robinhood state asks, Donalds' crypto proposals)

##### Takeaway
The Winklevosses' $1M ($500K each) to Donalds is a small piece of a $32M+ 2025 Republican giving surge ($21M in bitcoin to their own Digital Freedom Fund, $10M to MAGA Inc. in June 2026 three weeks after the administration moved to vacate a Gemini enforcement action) aimed at making the U.S. "the crypto capital of the world"; no Florida-specific ask was found. Fairshake's network (Protect Progress, Defend American Jobs) spent ~$2.2M in Florida 2026, mainly a failed $2.06M effort against Oliver Gilbert in FL-24, plus support for Patronis, Fine, Gruters, Frankel and Haridopolos; it has not reported spending in the governor's race. Florida's bitcoin reserve bill (HB 1039/SB 1038, Snyder/Gruters) cleared its first House committee 18-0 on Feb. 11, 2026 but no final passage was found. Kalshi ($268K Florida, $75K to Donalds) and Robinhood ($200K, all to Donalds) are spending as unlicensed sports event contract operators in a state where the Seminole Tribe holds the online sports betting monopoly and where Florida has "so far avoided challenging the sector."

##### Cited Findings

**Winklevoss / Gemini**
- $1M combined ($500K each) to Donalds' committee (reported by Aug. 2025)., [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/08/the-two-campaigns-byron-donalds-he-appears-violating-state-law/)
- Axios (Sept. 5, 2025): Winklevoss giving exceeds $32M: $21M to Digital Freedom Fund (new pro-Trump, pro-crypto super PAC, apparently targeting Sherrod Brown), $2.6M+ to CLF/NRCC, $1.6M+ to NRSC/SLF, $1M MAGA Inc., $800K+ RNC, $330K+ to RFK Jr. debt. They urged the White House to drop Brian Quintenz's CFTC nomination over his Kalshi board seat. Tyler: Republicans must keep Congress to make America "the crypto capital of the world.", [Axios](https://www.axios.com/2025/09/05/winklevoss-twins-gop-crypto-megadonors)
- The Lever (July 20, 2026): each brother gave $5M in bitcoin to MAGA Inc. on June 19, 2026, less than a month after the administration moved to vacate a Biden-era Gemini enforcement action with a $5M penalty; Quintenz's CFTC nomination allegedly withdrawn after he refused Tyler's texts to prioritize vacating it; Sen. Warren letter June 5 to CFTC Chair Michael Selig; they invest in American Bitcoin (Eric Trump co-founded). No Florida or state-level asks., [The Lever](https://www.levernews.com/the-winklevoss-twins-gave-trump-10-million-after-regulatory-win/)
- Bloomberg (Jan. 30, 2026): bitcoin price drop cut the Winklevoss super PAC donation by nearly $5M., [Bloomberg](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2026-01-30/bitcoin-slump-cuts-millions-from-winklevoss-super-pac-donation)
- Winklevoss Capital gave $21,000 directly to Jimmy Patronis (FL-1) and, with Oracle, $22,500 to Randy Fine (FL-6)., [Tech Influence Watch Florida](https://influence.citationneeded.news/2026/states/florida)

**Fairshake network in Florida**
- Fairshake (founded 2023, based in Palm City, Florida) entered 2026 with $120M to $193M; crypto midterm spending ~$206M by Oct. 2026; ~$30M against Sherrod Brown; endorsed 32 House candidates (19 R, 13 D)., [CB Insights](https://www.cbinsights.com/company/fairshake)
- Protect Progress (Fairshake affiliate) spent $2,056,911 opposing Oliver Gilbert in FL-24 (he won the Aug. 18, 2026 Democratic primary with ~35%) and $151,713 supporting Lois Frankel (FL-23); Defend American Jobs spent $558,813 for Patronis (FL-1 special), $1,673,736 for Randy Fine (FL-6 special), $501,743 for Sydney Gruters (FL-16), and with American Mission $269,559 for Haridopolos (FL-8). Kalshi, Meta, Google and others gave $29K to Darren Soto (FL-9). a16z and Google gave $25,500 to Donalds' House committee., [Tech Influence Watch Florida](https://influence.citationneeded.news/2026/states/florida)
- Protect Progress ads used mocked-up Miami Herald headlines the paper said misrepresented its reporting; Fairshake's Geoff Vetter defended them; Fairshake's congressional primary record 49 of 53., [Crypto Briefing, Aug. 19, 2026](https://cryptobriefing.com/fairshake-2m-setback-florida-primaries/); [Cointelegraph](https://regional-front.cointelegraph.com/news/crypto-pac-wins-primaries-loses-florida-race)

**Florida bitcoin reserve bill**
- HB 1039 filed Jan. 7, 2026 by Rep. John Snyder; creates a Strategic Cryptocurrency Reserve Fund outside the treasury managed by the CFO; no minimum allocation; a 2025 version (up to 10% of certain funds) was withdrawn May 2025; Sen. Joe Gruters and then-CFO Jimmy Patronis ("digital gold") backed earlier efforts; DeSantis signed a 2023 anti-CBDC law., [CoinDesk, Jan. 8, 2026](https://www.coindesk.com/policy/2026/01/08/florida-revives-push-for-bitcoin-reserve-with-new-2026-bill)
- House staff analysis (Feb. 13, 2026): CS/HB 1039, companion CS/SB 1038 (Gruters); reserve assigned to the State Board of Administration; eligible assets must average $100B+ market cap over 12 months (only BTC, ETH, Tether qualify); custodian and liquidity-provider rules; five-member advisory committee; biennial reports; effective July 1, 2026; Insurance & Banking Subcommittee approved 18-0 on Feb. 11, 2026; still needed Government Operations, State Administration & Budget, and Commerce., [Florida House analysis](https://www.flsenate.gov/Session/Bill/2026/1039/Analyses/h1039a.IBS.PDF)
- Bitcoin Magazine and crypto.news coverage of the 2026 revival and the "up to 10%" framing., [Bitcoin Magazine](https://bitcoinmagazine.com/news/florida-revives-bitcoin-reserve-push); [crypto.news](https://crypto.news/floridas-bitcoin-digital-gold-reserve-bill-targets-up-to-10-of-state-funds/)

**Kalshi, Robinhood and prediction markets in Florida**
- Florida gambling and prediction-market giving through July 31, 2026 topped $12.2M: Seminole Tribe ~$9.1M ($4M to Donalds); FanDuel ~$581K; DK Crown ~$525K; Kalshi $268K ($75K to Donalds); Robinhood $200K (all to Donalds); Caesars $245K; Sports Betting Alliance $250K (all Donalds); PrizePicks/Sideprize ~$400K; Underdog $280K+; Poarch Creek $555K ($100K to Donalds). Over $6M of gambling money went to Donalds. Prediction markets offer sports contracts without a Florida license; "Florida has so far avoided challenging the sector"; "the contributions do not establish how Donalds would approach prediction markets as governor." Trump is a vocal prediction-market supporter., [Gambling Insider, Aug. 12, 2026](https://www.gamblinginsider.com/news/185823/gambling-prediction-markets-florida-elections-byron-donalds); [Florida Online Gambling](https://www.floridaonlinegambling.net/news/florida-gambling-money-pours-in-before-2026-midterm-elections/)
- The 2021 compact gives the Seminole Tribe's Hard Rock Bet the legal mobile sports betting position in Florida via the hub-and-spoke server model., [Florida Online Gambling](https://www.floridaonlinegambling.net/news/florida-gambling-money-pours-in-before-2026-midterm-elections/)
- Robinhood's two donations totaling $200K came just over a month after Donalds and his wife sold Robinhood stock in March 2025; Robinhood co-founder Baiju Bhatt gave $50K., [Politico via NewsBreak](https://www.newsbreak.com/politico-560779/4782517602692-the-data-center-and-ai-donors-powering-donalds-bid-for-florida-governor)
- Kalshi's state fights (none in Florida): Massachusetts suit Sept. 2025 and Jan. 2026 injunction; Ohio federal ruling Mar. 9, 2026 that its products are gambling; Arizona charges Mar. 17, 2026 dismissed May 2026 on preemption; Michigan suit Mar. 2026; Nevada TRO Mar. 20, 2026 and July 24 agreement to halt; Washington injunction July 2026; Wisconsin suit Apr. 2026; Minnesota ban (Aug. 1, 2026) struck in July on CFTC exclusive jurisdiction; New York AG suit July 2026 seeking ~$36B; CFTC countersued Wisconsin and New York. Tribal suits: three California rancherias (July 2025), Ho-Chunk (Sept. 2025). Donald Trump Jr. is a Kalshi strategic adviser (Jan. 2025, $300K+ in shares) and Polymarket adviser/investor via 1789 Capital., [Wikipedia: Kalshi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kalshi)
- Peterffy's Interactive Brokers operates ForecastEx, a CFTC-regulated prediction market., [Business Wire, Jan. 22, 2026](https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20260122202508/en/ForecastEx-Appoints-Renowned-Forecaster-Dr.-Philip-Tetlock-to-Board-of-Directors)

##### Inferences
- The structural ask of Kalshi, Robinhood, Yass (Kalshi investor) and Peterffy (ForecastEx) from a Florida governor is non-enforcement: that Florida's AG and gaming regulators, and the Seminole Tribe with state backing, not follow Massachusetts, Nevada, Ohio, New York etc. in attacking sports event contracts. The Seminole Tribe's $4M to Donalds is the countervailing interest. No source documents Donalds' position; the Tampa Bay Times June 2026 piece notes Kalshi simultaneously promotes Donalds' odds and funds him.
- Fairshake's absence from the governor's race and its focus on congressional seats suggests the crypto industry views Donalds as safe and is spending where outcomes are uncertain.
- Donalds' pro-crypto positioning (Gemini/a16z/Robinhood donors; Trump-aligned) makes a bitcoin reserve signing plausible if HB 1039/SB 1038 reaches a governor, but no Donalds proposal for a Florida bitcoin reserve was found.

##### Gaps
- No source found for any Donalds-authored Florida crypto proposal (bitcoin reserve, stablecoin, mining, or state pension crypto allocation).
- Final 2026 session status of HB 1039/SB 1038 not found (last documented step: Feb. 11, 2026 committee vote).
- No source on Gemini/Winklevoss Florida-specific operations or asks.
- Fairshake donor list not retrieved (Coinbase, Ripple, a16z are the known 2024 funders but no 2026 source was fetched).

#### Key Question 7: Club for Growth's Florida PAC structure and who else it funded in 2026 Florida

##### Takeaway
Club for Growth operates in Florida through at least four vehicles: Club for Growth PAC (federal, endorsements and candidate-directed giving; endorsed Donalds, Uthmeier, Luna, Beltran), Club for Growth Action (federal super PAC; ~$705K against Casey Askar in FL-22, ~$456K against Kathy Castor in FL-14), Win It Back PAC (affiliated super PAC, $4.5M+ from CFG Action; $1.1M against Chris Collins in FL-19), and a Florida state political committee ("Florida branch of Club for Growth" / Club for Growth Action Florida) that put $975K into Friends of Byron Donalds in Q2 2025 and through which round one traced $3.5M of Yass money; the state committee's exact name and ledger could not be retrieved. No Club spending on Florida legislative races was found.

##### Cited Findings
- Endorsements: Donalds (Gov), Uthmeier (AG), Luna (FL-13), Beltran (FL-14)., [Club for Growth PAC](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/elections/pac-endorsed-candidates/)
- CFG Action Florida IEs: ~$704,806 opposing Casey Askar (FL-22 GOP primary); ~$455,986 opposing Kathy Castor (FL-14)., [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/club-for-growth-action-2026-spent-957-million-1); [FactCheck.org](https://www.factcheck.org/2026/10/club-for-growth-action-8/)
- Win It Back PAC: $1.1M FL-19 buy against Chris Collins starting late July 2026., [Axios](https://www.axios.com/2026/07/27/republicans-florida-19-chris-collins-club-for-growth); [Club for Growth](https://www.clubforgrowth.org/icymi-win-it-back-pac-hits-collins-with-1-1-million-buy-in-fl-19/)
- Florida state committee: "Florida branch of Club for Growth" $975K to Donalds' PC in Q2 2025., [Yahoo/Tampa Bay Times](https://www.yahoo.com/news/byron-donalds-dwarfs-david-jolly-163544449.html); Club for Growth PAC at $3.65M to Friends of Byron Donalds., [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-tiktok-billionaire-bankrolling-byron-donalds-40558946/)
- CFG Action funders: Yass ($10M Nov. 2025 plus others; $15M in 2026 per Wikipedia), Restoration of America (Uihlein) $17.5M, Marsha for Senate $4.8M., [Legis1](https://legis1.com/news/club-for-growth-action-2026-spent-957-million-1); [Wikipedia: Jeff Yass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Yass)
- WUSF explainer on Florida PACs (Sept. 9, 2026)., [WUSF](https://wusf.org/text/politics-issues/2026-09-09/what-to-know-about-pacs-your-florida-breaks-it-down)

##### Inferences
- The Club's Florida footprint is overwhelmingly federal (House primaries) plus the governor and AG races; it is not a visible player in Florida legislative primaries in 2026 based on retrieved sources.
- Uthmeier's endorsement matters for the prediction-market and AI questions above: as AG he would decide whether Florida joins other states in suing Kalshi, and whether to enforce any state AI law; the Club's backing of both Donalds and Uthmeier aligns the executive and AG on a deregulatory line. Inference.

##### Gaps
- Exact Florida Division of Elections name, chair, treasurer and cumulative receipts/expenditures of the Club's Florida state committee not retrieved (floridapolitics.com and Transparency USA fetches failed; search budget exhausted).
- No source on Club for Growth or School Freedom Fund spending in 2026 Florida state House or Senate primaries.
- Whether the Club's Florida committee spent in the Aug. 18 gubernatorial primary against Collins/Renner/Fishback beyond transfers to Donalds' PC is unknown.

### Note: detention_second_hop.md

_Folder: Donalds second hop who gains_

### Second hop: the immigration detention and enforcement money behind Byron Donalds, the vendors, their state contracts, what they are owed, what they want, and what a Donalds administration would mean

Scope note. Research run Oct 22, 2026. WebFetch was rate limited for part of the run and the sandbox proxy blocks direct page downloads, so a few pages (Tampa Bay Times July 3, 2025 contractor list; the Yahoo/Miami Herald "fraud, price-gouging" piece; GEO Q1 2026 8-K) could not be read in full. Figures below are only those a readable page stated. Round one facts (GEO $2.5M plus $1M Oct 3 to 9; CDR $620K plus; ARS $100K plus; Lemoine $59K; CRS $10K; CDR Enterprises $250K to Collins) are treated as established and not re-sourced here except where a readable page restated them.

#### Key question 1: Alligator Alcatraz, Deportation Depot and any others: contractors, contract values, emergency procurement, the $417M owed, FEMA reimbursement, litigation and current operating status

##### Takeaway
Alligator Alcatraz (Everglades, opened July 3, 2025) was formally closed by DeSantis on June 25, 2026 with zero detainees and was fully dismantled by mid July 2026; Deportation Depot (Baker Correctional Institution, Sanderson) is still operating with about 1,000 detainees under a $101M GEO|CDR Baker contract. As of June 30, 2026 FDEM owed more than $417M on 592 immigration invoices; the Legislature gave FDEM another $250M on Sept 11, 2026 on party line votes; of the $608M FEMA grant Florida had received $84.5M; FDEM projects $500M more in immigration spending in FY 2026-27.

##### Cited Findings

Facilities and status
- Alligator Alcatraz: federal government approved the facility in June 2025; it opened July 2025; first detainees arrived Thursday July 3, 2025, [WLOS/CBS, May 12, 2026](https://wlos.com/news/nation-world/alligator-alcatraz-close-june-1400-detainees-locate-detention-facility-florida-everglades-tax-payer-money-controversy-governor-ron-desantis-donald-trump-florida-news); [NBC Miami/AP, July 3, 2025](https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/local/republican-donors-and-floridas-hurricane-know-how-helped-build-alligator-alcatraz-so-quickly/3651580/)
- May 12, 2026: vendors were told the Everglades facility was closing; about 1,400 detainees remained; CBS News reported the decision was driven primarily by rising operating costs, estimated to reach $1 billion; sources said the state expected the $608M to be reimbursed but had no guarantee for an additional roughly $300M, [WLOS/CBS, May 12, 2026](https://wlos.com/news/nation-world/alligator-alcatraz-close-june-1400-detainees-locate-detention-facility-florida-everglades-tax-payer-money-controversy-governor-ron-desantis-donald-trump-florida-news)
- June 25, 2026: DeSantis announced at Ochopee that Alligator Alcatraz was "officially shutting down," with zero detainees; he said it facilitated almost 21,000 deportations; demobilization was underway, [E&E News, June 25, 2026](https://www.eenews.net/articles/alligator-alcatraz-officially-shutters-after-weeks-of-wind-down-rumors/)
- Site reported "completely dismantled" one month after the closure announcement, [WFLX, July 15, 2026](https://www.wflx.com/2026/07/15/alligator-alcatraz-completely-dismantled-1-month-after-closure-announcement/) (headline only; body not fetched)
- Deportation Depot (former Baker Correctional Institution, Sanderson, about 40 miles west of Jacksonville) continues to operate, holding about 1,000 detainees as of Oct 15, 2026; an ICE spokesperson said at the September 2025 opening it could hold up to 2,000; two detainees were mistakenly released and recovered the weekend before Oct 15, 2026; FDEM spokeswoman Stephanie Hartman: "There is no ongoing threat to the public.", [WLRN, Oct 15, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/immigration/2026-10-15/state-mistakenly-releases-and-recovers-two-detainees-from-deportation-depot)
- Deportation Depot operator contract: GEO|CDR BAKER, created late August 2025, leaders from The GEO Group and CDR Health Care; maximum contract value $101M; about $32M paid as of Sept 22, 2026, [WLRN, Oct 15, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/immigration/2026-10-15/state-mistakenly-releases-and-recovers-two-detainees-from-deportation-depot)
- Deportation Depot was announced Aug 14, 2025 and chosen over Camp Blanding for its "lowest price-point", [Florida Phoenix, Aug 14, 2025](https://floridaphoenix.com/2025/08/14/second-immigrant-deportation-facility-to-open-in-north-florida/); [Action News Jax](https://www.actionnewsjax.com/news/local/lowest-price-point-deportation-depot-will-open-empty-baker-county-prison-not-camp-blanding/H6EJ6EOE7BA4ZBGG37QDZULJMM/) (headline only)
- A third facility, "Panhandle Pokey," was floated by DeSantis in September 2025, [Orlando Weekly](https://www.orlandoweekly.com/news/florida-news/desantis-talks-new-immigrant-detention-facility-panhandle-pokey/); [NewsNation](https://www.newsnationnow.com/us-news/immigration/panhandle-pokey-florida-immigration-detention-center/) (headlines only; I found no readable report that it was ever opened)

Contracts and emergency procurement
- By July 22, 2025 the state had awarded at least two dozen Alligator Alcatraz contracts totaling more than $245M; named: Critical Response Strategies $78.5M (warden, camp managers, corrections officers, IT); Longview Solutions Group $25.6M (site prep, civil engineering, roads, fencing); Doodie Calls $22M (toilets, shower trailers); Gothams $21.1M (IT, badges, detainee wristbands); SLSCO LTD $19.7M and Garner Environmental Services $19.7M (buildout and maintenance). Full contracts were later removed from the state database, reportedly for "proprietary information," leaving one page invoices, [NBC Miami, July 22, 2025](https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/local/florida-signs-245-million-in-contracts-for-alligator-alcatraz-heres-a-look-by-the-numbers/3663736/)
- Early contracts: Lemoine CDR Logistics $60M (June 23, 2025); CDR Health TNT $56M (about two weeks later); Garner $102M buildout (July 2025) and $49M for Baker Correctional (September 2025); IRG Global Emergency Management $51M transportation (June 2025); Williams Communications $245,000 (July 2025); AT&T $12,500 (July 2025), [Florida Trib (Kate Payne), Oct 22, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/10/22/from-themed-merch-to-donor-checks-how-uthmeier-profited-from-alligator-alcatraz/)
- Other early deals: Granny's Alliance Holdings $3.3M meals; IRG Global $1.1M "flight and operational support", [NBC Miami/AP, July 3, 2025](https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/local/republican-donors-and-floridas-hurricane-know-how-helped-build-alligator-alcatraz-so-quickly/3651580/)
- IRG Global: $1.1M contract June 24, 2025; $2.9M and $2.2M contracts July 1, 2025 for shuttles, armory, on-site emergency services and air operations, [Miami Herald via Daily Gazette syndication](https://www.dailygazette.com/tribune/alligator-alcatraz-firm-donated-to-florida-gop-before-getting-a-contract/article_604b80e5-9602-52f4-a3e1-92c4ff5dd05e.html)
- Purchase orders June 18, 2025: Garner about $6M for four Boeing 777s; IRG $19.68M for Boeing aircraft, [Unicorn Riot, Feb 6, 2026](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)
- GEO-CDR BAKER LLC billed the state $100,357,654.76, the largest single bill in the invoices reviewed; registered in Florida August 2025; authorized persons include Carlos Duart, Tina Vidal-Duart, GEO founder George Zoley and GEO CEO James David Donahue; shares an address with CDR Health, [Unicorn Riot, Feb 6, 2026](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)
- Florida Bulldog found 719 emergency procurement contracts without competitive bidding, including 45 to CDR Maguire (as cited by Unicorn Riot), [Unicorn Riot, Feb 6, 2026](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)
- DeSantis has extended his January 2023 immigration state of emergency more than 20 times, [Florida Phoenix, Sept 11, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/09/11/desantis-administration-misses-legislative-deadline-gets-more-money-for-immigration-anyway/)
- Daily burn: state estimated more than $1.2M per day to support 500 detainees; more than $3M per day in the earliest weeks; Doodie Calls paid more than $92M by March 2026; warden paid $1,000 per day; corrections officers projected base above $120,000 per year; 396 staff at one point against roughly 500 needed, [Florida Trib, Mar 1, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/03/01/new-records-show-florida-officials-burned-more-than-1-2-million-per-day-on-alligator-alcatraz/)

What is owed
- As of June 17, 2026: Florida still owed at least $603M to 27 of 38 companies with contracts; FDEM had paid about 40% of immigration contracts; detention contract obligations about $991M (Miami Herald count) or $1.01B (Phoenix count); FDEM's own September 2025 projection was $1.7B for the two facilities; emergency fund balance $47M, down from $199M in late April 2026. Named: Doodie Calls at least $219M total, $96M paid, $122M owed; IRG Global about $112M in contracts; GEO-CDR Baker about $100M; GardaWorld $78M promised; Nassau County Sheriff nearly $500,000 owed; Lee and Hendry sheriffs about $332,000 combined, [Florida Phoenix (Liv Caputo), June 17, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/06/17/florida-still-owes-603-million-on-immigration-enforcement-contracts/)
