- As of June 30, 2026: more than $417M unpaid across 592 pending immigration vendor invoices, plus $43.4M in storm invoices; FDEM director Jared Perdue said the federal government still owes Florida more than $523M for immigration enforcement, [WUSF/Florida Trib (Kate Payne), Sept 14, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/local-state/2026-09-14/this-state-agency-owes-millions-in-immigration-bills-florida-lawmakers-just-cut-a-check)
- Sept 30, 2026 vendor level owed figures: IRG Global more than $68M; Critical Response Strategies more than $35M; CDR more than $40M for immigration work beyond the $5,184,400 paid to CDR Health from the emergency fund; GEO|CDR Baker nearly $40M; Lemoine CDR Logistics about $27.3M, [WUSF (Meghan Bowman), Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)
- FY 2025-26 FDEM spending about $989M, of which nearly $568M immigration related, the costliest "emergency" of the year, [WUSF, Sept 17, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-17/fdem-spent-about-1-billion-immigration-last-year-now-itll-get-more)
- FDEM's cumulative immigration spending since the emergency fund's 2022 creation was $573M as of records released around March 1, 2026, [Florida Trib, Mar 1, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/03/01/new-records-show-florida-officials-burned-more-than-1-2-million-per-day-on-alligator-alcatraz/)

Legislative funding
- Sept 11, 2026 Joint Legislative Budget Commission, party line vote: $240M general revenue transfer to the Emergency Preparedness and Response Fund; up to $250M spending authority for FDEM to pay pending immigration, hurricane and wildfire invoices; $7.56M for Department of Military Affairs payroll. FDEM's required report was due July 15 and arrived about an hour before the meeting. About $187M of the $250M (roughly three quarters) is for immigration and detention invoices. FDEM reserves July 1 were about $6.4M. Projected $1.5B deficit in the fund for FY 2026-27 assuming $2B for two major hurricanes, $100M non-hurricane weather, $500M continued immigration enforcement. Since 2022 lawmakers have moved more than $5B into the fund, [Florida Phoenix, Sept 11, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/09/11/desantis-administration-misses-legislative-deadline-gets-more-money-for-immigration-anyway/); [WUSF, Sept 17, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-17/fdem-spent-about-1-billion-immigration-last-year-now-itll-get-more); [WUSF, Sept 14, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/local-state/2026-09-14/this-state-agency-owes-millions-in-immigration-bills-florida-lawmakers-just-cut-a-check)
- Quotes: Perdue: "The bulk of this request is for outstanding invoices and services that have been rendered"; Rep. Allison Tant: "I want to make sure that we're making those vendors whole"; Perdue: "And I can assure you we are"; Sen. Lori Berman: "The continued abuse of this emergency power is an insult to our constitution and the people of Florida"; Rep. Anna Eskamani: "This agency was given a blank check, acted irresponsibly (and) just got more money of your tax dollars"; Karen Woodall (Florida Center for Fiscal and Economic Policy): "It was our hope that by having the legislative budget commission involved, we would have legislative oversight... That's obviously not what's happening.", [WUSF, Sept 14, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/local-state/2026-09-14/this-state-agency-owes-millions-in-immigration-bills-florida-lawmakers-just-cut-a-check); [WUSF, Sept 17, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-17/fdem-spent-about-1-billion-immigration-last-year-now-itll-get-more)
- DeSantis said he is not responsible for paying vendors; FDEM is. DeSantis: "I'd rather do what's right, and I'd rather protect the people that I was elected to serve.", [Florida Phoenix, June 17, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/06/17/florida-still-owes-603-million-on-immigration-enforcement-contracts/)

FEMA reimbursement timeline
- FDEM grant application signed Aug 7, 2025 for $1.49B; FEMA award $608M effective Sept 30, 2025; DeSantis celebrated Oct 2, 2025; $89,670,048.85 made available Nov 26, 2025; FDEM requested more than $30M Dec 8, 2025; FEMA denied it Dec 10, 2025 citing an environmental review hold; Feb 24, 2026 state attorneys told a court reimbursement may never come, [Florida Trib, Mar 1, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/03/01/new-records-show-florida-officials-burned-more-than-1-2-million-per-day-on-alligator-alcatraz/)
- First $58M reimbursement arrived in late May 2026, [Florida Phoenix, May 15, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/05/15/alligator-alcatraz-payments-land-at-last-58-million-to-hit-florida-next-week/); [Florida Phoenix, May 29, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/05/29/trump-admin-pays-florida-first-58-million-in-alligator-alcatraz-reimbursements-state-says/) (headlines; body confirmed via June 17 piece)
- Federal rules bar using the grant for construction or facility modification, [Florida Phoenix, June 17, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/06/17/florida-still-owes-603-million-on-immigration-enforcement-contracts/)
- Received to date: $84M (Phoenix, Sept 11) / $84.5M (Trib, Sept 14) / about 14% of $608M (WUSF, Sept 17), [Florida Phoenix, Sept 11, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/09/11/desantis-administration-misses-legislative-deadline-gets-more-money-for-immigration-anyway/); [WUSF, Sept 14, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/local-state/2026-09-14/this-state-agency-owes-millions-in-immigration-bills-florida-lawmakers-just-cut-a-check)

Litigation
- Environmental case (Friends of the Everglades et al. v. FDEM and federal defendants, Miccosukee Tribe intervening): a Florida federal judge (Judge Kathleen Williams, per the Phoenix headline "Federal judge halts operations") preliminarily enjoined construction in August 2025; the 11th Circuit stayed that order in September 2025, [Legal Insurrection, Sept 2025](https://legalinsurrection.com/2025/09/court-lifts-injunction-alligator-alcatraz-can-remain-open/amp/); [Florida Phoenix](https://floridaphoenix.com/?p=233726) (headlines)
- April 30, 2026: a split 11th Circuit panel vacated and remanded the injunction, holding plaintiffs failed to show "final agency action" under the APA or "major federal action" under NEPA, [Mealey's, Apr 30, 2026](https://www.mealeys.com/mealeys/mealeys-pollution-liability/articles/2472322/split-11th-circuit-revives-alligator-alcatraz-says-apa-nepa-claims-won-t-stick); environmental groups asked for reconsideration, [Denver Gazette](https://www.denvergazette.com/?p=381088) (headline)
- A separate air pollution lawsuit was reported May 27, 2026, [E&E News, June 25, 2026](https://www.eenews.net/articles/alligator-alcatraz-officially-shutters-after-weeks-of-wind-down-rumors/) (related headline only)
- Public records: Second Judicial Circuit Judge Angela Dempsey ordered release of FDEM records in a Friends of the Everglades suit, [Florida Trib, Mar 1, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/03/01/new-records-show-florida-officials-burned-more-than-1-2-million-per-day-on-alligator-alcatraz/)
- A DHS inspector general report on Alligator Alcatraz conditions was released Sept 11, 2026, [WUSF, Sept 17, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-17/fdem-spent-about-1-billion-immigration-last-year-now-itll-get-more)

##### Inferences
- The $417M figure (June 30) and the $603M figure (June 17) are not in conflict: the Phoenix number is total unpaid across contracts including amounts not yet invoiced; the Trib/FDEM number is pending invoices. Vendor owed figures reported Sept 30 ($68M IRG, $40M CDR, $40M GEO|CDR, $35M CRS, $27.3M Lemoine CDR) sum to about $210M, roughly half the $417M, with Doodie Calls likely the largest remaining creditor.
- Because the facility is dismantled and the FEMA grant cannot pay construction costs, a large part of the Alligator Alcatraz bill will fall on state general revenue regardless of who is governor; the $187M approved Sept 11 is the first installment.
- Deportation Depot's $101M cap with only $32M drawn by Sept 22, 2026 means the GEO|CDR Baker contract has roughly $69M of remaining headroom entering the next governor's term.

##### Gaps
- Could not read the Tampa Bay Times July 3, 2025 contractor list or the Miami Herald "links to allegations of fraud, price-gouging" piece (fetch blocked).
- No readable source gave per vendor paid vs owed for Gothams, Longview, SLSCO, Garner, GardaWorld as of fall 2026.
- No readable report confirmed whether "Panhandle Pokey" ever moved past the concept stage.
- Current status of the environmental case after the April 30, 2026 remand (and whether it is moot after the closure) was not found.

#### Key question 2: Each vendor: ownership, payments, prior Florida contracts, giving to DeSantis, Collins, Uthmeier, legislative leaders and RPOF

##### Takeaway
The Alligator Alcatraz vendor base is dominated by DeSantis era emergency contractors, above all Carlos Duart's CDR companies (about $1.9M to DeSantis and RPOF, $500K to a DeSantis committee, $275K to Uthmeier, $250K to Collins, $620K plus to Donalds) and the Canadian owned ARS/IRG group ($99,980 to Friends of Ron DeSantis in 2022, $100K to RPOF in Oct 2025, $100K plus to Donalds). Giving to Uthmeier clusters around contract dates; the Oct 22, 2026 Florida Trib piece is the most current accounting.

##### Cited Findings

CDR Maguire / CDR Health / Lemoine CDR Logistics / GEO|CDR Baker / Carlos Duart
- CDR Companies CEO Carlos Duart; co-CEO Tina Vidal-Duart; CDR has long been a go-to state vendor for engineering, emergency management and health care; Duart confirmed involvement but cited an NDA; "we get chosen because we do exceptional work", [NBC Miami/AP, July 3, 2025](https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/local/republican-donors-and-floridas-hurricane-know-how-helped-build-alligator-alcatraz-so-quickly/3651580/)
- Duart and Vidal-Duart gave about $1.9M to DeSantis and RPOF, [Unicorn Riot, Feb 6, 2026](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)
- CDR Enterprises: $25,000 (Mar 11, 2025) and $250,000 (Sept 17, 2025) to Friends of James Uthmeier, total $275,000, its second largest contribution this cycle; the largest was $500,000 to a DeSantis affiliated committee; the $250,000 came about two weeks after CDR's $100M Deportation Depot staffing contract in early September 2025; Duart personally gave Uthmeier $3,000 on Mar 11, 2025; CDR Maguire gave RPOF a $3,200 in-kind donation, [Florida Trib, Oct 22, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/10/22/from-themed-merch-to-donor-checks-how-uthmeier-profited-from-alligator-alcatraz/)
- Duart, CDR Companies, CDR Enterprises Inc. and CDR Maguire Companies gave more than $620,000 combined to Friends of Byron Donalds; Duart via spokesperson: "Florida is our home, and we've always believed that being part of a community means being engaged in its civic life.", [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)
- CDR Health paid $5,184,400 from the Emergency Preparedness and Response Fund for "illegal migration" work; CDR still owed more than $40M; GEO|CDR Baker owed nearly $40M; Lemoine CDR Logistics owed about $27.3M, [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)
- CDR also gives locally: $70,000 to Miami-Dade Commissioner Oliver Gilbert and about $105,000 total to Miami-Dade incumbents, [Miami Herald via Daily Gazette](https://www.dailygazette.com/tribune/congressional-candidates-clash-over-donations-tied-to-alligator-alcatraz/article_639be44a-aef4-5ce8-b532-ba50d83cb703.html)
- Carlos Duart chairs the FIU board of trustees and his company is an Alligator Alcatraz contractor, [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/fiu-board-chairs-company-is-an-alligator-alcatraz-contractor-23574722/) (headline only)
- Longview Solutions Group LLC ($25.6M site prep contract) is owned by Roy E. Dunn, a former FDEM official and former CDR Maguire executive, [Unicorn Riot, Feb 6, 2026](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)

IRG Global Emergency Management / Access Restoration Services (ARS)
- ARS: owned by a Canadian family (founded Toronto 1959 by Antonio Gagliano, died Dec 2024; son Joseph Gagliano now heads it), Delaware registered, Texas based; IRG Global Inc. is an ARS subsidiary created in 2025, president Robbie Meek; related firms Echo 1 Emergency Logistics and Global Estimating Services led by ex ARS VP Nathan Normoyle; ARS partnered with Houston law firm McClenny, Moseley & Associates, under FBI investigation, [Florida Trident via WGCU, July 25, 2025](https://www.wgcu.org/top-story/2025-07-25/desantis-gave-company-accused-of-ripping-off-hurricane-ian-victims-200-million-in-emergency-state-business)
- Hurricane Ian allegations: ARS filed liens over $30M against 20 Lee County condo associations and sued in at least 17 cases; several countersued alleging fraud and excessive fees; Punta Rassa and Ocean Harbor still litigating over more than $25M; homeowner Donald Roedding alleged a $180,000 bill for work his insurer valued at about $35,000; ARS denies wrongdoing, same source
- Prior state business: about $200M in state contracts and purchase orders with little oversight or competitive bidding, most under the 2023 immigration emergency, including a tens of millions contract in May 2023, Haiti evacuation flights, Helene relief; Echo 1 about $9.7M including a $10M July 10, 2025 contract, same source
- Giving: ARS $99,980 to Friends of Ron DeSantis Nov 3, 2022 (about two weeks after a Punta Rassa contract); another $99,980 to RPOF about three months later; at least $170,000 more to GOP committees including $50,000 to the state party and $75,000 to Ashley Moody; IRG and Meek $6,000 to Uthmeier March 2025; ARS hired Ballard Partners Jan 2023, three of four listed lobbyists ex DeSantis administration, same source
- IRG $10,000 to RPOF June 24, 2025, same day as its $1.1M contract and the day after its $51M contract; ARS $100,000 to RPOF October 2025, [Miami Herald via Daily Gazette](https://www.dailygazette.com/tribune/alligator-alcatraz-firm-donated-to-florida-gop-before-getting-a-contract/article_604b80e5-9602-52f4-a3e1-92c4ff5dd05e.html); [Florida Trib, Oct 22, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/10/22/from-themed-merch-to-donor-checks-how-uthmeier-profited-from-alligator-alcatraz/)
- IRG owed more than $68M; ARS gave more than $100,000 to Donalds, [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)

The Lemoine Company
- Partner in Lemoine CDR Logistics LLC ($60M contract June 23, 2025; owed about $27.3M); gave $59,000 to Donalds, [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race); [Florida Trib, Oct 22, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/10/22/from-themed-merch-to-donor-checks-how-uthmeier-profited-from-alligator-alcatraz/)

Critical Response Strategies LLC (Jacksonville)
- $78.5M contract (state DFS records; some reports say $78.8M) to hire warden, camp managers, corrections officers and IT; Emergency Management Director Todd Smith, a Jacksonville Fire and Rescue lieutenant; Rep. Angie Nixon criticized the deal, [Action News Jax, July 16, 2025](https://www.actionnewsjax.com/news/investigates/action-news-jax-investigates-jacksonville-based-company-paid-788m-staff-alligator-alcatraz/XATPMXNW2ZEUVBXZ2RWD3HKTVY/)
- Described as having "obscure origins", [Florida Trib, July 16, 2025](https://floridatrib.org/2025/07/16/jacksonville-firm-with-78-million-alligator-alcatraz-contract-has-obscure-origins/); [Prison Legal News, Aug 1, 2025](https://www.prisonlegalnews.org/news/2025/aug/1/shady-firm-awarded-78-million-contract-services-floridas-alligator-alcatraz) (headlines only)
- About $26,000 in linked political donations, [Unicorn Riot, Feb 6, 2026](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)
- Owed more than $35M; gave $10,000 to Donalds, [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)

Garner Environmental Services
- $19.7M buildout contract (July 2025 list), $102M buildout (July 2025) and $49M Baker Correctional (Sept 2025); about $6M for four Boeing 777s (June 18, 2025); executive Jason Osborne gave Uthmeier $2,000 July 7, 2025, two days before the $102M contract; about $6,600 in linked donations overall, [NBC Miami, July 22, 2025](https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/local/florida-signs-245-million-in-contracts-for-alligator-alcatraz-heres-a-look-by-the-numbers/3663736/); [Florida Trib, Oct 22, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/10/22/from-themed-merch-to-donor-checks-how-uthmeier-profited-from-alligator-alcatraz/); [Unicorn Riot](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)

Gothams LLC
- $21.1M for IT, badges, wristbands, portable radios, detainee phone calls, RFID, credentialing; about $50,000 in linked donations, [NBC Miami, July 22, 2025](https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/local/florida-signs-245-million-in-contracts-for-alligator-alcatraz-heres-a-look-by-the-numbers/3663736/); [Unicorn Riot](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)
- Florida spent $34M on tech and IT at the Everglades lockup, [Florida Phoenix](https://floridaphoenix.com/?p=240075) (headline only)

SLSCO LTD
- $19.7M buildout and maintenance; also listed for vehicles, [NBC Miami, July 22, 2025](https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/local/florida-signs-245-million-in-contracts-for-alligator-alcatraz-heres-a-look-by-the-numbers/3663736/); [Unicorn Riot](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)

GardaWorld
- Canadian security firm; $78M promised; translators and barbers; about $5,000 linked donations; referred questions to FDEM, [Florida Phoenix, June 17, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/06/17/florida-still-owes-603-million-on-immigration-enforcement-contracts/); [Unicorn Riot](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)

Doodie Calls (St. Petersburg)
- At least $219M in contracts, $96M paid, $122M owed as of June 2026; more than $92M paid by March 2026; no political giving reported, [Florida Phoenix, June 17, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/06/17/florida-still-owes-603-million-on-immigration-enforcement-contracts/); [Florida Trib, Mar 1, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/03/01/new-records-show-florida-officials-burned-more-than-1-2-million-per-day-on-alligator-alcatraz/)

Other vendors in state lists
- Baker's Communications, CDW Government, ContainGo, Granny's Alliance Holdings ($3.3M meals), LTS Inc, Motorola Solutions, Meridian Rapid Defense Group, Public Safety Solutions MO, WeatherSTEM (about $3,000 donations), WILL-BURT, Williams Communications ($245,000 contract; $5,000 to Uthmeier Nov 2025), AT&T ($12,500 contract; $15,000 to Uthmeier), AC Disaster Consulting and FORTS Services LLC (nominal gifts to Donalds), Innovative Emergency Management (Madhu Beriwal, gives to both parties), DBT Transportation ($64,000, about $1,500 overdue), [Unicorn Riot](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/); [Florida Trib, Oct 22, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/10/22/from-themed-merch-to-donor-checks-how-uthmeier-profited-from-alligator-alcatraz/); [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race); [Florida Phoenix, June 17, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/06/17/florida-still-owes-603-million-on-immigration-enforcement-contracts/)
- Unicorn Riot tallied about $2.24M in donations from people and entities linked to nine Alligator Alcatraz companies, [Unicorn Riot](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)
- Uthmeier's campaign sells Alligator Alcatraz merch ($30 shirts, $27 hats, $8 stickers); alligatoralcatraz.com redirects to his store; RPOF gave Friends of James Uthmeier $500,000 over the period, [Florida Trib, Oct 22, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/10/22/from-themed-merch-to-donor-checks-how-uthmeier-profited-from-alligator-alcatraz/)

##### Inferences
- The same five firms that are owed the most by the state (IRG, CDR entities, GEO|CDR, CRS, Lemoine CDR) are the vendors that gave to Donalds, and none gave to Jolly, which is consistent with giving aimed at the candidate most likely to keep paying and renewing.
- CDR's cycle giving (about $1.9M DeSantis/RPOF historical, $500K DeSantis committee, $275K Uthmeier, $250K Collins, $620K plus Donalds) covers every plausible successor to DeSantis, so CDR is hedged across the GOP field rather than betting on Donalds alone.

##### Gaps
- Could not confirm giving by Gothams, Longview, SLSCO, Garner, GardaWorld to Collins, legislative leaders (Albritton, Perez) or to the Florida House/Senate GOP committees; no readable source addressed it.
- Round one's $250K CDR Enterprises to Collins could not be independently re-sourced from a readable page this run.
- No readable source identified Gothams LLC or SLSCO ownership.

#### Key question 3: GEO Group: Florida facilities, ICE contracts, Boca Raton HQ, 2025 to 2026 revenue, lobbying, giving to Trump and Florida officials, Donalds bills

##### Takeaway
GEO (Boca Raton) is in a federal detention boom: 2025 revenue $2.6B, Q2 2026 revenue $732.1M up 15%, FY2026 guidance raised to $2.95B to $3.05B, with ICE 48% of revenue, and it gave $1.4M to MAGA Inc. in July 2026 within days of two new ICE contract awards. In Florida it runs Broward Transitional Center for ICE, South Bay prison, and is slated to take Graceville and Bay prisons July 1, 2027 (about $100M a year); in Tallahassee it is the joint venture partner on Deportation Depot; in D.C. it spent about $690,000 in late 2024 lobbying on Donalds' bills.

##### Cited Findings
- Q2 2026 (Aug 6, 2026 8-K): revenue $732.1M vs $636.2M; net income to GEO Operations $47.5M; FY2026 guidance revenue $2.95B to $3.05B, net income $168M to $175M, adjusted EBITDA $550M to $560M; new ICE contracts: Big Horn, Hudson CO (1,188 beds, about $85M first full year, effective July 9, 2026) and Rivers, Winton NC (1,320 beds, about $80M, effective Aug 1, 2026), activation by end of 2026 with ICE reimbursing capex and startup; Florida: Graceville (1,884 beds) and Bay (985 beds) managed only contracts, about $100M annual revenue combined, now expected to transition to GEO July 1, 2027, excluded from guidance; HQ Boca Raton, [GEO 8-K Ex. 99.1, Aug 6, 2026](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0000923796/000119312526336562/d118675dex991.htm)
- 2025 revenue $2.6B, up 8.6%; Q1 2026 revenue $705.2M, up 17%; ICE 48% of revenue per 2025 annual report; One Big Beautiful Bill Act (July 4, 2025) tripled ICE's budget with $45B for detention; up to $520M in new or expanded 2025 contracts, about $300M from four reactivated GEO owned facilities (about 6,000 beds in NJ, MI, GA), a California processing center and a renewal of Broward Transitional Center (Pompano Beach); also ICE transportation, electronic monitoring of about 48,000 people and skip tracing; 28 active federal contracts worth over $2.5B; founder and executive chairman George Zoley led more than $5M in giving since the 2024 cycle to Trump, Trump super PACs and GOP allies; $250,000 to a Jim Jordan linked hybrid PAC was never reported, prompting a Campaign Legal Center FEC complaint in late May 2026 alleging a federal contractor ban violation; Acting ICE Director David Venturella (named May 12, 2026) is ex GEO; Tom Homan reported as a paid GEO consultant; Human Rights Watch reported abusive conditions at Broward Transitional Center, [Florida Bulldog (Dan Christensen), July 16, 2026](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2026/07/geo-group-immigration-detention-windwall-follows-millions-pro-trump-political-spending/)
- GEO Reentry Services gave $1M to MAGA Inc. July 16, 2026, three days after the Hudson CO award, and $413,000 on July 31, 2026, two days after the Winton NC deal; total $1.4M, [Prison Legal News, Oct 1, 2026 (citing NYT)](https://www.prisonlegalnews.org/news/2026/oct/1/geo-group-funnels-millions-to-trump-pac-following-windfall-ice-contracts/)
- GEO federal lobbying: nearly $2M in 2024 on DHS and lawmakers including Donalds; $340,000 July to Sept 2024 and $350,000 Oct to Dec 2024 lobbying on Donalds' bills; $690,000 earlier in 2024 on H.R. 2; Donalds co-sponsored the Secure the Border Act of 2023 (H.R. 2) and was primary sponsor of the Reshape Alternatives to Detention Act of 2024 (H.R. 8430); neither became law, [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race); bill text [Congress.gov H.R. 8430](https://www.congress.gov/118/bills/hr8430/BILLS-118hr8430ih.htm); Donalds' one pager [donalds.house.gov](https://donalds.house.gov/uploadedfiles/reshape_atd_act.pdf)
- GEO Florida giving this cycle: $2.5M to Donalds (plus $1M more in the Oct 3 to 9 week per round one and [Jason Garcia on X](https://x.com/Jason_Garcia/status/2098519834094932352)); $50,000 to Friends of James Uthmeier; $15,000 to Friends of Blaise Ingoglia (CFO candidate), [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)
- GEO gave about $146,500 in donations linked by Unicorn Riot's review; GEO-CDR BAKER authorized persons include Zoley and GEO CEO James David Donahue, [Unicorn Riot, Feb 6, 2026](https://unicornriot.ninja/2026/new-documents-reveal-florida-paid-over-573-million-to-desantis-connected-immigrant-detention-contractors-in-2025/)
- Historical DeSantis route: GEO gave more than $740,000 to the Republican Governors Association in 2022, including $150,000 in mid August 2022 less than two weeks before the DeSantis administration renewed GEO's contract for the 1,948 bed South Bay Correctional and Rehabilitation Facility (Palm Beach County), on which GEO has collected more than $320M since 2009; GEO made more than $110M from Florida contracts in 2021; the RGA was DeSantis's largest 2022 donor at more than $14M, [Seeking Rents (Jason Garcia), 2022](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/how-private-prisons-backdoor-big)
- GEO gave $2,500 in 2024 and $2,500 in 2026 to state Sen. Shevrin Jones's PAC, which says it will return them, [Miami Herald via Daily Gazette](https://www.dailygazette.com/tribune/congressional-candidates-clash-over-donations-tied-to-alligator-alcatraz/article_639be44a-aef4-5ce8-b532-ba50d83cb703.html)
- Broader context: ICE and a Florida company inked a 1,320 bed North Carolina deal, [Yahoo/AP](https://www.yahoo.com/news/politics/articles/ice-florida-company-ink-deal-155300202.html) (headline; matches GEO's Rivers facility)

##### Inferences
- GEO's Florida stake under the next governor is concrete: the Graceville and Bay transitions (about $100M a year from July 1, 2027), the South Bay contract, and the GEO|CDR Baker contract at Deportation Depot (nearly $40M owed, about $69M of headroom). A governor who keeps Deportation Depot open, expands state detention beds and keeps Department of Corrections privatization is worth well over $100M a year to GEO.
- The 2024 lobbying on H.R. 8430 fits GEO's business: its BI Incorporated subsidiary is the dominant ICE electronic monitoring contractor and the bill concerned alternatives to detention, which is a GEO line of business (inference; the lobbying filings themselves were not read).

##### Gaps
- Could not open OpenSecrets or GEO's Q1 2026 8-K directly; the Q1 figures above come via Florida Bulldog.
- No readable source quantified GEO's 2025 to 2026 Tallahassee lobbying spend or named its state lobbyists.
- The content of GEO's 2024 lobbying reports (which specific Donalds provisions) was not available.

#### Key question 4: What Donalds has promised, and what the governor controls

##### Takeaway
Donalds has promised to remove local officials and state attorneys who adopt "sanctuary" policies and says Florida will "never" be a sanctuary state, and he frames enforcement as a nonnegotiable federal mandate; the governor controls FDEM (which runs the detention sites and emergency no bid procurement under a state of emergency the governor can keep extending), sits on and chairs the unanimous vote State Board of Immigration Enforcement that controls $250M in local grants and detention bed strategy, and under SB 2-C can suspend officials who do not comply.

##### Cited Findings
- Aug 2026, after a Tampa rally: Donalds said Florida is not and will never be a sanctuary state; local officials who create sanctuaries will be removed; state attorneys who do not enforce the law "are going to be removed from office, because it destabilizes law and order"; he would not hesitate to suspend them, [Florida Phoenix (Mitch Perry), Aug 12, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/08/12/byron-donalds-says-hell-remove-local-officials-who-indulge-in-sanctuary-policies/)
- Oct 13, 2026 Jacksonville business roundtable: Donalds said a governor must follow federal law, "States don't set immigration policy. The federal government does," rejected the idea governors choose how enforcement is carried out, and was unsympathetic to a homebuilder's complaint about ICE worksite raids, [Florida Phoenix, Oct 14, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/10/14/byron-donalds-says-states-must-work-with-ice-but-a-dozen-of-them-arent/)
- SB 2-C (approved Feb 13, 2025; Senate 27-10, House 85-30): creates the State Board of Immigration Enforcement inside FDLE with the Governor and Cabinet as agency head acting by unanimous vote, designated "chief immigration enforcement officer"; a Local Law Enforcement Immigration Grant Program with $250M nonrecurring GR for reimbursements and bonus payments; an eight member council of police chiefs and sheriffs that recommends grant funding and "detention bed strategies" and must recommend strategies to increase ICE detention beds; a report on vacant state and county beds that could be sublet to ICE; mandatory detainer compliance; and executive or administrative officers who violate the chapter may face Governor action including suspension, [Florida Senate bill summary, SB 2-C](https://www.flsenate.gov/Committees/BillSummaries/2025C/Html/2)
- A 2025 state law requires every county detention facility operator (sheriff) to enter 287(g) agreements; municipalities are not required; AG Uthmeier has threatened removal of officials in Fort Myers, Key West, Tampa and Orlando; South Miami has sued over whether cities must participate, [Florida Phoenix, Aug 12, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/08/12/byron-donalds-says-hell-remove-local-officials-who-indulge-in-sanctuary-policies/); [Florida Phoenix, Oct 14, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/10/14/byron-donalds-says-states-must-work-with-ice-but-a-dozen-of-them-arent/)
- FDEM "sits under Gov. Ron DeSantis' executive office" and runs the state's immigration detention facilities, [WLRN, Oct 15, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/immigration/2026-10-15/state-mistakenly-releases-and-recovers-two-detainees-from-deportation-depot)
- FDEM director Jared Perdue is also transportation secretary; the emergency fund was re-established by the Legislature on condition of a report FDEM delivered two months late, [Florida Phoenix, Sept 11, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/09/11/desantis-administration-misses-legislative-deadline-gets-more-money-for-immigration-anyway/)
- FDEM projects $500M for Operation Vigilant Sentry in FY 2026-27, [WUSF, Sept 17, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-17/fdem-spent-about-1-billion-immigration-last-year-now-itll-get-more)
- Kevin Guthrie (then FDEM director) on why the state could build so fast: "We understand how to act fast without bureaucracy in the face of any emergency", [NBC Miami/AP, July 3, 2025](https://www.nbcmiami.com/news/local/republican-donors-and-floridas-hurricane-know-how-helped-build-alligator-alcatraz-so-quickly/3651580/)

##### Inferences
- The specific levers a Governor Donalds would hold: (1) whether to keep extending the Jan 2023 immigration emergency, which is the legal basis for FDEM's no bid purchase orders; (2) who runs FDEM and thus which vendors get paid first from the $417M backlog and the $187M just appropriated; (3) whether to renew or expand GEO|CDR Baker at Deportation Depot or reopen a third site; (4) the Board of Immigration Enforcement's $250M grant program and detention bed strategy; (5) the FY 2027-28 budget request for Operation Vigilant Sentry; (6) suspension power over noncompliant local officials.
- Donalds' statements found in this run do not mention E-Verify, the Florida State Guard, or a plan to expand state run detention. His promises are about removing officials and total cooperation with ICE. Anything about new facilities under Donalds is inference, not a documented pledge.

##### Gaps
- No readable source captured a Donalds position on E-Verify, the State Guard, Deportation Depot's future, or paying the vendor backlog. His campaign had no quote in the WUSF Sept 30 piece.
- The Florida Senate summary does not specify the 287(g) mandate text (that is in a companion bill); I relied on the Phoenix description.

#### Key question 5: What Jolly has said, and watchdog or press analysis of who profits under a Donalds administration

##### Takeaway
Jolly has said he would end the state mandate requiring counties to join 287(g); no vendor with outstanding invoices has donated to him. The most direct "who profits" analysis is the Sept 30, 2026 WUSF/Your Florida piece (vendors owed $417M gave $3.3M plus to Donalds and nothing to Jolly) and the Oct 22, 2026 Florida Trib piece on Uthmeier; I found no Miami Herald or Tampa Bay Times piece in this run specifically modeling a Donalds administration.

##### Cited Findings
- Jolly said that, if elected, he would end the state mandate requiring counties to participate in 287(g) agreements with ICE, [Florida Phoenix, Oct 14, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/10/14/byron-donalds-says-states-must-work-with-ice-but-a-dozen-of-them-arent/)
- Jolly discussed Alligator Alcatraz in an Aug 3, 2025 CBS Miami interview (video only; no transcript available), [CBS Miami, Aug 3, 2025](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/democratic-candidate-governor-david-jolly-abortion-rights-alligator-alcatraz)
- No vendor with outstanding invoices has donated to Jolly; Jolly raised about $7M for Florida 2026 vs more than $115M for Friends of Byron Donalds, [WUSF, Sept 30, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)
- Jason Garcia (Seeking Rents) flagged GEO's additional $1M: "GEO has now donated more than $2.5 million to Donalds since he launched his campaign", [Jason Garcia on X](https://x.com/Jason_Garcia/status/2098519834094932352)
- Karen Woodall (Florida Center for Fiscal and Economic Policy) on the lack of legislative oversight of FDEM's immigration spending, [WUSF, Sept 14, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/local-state/2026-09-14/this-state-agency-owes-millions-in-immigration-bills-florida-lawmakers-just-cut-a-check)
- Precedent: in 2018 all four Democratic gubernatorial candidates signed Dream Defenders' "Freedom Pledge" to refuse private prison money, [Miami New Times, 2018](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/floridas-four-democratic-governor-candidates-promise-not-to-take-private-prison-donations-10435029)

##### Inferences
- The partisan split in vendor giving is total: every detention vendor in the state's unpaid list that gave in the governor's race gave to Donalds. Combined with Jolly's pledge to end the 287(g) mandate, the industry's financial interest in the outcome is explicit.

##### Gaps
- No Jolly statement found specifically on GEO's $2.5M to $3.5M, on Deportation Depot, or on whether he would pay the vendor backlog; searches surfaced nothing readable.
- No Miami Herald, Tampa Bay Times or Orlando Sentinel piece specifically analyzing a Donalds administration's detention plans was found; the WUSF and Trib pieces are the closest.

#### Key question 6: Dollar stakes and timelines

##### Takeaway
Through Oct 22, 2026 the measurable stakes are: $417M plus owed by FDEM (592 invoices as of June 30), $187M of the Sept 11 appropriation earmarked to start paying it, $523M FDEM says Washington still owes, $500M FDEM plans to spend on immigration in FY 2026-27, the $101M GEO|CDR Baker contract ($32M drawn), and GEO's $100M a year Graceville and Bay takeover on July 1, 2027.

##### Cited Findings
- $608M FEMA award effective Sept 30, 2025; $84.5M received by mid September 2026; Perdue claims $523M still owed by the federal government, [WUSF, Sept 14, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/local-state/2026-09-14/this-state-agency-owes-millions-in-immigration-bills-florida-lawmakers-just-cut-a-check); [Florida Trib, Mar 1, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/03/01/new-records-show-florida-officials-burned-more-than-1-2-million-per-day-on-alligator-alcatraz/)
- FEMA application was $1.49B; FDEM September 2025 projection $1.7B for two facilities, [Florida Trib, Mar 1, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/03/01/new-records-show-florida-officials-burned-more-than-1-2-million-per-day-on-alligator-alcatraz/); [Florida Phoenix, June 17, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/06/17/florida-still-owes-603-million-on-immigration-enforcement-contracts/)
- Sept 11, 2026: $250M spending authority, about $187M for immigration invoices; FY 2026-27 immigration estimate $500M; worst case $1.5B fund deficit by end of June 2027, [WUSF, Sept 17, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-17/fdem-spent-about-1-billion-immigration-last-year-now-itll-get-more); [Florida Phoenix, Sept 11, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/09/11/desantis-administration-misses-legislative-deadline-gets-more-money-for-immigration-anyway/)
- GEO|CDR Baker: $101M cap, $32M paid as of Sept 22, 2026, [WLRN, Oct 15, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/immigration/2026-10-15/state-mistakenly-releases-and-recovers-two-detainees-from-deportation-depot)
- GEO Graceville and Bay: about $100M annual revenue, transition July 1, 2027, [GEO 8-K, Aug 6, 2026](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0000923796/000119312526336562/d118675dex991.htm)
- 11th Circuit remand April 30, 2026; ARS Hurricane Ian condo cases (Punta Rassa, Ocean Harbor, more than $25M) expected at trial in late 2025 per the July 2025 report; CLC FEC complaint against GEO filed late May 2026, [Mealey's](https://www.mealeys.com/mealeys/mealeys-pollution-liability/articles/2472322/split-11th-circuit-revives-alligator-alcatraz-says-apa-nepa-claims-won-t-stick); [Florida Trident via WGCU](https://www.wgcu.org/top-story/2025-07-25/desantis-gave-company-accused-of-ripping-off-hurricane-ian-victims-200-million-in-emergency-state-business); [Florida Bulldog](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2026/07/geo-group-immigration-detention-windwall-follows-millions-pro-trump-political-spending/)

##### Inferences
- The next governor takes office Jan 2027 with roughly $230M of the June 30 backlog still unfunded (417 minus 187), a $500M FY 2026-27 immigration line, and the FY 2027-28 budget to write; the FEMA balance (about $523M) is the single largest variable and depends on the Trump administration, which Donalds is aligned with.

##### Gaps
- No readable source gave the Deportation Depot contract's end date or renewal terms.
- Outcome of the ARS condo trials and the CLC complaint not found.

### Note: gambling_second_hop.md

_Folder: Donalds second hop who gains_

### Gambling and Prediction Market Money Behind Byron Donalds: Second Hop (Who Gains, What the Governor Controls)

Research notes compiled Oct 22, 2026. Tool constraints: the WebFetch quota was exhausted for part of the session and the WebSearch budget ran out before every thread could be closed, so several items (notably Steve Wynn's 2026 Florida interests, the Seminole lobbyist roster, Poarch Band 2026 activity, and the Oct 2, 2026 Tampa Bay Times article body) are reported from secondary summaries or flagged as gaps. Tampa Bay Times pages returned only headlines and metadata, not article bodies.

Round-one figures (treated as established per assignment): Seminole Tribe $5.5M to Friends of Byron Donalds by Sept 18, 2026; Steve Wynn $1M; FanDuel $581K; DraftKings $525K; Sports Betting Alliance $250K; Caesars $245K; PrizePicks $100K; Poarch Band $100K; Robinhood $200K; Kalshi $75K.

#### 1. The 2021 Seminole gaming compact: terms, revenue share, exclusivity, Hard Rock Bet, reopeners, and what the governor controls

##### Takeaway
The compact runs to July 31, 2051, gives the Tribe exclusivity over slots, craps, roulette, banked card games, fantasy contests and statewide mobile sports betting via a hub-and-spoke server model, and guaranteed the state at least $2.5B over its first five years; actual payments were $817.3M in FY2024-25 and are forecast at $903M in FY2025-26 rising past $1B by FY2028-29. The governor's formal levers are executing or amending the compact (subject to legislative ratification), appointing all five Florida Gaming Control Commission members (Senate-confirmed, four-year terms), and the posture of the executive branch and AG toward enforcement; the compact's only built-in reopener was a 36-month good-faith negotiation window on full online casino gaming that has already lapsed without action.

##### Cited Findings
- Compact executed by Gov. DeSantis and the Tribe April 23, 2021, amended May 17, 2021, ratified by the Legislature; term runs through July 31, 2051 (21 years beyond the 2010 compact's July 31, 2030 end). Land-based sports betting revenue share is 13.75 percent of net win., [TribalGaming.com Florida deep dive](https://tribalgaming.com/news/florida-tribal-gaming-market-deep-dive-2026/); [Florida Senate President's Summary of 2021 Compact](https://www.flsenate.gov/PublishedContent/Offices/2020-2022/President/Documents/Summaryof2021Compact.pdf)
- Revenue share tiers: slots and new games 12 percent ($0 to 2B net win) rising to 25 percent above $3.5B; table games 15 percent rising to 25 percent above $2B; sports betting 13.75 percent of net win, or 10 percent on net win from patrons using a pari-mutuel partner's brand. "Guaranteed Minimum Compact Term Payment of $2.5B" covering all revenue share in the first five years., [Senate Summary of 2021 Compact](https://www.flsenate.gov/PublishedContent/Offices/2020-2022/President/Documents/Summaryof2021Compact.pdf)
- Exclusivity covers slots, craps, roulette, banking card games, fantasy sports contests and sports betting at Tribal facilities and, for sports betting and fantasy, to players anywhere in the state via mobile or electronic devices, with wagers "deemed conducted where the servers are located on Indian lands." Exceptions: slots at eight pari-mutuels in Broward and Miami-Dade, pari-mutuel wagering, certain cardroom poker including limited designated player games, lottery, bingo, amusement games, and fantasy contests under the state fantasy act., [Senate Summary](https://www.flsenate.gov/PublishedContent/Offices/2020-2022/President/Documents/Summaryof2021Compact.pdf)
- Pari-mutuel partnership requirement: the Tribe's sportsbooks must contract with any willing, qualified pari-mutuel permitholder for marketing; the permitholder gets not less than 60 percent of profit from its branded patrons., [Senate Summary](https://www.flsenate.gov/PublishedContent/Offices/2020-2022/President/Documents/Summaryof2021Compact.pdf); Hard Rock was working with 16 of 32 pari-mutuels per a Dec 2023 report, partners get 60 percent and Tribe keeps 40 percent, [Legal Sports Report, Apr 25, 2024](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/?p=178605)
- Reopener: parties agree to "engage in good faith negotiations within 36 months after the Effective Date" to consider amending the compact to allow online or mobile wagering on all Covered Games (i.e., iGaming). A dispute over good faith "is not subject to suit nor a waiver of the State's sovereign immunity.", [Senate Summary](https://www.flsenate.gov/PublishedContent/Offices/2020-2022/President/Documents/Summaryof2021Compact.pdf)
- The state's revenue estimating conference lists the 2021 compact's effective date as 9/1/2021., [EDR Indian Gaming conference, Aug 4, 2025 (PDF)](https://edr.state.fl.us/Content/conferences/Indian-gaming/archives/250804IndianGaming.pdf)
- Compact "bars the tribe from interfering with a state gambling license for a facility more than 15 miles 'in a straight line' from the Seminole Hard Rock Hotel & Casino Hollywood"; Trump's Doral resort is slightly more than 15 miles away; Sen. Jeff Brandes said the buffer seemed aimed at Doral and the Fontainebleau. Senate President Wilton Simpson authored the 2021 legislative package., [SoloAzar, May 20, 2021](https://soloazar.com/en/category/casino/florida-gop-eases-path-for-trump-to-pursue-casino-license)
- Compact up to three additional facilities on the Hollywood Reservation; annual oversight assessment rises $150,000 per facility; $250,000 per operational facility to the Florida Council on Compulsive Gaming., [Senate Summary](https://www.flsenate.gov/PublishedContent/Offices/2020-2022/President/Documents/Summaryof2021Compact.pdf)
- Actual and forecast Seminole revenue share payments to the state ($ millions): FY2023-24 $357.0 (actual); FY2024-25 $817.3 (actual); FY2025-26 $903.3 (forecast); FY2026-27 $941.1; FY2027-28 $989.7; FY2028-29 $1,040.7; FY2029-30 $1,093.2; FY2030-31 $1,147.8. Forecast period totals about $6.1B. The document explicitly does not address minimum guarantee calculations., [EDR Indian Gaming conference, Aug 4, 2025](https://edr.state.fl.us/Content/conferences/Indian-gaming/archives/250804IndianGaming.pdf)
- Tribe-hosted sports betting net win in the EDR table is small ($2.7M in 2024-25 with 10 percent share), which indicates the bulk of Hard Rock Bet net win is booked under other categories; the EDR notes sports betting is "running stronger than the prior forecast.", [EDR Aug 4, 2025](https://edr.state.fl.us/Content/conferences/Indian-gaming/archives/250804IndianGaming.pdf)
- Popular shorthand used by industry press: state guaranteed "at least $500M per year," "$2.5B over five years," and "near $20B over the agreement's life.", [Bettors Insider, Aug 12, 2026](https://bettorsinsider.com/news/2026/08/12/gambling-industry-pours-12-2m-into-floridas-2026-midterms-as-seminole-tribe-leads-spending/); [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
- Hard Rock Bet metrics (TribalGaming.com, no external data source cited): 2026 projected handle roughly $4.2B; Q1 2026 handle about $1.2B; mobile share about 92 percent; Tribe transfers to the state "more than $400 million" by mid-2025; projected flow to the state through 2027 roughly $1.4B; Hard Rock Bet mobile licensed in 8 states as of late May 2026., [TribalGaming.com, July 10, 2026](https://tribalgaming.com/news/seminole-hard-rock-bet-2026-handle-mobile-dominance/)
- Legal history: U.S. District Judge Dabney Friedrich vacated the compact in Nov 2021; D.C. Circuit reversed June 2023; Hard Rock Bet resumed Nov 7, 2023; Supreme Court declined review June 2024; Florida Supreme Court rejected a separate state challenge March 2024 on procedural grounds; West Flagler later settled and agreed to offer jai alai wagering through Hard Rock Bet., [Legal Sports Report, Oct 8, 2026](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/281066/draftkings-sued-by-seminole-tribe-over-sports-predictions-in-florida/); [CasinoBeats, Nov 6, 2025](https://casinobeats.com/2025/11/06/florida-judge-dismisses-protect-the-constitution-lawsuit-seminole-sports-betting/)
- Governor's statutory role: s. 285.710 shows the Governor executing compacts (2010 on April 7, 2010; 2021 on April 23, 2021), with compacts effective only after Legislature ratifies; the Governor must cooperate with the Tribe in seeking Interior approval; if the 2021 compact were invalidated, the 2010 compact remains in effect. No general amendment procedure is specified., [Fla. Stat. 285.710 via FindLaw](https://codes.findlaw.com/fl/title-xix-public-business/fl-st-sect-285-710/)
- Florida Gaming Control Commission (s. 16.71): five members appointed by the Governor, subject to Senate confirmation; four-year terms, 12-year cap; must include one member with 10 years law enforcement experience, one CPA with 10 years, one attorney with 10 years; Governor holds constitutional suspension and removal power and must remove anyone convicted of a gambling, dishonesty, theft or fraud misdemeanor; Governor may not solicit candidate recommendations from persons tied to a tribe's gaming operation holding an active compact., [Fla. Stat. 16.71 (2025)](https://www.flsenate.gov/Laws/Statutes/2025/16.71)
- FGCC has "regulatory oversight of all gambling in Florida, including gaming compacts executed by the state," reviews the Tribe's sports betting rules; first appointees Julie Brown (chair), Michael Yaworsky, Charles Drago, effective Jan 1, 2022; salary $131,136. The commission was created in May 2021 to win House support for the compact., [The Capitolist, Dec 23, 2021](https://thecapitolist.com/julie-brown-will-lead-new-florida-gaming-commission/)
- Precedent for exclusivity leverage: under the 2010 compact, Judge Robert Hinkle ruled the state breached banked-game exclusivity by allowing pari-mutuel "designated player" games, letting the Tribe keep offering blackjack for the compact term without sharing that revenue; the Tribe kept paying $19.5M per month into a reserve the state could not spend; TaxWatch estimated nearly $250M lost and up to $2B more over seven years., [Florida TaxWatch](https://www.floridataxwatch.org/lack-of-a-seminole-gaming-compact-proving-very-costly-to-florida/)
- Hard Rock CEO Jim Allen (Apr 2024) said Hard Rock has "never" ruled out working with other operators and named Caesars, BetMGM, DraftKings and FanDuel as possibilities, but called any revenue share framework "premature." In 2024 Allen said the Tribe would need to reopen talks with state leaders on online casino gaming and "the polling suggests" Floridians want it., [Legal Sports Report, Apr 25, 2024](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/?p=178605); [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)

##### Inferences
- With an effective date of Sept 1, 2021 (EDR) or deemed approval in Aug 2021, the 36-month good-faith iGaming negotiation window closed around Aug or Sept 2024 with no amendment. Any iGaming expansion for the Tribe therefore needs a fresh compact amendment negotiated by the governor and ratified by the Legislature, and likely a fight over whether Amendment 3 applies. This is the single biggest thing a Donalds administration could give the Tribe.
- The governor's practical control over the compact is (a) whether to open amendment talks at all, (b) who sits on the FGCC, (c) whether the executive branch treats prediction markets and sweepstakes as violations of the Tribe's exclusivity, which in turn affects whether the Tribe can claim a breach and reduce payments as it did in 2016 to 2019.
- The EDR's sports betting line is tiny relative to Hard Rock Bet's reported $4B plus handle, so the public tables do not isolate how much of the roughly $900M annual payment is sports betting; that split is a gap.

##### Gaps
- Exact compact text on reduction or cessation of revenue share if the state authorizes competing games (Part XII type provisions) was not retrieved; the Senate summary said it does not specify triggers. The 2010 precedent is the best available illustration.
- Hard Rock Bet gross revenue and official handle: the Tribe does not publish; TribalGaming.com figures are unattributed.
- Who within the governor's office negotiates compacts in practice (2021 was DeSantis's office with Simpson's Senate) and whether the Legislature's 2026 leadership (Senate President Ben Albritton, House Speaker Daniel Perez) has taken a position on reopening was not found.

#### 2. The prediction markets fight in Florida: Kalshi, Robinhood, Crypto.com, Polymarket, DraftKings; Seminole and state actions; CFTC posture; what a governor can do

##### Takeaway
Through Oct 22, 2026, no Florida state agency has acted against prediction markets: Kalshi remains available in Florida, AG Uthmeier did not join the multistate AG briefs against Kalshi, and DeSantis only raised the question publicly in Feb 2026. The Seminole Tribe moved on its own on Oct 8, 2026, suing DraftKings, CEO Jason Robins and GUS III LLC in Broward Circuit Court over DraftKings Predictions and Pick6, which is the Tribe's first direct Florida enforcement of its sports betting exclusivity against a prediction market product; Kalshi, Robinhood, Crypto.com and Polymarket have not been sued in Florida as of the sources found.

##### Cited Findings
- Seminole Tribe v. DraftKings: 72-page complaint filed Thursday (Oct 8, 2026) in Broward County Circuit Court against DraftKings, CEO Jason Robins, and GUS III LLC (operator of DraftKings Predictions); seeks injunction and disgorgement of all Florida revenue and profits; includes a civil racketeering claim against Robins personally, citing his roughly 89 percent voting control. Alleges moneylines, spreads, totals, player props and parlays offered in Florida through the DraftKings Sports & Casino app; college player props barred by the compact; contracts offered to 18-year-olds while Florida's betting age is 21; on Aug 28, 2026 DraftKings replaced American odds with cent-denominated prices for Florida users. Complaint quotes: "That DraftKings Predictions may run in the background does not transform sports wagers into 'commodities trading'" and "DraftKings changed the label on the price. It did not change the price, the product, or the transaction.", [Legal Sports Report, Oct 8, 2026](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/281066/draftkings-sued-by-seminole-tribe-over-sports-predictions-in-florida/); also [Covers, Oct 8, 2026](https://www.covers.com/industry/seminole-tribe-sues-draftkings-florida-sports-betting-oct-8-2026); [SBC Americas, Oct 8, 2026](https://sbcamericas.com/2026/10/08/draftkings-sued-seminole-tribe-florida/)
- The complaint also alleges DraftKings secretly financed the West Flagler challenge to the compact and formed and funded Protect the Constitution LLC, which sued in Leon County in April 2025 arguing mobile sports betting is "casino gambling" requiring voter approval under Amendment 3; Judge Jonathan Sjostrom dismissed for lack of standing Nov 6, 2025 without reaching the merits; DraftKings joined an amended complaint Sept 17, 2026 as the LLC's sole member; case unresolved. AG Uthmeier's office urged dismissal in June 2025, arguing the compact is consistent with Amendment 3 because bets are deemed to occur on tribal land., [Legal Sports Report, Oct 8, 2026](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/281066/draftkings-sued-by-seminole-tribe-over-sports-predictions-in-florida/); [CasinoBeats, Nov 6, 2025](https://casinobeats.com/2025/11/06/florida-judge-dismisses-protect-the-constitution-lawsuit-seminole-sports-betting/)
- DraftKings entered prediction markets Dec 2025 offering sports contracts in Florida, California and Texas; launched Pick6 in Florida Jan 2026; unified Sports & Casino app nationwide June 2026; said integrating Predictions cut customer acquisition cost more than 80 percent; plans $200M to $300M investment in 2026; cites avoiding state sportsbook taxes up to 51 percent; its conventional sportsbook and iGaming are unavailable in Florida., [Legal Sports Report, Oct 8, 2026](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/281066/draftkings-sued-by-seminole-tribe-over-sports-predictions-in-florida/)
- DeSantis on CNBC Squawk Box (reported Feb 23 to 25, 2026): "In Florida, we kind of have a unique relationship with the Seminole Tribe of Florida." "They also have the exclusive rights over sports betting." "And so the question is: is something like Kalshi clashing with the laws of the state of Florida? Or is it separate? It's more of a prediction market." "This is something (that) really within the last year has really become a big deal.", [Sportsbook Review, Feb 25, 2026](https://www.sportsbookreview.com/news/florida-governor-desantis-questions-kalshi-feb-25-2026/); [CDC Gaming republishing Florida Politics (A.G. Gancarski), Feb 23, 2026](https://cdcgaming.com/brief/florida-governor-raises-questions-about-kalshi-crowding-seminole-tribe-gambling-rights/)
- Kalshi status: "legal in Florida as of October 2026," no Florida state action blocking it; basis is CFTC Designated Contract Market status granted 2020. HB 189 (internet gambling, sports wagering, fantasy) did not become law in the 2026 session., [Covers Florida Kalshi page, updated Oct 21, 2026](https://www.covers.com/betting/prediction-sites/usa/florida-kalshi)
- Florida "has so far avoided challenging the sector," and "the contributions do not establish how Donalds would approach prediction markets as governor." Prediction market operators say they are federally regulated under the Commodity Exchange Act; DraftKings, Underdog and Robinhood run in-house prediction platforms; FanDuel and PrizePicks offer contracts through third-party exchanges; none hold a Florida gaming license or participate in the compact. Donald Trump Jr. is a paid Kalshi adviser and a Polymarket investor., [Gambling Insider, Aug 12, 2026](https://www.gamblinginsider.com/news/185823/gambling-prediction-markets-florida-elections-byron-donalds)
- Florida is one of six states that did not sign the attorneys general letter asserting state control over legal gambling; Uthmeier "has taken a passive approach on prediction markets but has defended the tribe's compact and pursued challengers to its gaming operations." The Lines frames the next governor's choices as: oppose President Trump's position, wait until Trump leaves office in 2029, or stay out and let the Supreme Court decide., [The Lines, Aug 15, 2026](https://www.thelines.com/legal-betting/seminole-tribe-spending-to-protect-florida-gambling-interests/)
- Unlike New Jersey, Nevada and New York, "Florida state officials have not yet taken a public position on prediction markets." Attorney Daniel Wallach: "The next battleground is prediction markets and online gaming"; more prediction market activity could reduce Seminole sports betting revenue to the state., [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
- Kalshi's Florida giving: Gary Fineout (Politico) tweet summarized in search result: Kalshi "has donated more than $400k in campaign donations in Fla primarily to GOP including $50k to Uthmeier PC & $150k to Fla Leg. leadership funds & 75k to Byron Donalds PC." (Tweet body could not be fetched; figure is from the search snippet.), [Gary Fineout on X](https://x.com/fineout/status/2112529622047105389). Earlier figure: Kalshi $268K total in Florida with $75K to Donalds through July 2026, [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617). Kalshi gave $25,000 to Donalds' committee "last year" (2025), [Tampa Bay Times, June 15, 2026 (headline and caption only)](https://tampabay.com/news/florida-politics/elections/2026/06/15/byron-donalds-kalshi-campaign-donations-sports-election/)
- Robinhood's stated reason for donating: the brand "remains focused on preserving and expanding access to the financial markets.", [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
- CFTC posture: On Aug 7, 2026 the CFTC issued guidance warning operators that displaying prices in sportsbook-style odds could mislead consumers; DraftKings changed its Florida display Aug 28. CFTC Chairman Michael Selig has said only the CFTC can decide which contracts facilitate gambling., [Legal Sports Report, Oct 8, 2026](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/281066/draftkings-sued-by-seminole-tribe-over-sports-predictions-in-florida/); [Sportsbook Review, Feb 25, 2026](https://www.sportsbookreview.com/news/florida-governor-desantis-questions-kalshi-feb-25-2026/)
- National litigation map: Third Circuit granted Kalshi an injunction against New Jersey (KalshiEX v. Flaherty); New Jersey petitioned SCOTUS (Docket 26-299); Ninth Circuit (Sept 16, 2026) found two California tribes likely to succeed on IGRA claims; Sixth Circuit (Sept 25, 2026) held Kalshi had not shown its sports contracts are swaps under exclusive CFTC jurisdiction and that the CEA does not preempt Ohio or Tennessee gambling law; Michigan geofencing injunction Sept 2026; New York AG suit July 31, 2026; Cabazon Band amicus Oct 6, 2026; multi-tribe amicus of 130 tribes and 15 organizations Oct 8, 2026; 39 state AGs plus D.C. led by Ohio AG Andrew Wilson filed Oct 7, 2026 urging cert., [Covers Florida Kalshi page](https://www.covers.com/betting/prediction-sites/usa/florida-kalshi); [Pillsbury, Oct 19, 2026](https://www.pillsburylaw.com/en/news-and-insights/indian-gaming-prediction-markets-scotus.html); [CDC Gaming, Oct 7, 2026](https://cdcgaming.com/brief/tribe-39-state-attorneys-general-latest-to-ask-supreme-court-to-hear-prediction-markets-case/)
- Tribal legal theory: Kalshi contracts on Indian lands disregard tribal sovereignty under IGRA, divert revenue IGRA intends for tribes, and avoid regulatory costs and revenue sharing; tribes spend over $450M a year on gaming regulation., [Pillsbury, Oct 19, 2026](https://www.pillsburylaw.com/en/news-and-insights/indian-gaming-prediction-markets-scotus.html)
- Legislative attempts: HB 189 (2026 session, Rep. Dana Trabulsy) would make operating or promoting online sports betting, iGaming or illegal gambling a third-degree felony with exceptions for Hard Rock Bet and pari-mutuels, codify DFS, and preempt local regulation; the targets "could range from offshore sportsbooks to federally regulated prediction markets"; it did not change the compact and did not become law. HB 591 (2026) separately defined and prohibited "Internet gambling" and "Internet sports wagering" with compact exemptions., [Covers, Nov 19, 2025](https://www.covers.com/industry/florida-gaming-bill-seminole-sports-betting-igaming-ban-november-2025); [TribalGaming.com, June 12, 2026](https://tribalgaming.com/news/florida-hb-189-shadow-gambling-seminole-exclusivity-2026/); [HB 591 filed text](https://www.flsenate.gov/Session/Bill/2026/591/BillText/Filed/PDF)
- Susquehanna analyst Joe Stauff (Aug 2025) wrote that FanDuel's prediction expansion would target states where sports betting is illegal or restricted and "FL will be targeted.", [InGame, Aug 2025](https://www.ingame.com/?p=30596)

##### Inferences
- What a governor can do on prediction markets: (1) direct the FGCC (his appointees) to issue cease-and-desist letters or refer operators for prosecution under ch. 849, as Nevada, Maryland and others did; (2) have the AG (an independently elected official, but currently a DeSantis appointee and ally, James Uthmeier) sue or join multistate briefs; (3) sign or veto legislation like HB 189 or HB 591 that would criminalize non-compact online wagering; (4) decline to act, which is Florida's current posture and which effectively preserves access for Kalshi, Robinhood and Polymarket. Because the Seminole Tribe has now sued DraftKings itself, the governor's enforcement choice is also a choice about whether the state backs the Tribe's reading of exclusivity.
- The Tribe's selection of DraftKings as the first Florida target rather than Kalshi is consistent with DraftKings' sportsbook-identical interface and the Protect the Constitution history; Kalshi's defense (CFTC exclusive jurisdiction) is the stronger federal preemption argument, and Florida's AG stayed out of the national AG coalition, which Kalshi's Florida donations plausibly aim to preserve.
- A governor who sides with the Tribe risks the Trump family's prediction market ties (Trump Jr. at Kalshi and Polymarket) and Jeff Yass, Donalds' biggest individual donor and a Kalshi investor; a governor who sides with prediction markets risks a Seminole exclusivity claim that could reduce the roughly $900M annual payment, as happened in the 2015 to 2019 designated player dispute.

##### Gaps
- No source found describing any Seminole or Florida legal action against Kalshi, Robinhood, Crypto.com or Polymarket specifically in Florida through Oct 22, 2026; only the DraftKings suit was found.
- Whether Florida's AG signed the 38-state Massachusetts amicus (April 27, 2026) or the 39-state Oct 7 SCOTUS brief could not be confirmed; The Lines says Florida was one of six non-signers of the AG letter.
- Any Donalds response to the Oct 8 DraftKings lawsuit was not found.

#### 3. FanDuel, DraftKings, Caesars, Sports Betting Alliance: what they want in Florida; the Amendment 3 constraint

##### Takeaway
Commercial sportsbooks are shut out of Florida's mobile sports betting by the compact, and Amendment 3 (2018) requires a citizen initiative plus 60 percent voter approval for any casino gambling expansion, which killed their $20M plus 2022 ballot effort; their realistic routes are a future ballot initiative, a compact amendment in which the Tribe subcontracts to them (Jim Allen has said this is possible), or prediction market products that bypass the compact. No 2026 or 2028 Florida sports betting initiative was found to be active.

##### Cited Findings
- Amendment 3 (2018) passed with nearly 70 percent; requires voter approval for any casino gambling expansion; bankrolled by the Seminole Tribe with Disney; intent was to block legislative approval of commercial gaming; any new gambling form requires a citizen initiative with a 60 percent supermajority; DeSantis opposed it., [Casino.org, Oct 20, 2021](https://www.casino.org/news/seminoles-strike-back-against-ballot-threats-from-lvs-draftkings/); [The Lines, Aug 15, 2026](https://www.thelines.com/legal-betting/seminole-tribe-spending-to-protect-florida-gambling-interests/); [SoloAzar, May 2021](https://soloazar.com/en/category/casino/florida-gop-eases-path-for-trump-to-pursue-casino-license)
- 2022 ballot effort: Florida Education Champions (registered June 2021, DraftKings and FanDuel backed) sought a constitutional amendment authorizing sports and event betting at pro venues, pari-mutuels and statewide online, taxes to the Educational Enhancement Trust Fund; needed 891,589 signatures; DraftKings contributed $22.7M; the effort failed to qualify for Nov 2022 after "spending tens of millions" and missing the signature deadline. Ballard Partners deregistered for DraftKings and FanDuel the day the initiative was reported and now represents Hard Rock International., [Legal Sports Report, 2021](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/53512/ballot-measure-florida-sports-betting/); [Legal Sports Report, Oct 8, 2026](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/281066/draftkings-sued-by-seminole-tribe-over-sports-predictions-in-florida/); [InGame, Aug 2025](https://www.ingame.com/?p=30596); [The Capitolist, Nov 2, 2021](https://thecapitolist.com/florida-education-champions-touts-sports-betting-amendment-as-a-win-win/)
- Seminole counter-campaign in 2021: "Standing Up for Florida" committee with "Watch Out, Florida" and "Don't Sign Petitions" ads; Las Vegas Sands partnered with the Poarch Band on a separate "Florida Voters in Charge" initiative to let North Florida card rooms 130 miles or more from Seminole land become casinos., [Casino.org, Oct 20, 2021](https://www.casino.org/news/seminoles-strike-back-against-ballot-threats-from-lvs-draftkings/)
- Sports Betting Alliance members: DraftKings, FanDuel, Fanatics, BetMGM, bet365; gave $250K, all to Donalds. FanDuel $581K through July, nearly all to Donalds' committee; DK Crown Holdings $525K mostly to Donalds; Caesars $245K; Sideprize (PrizePicks) nearly $400K including $100K to Donalds and $100K to RPOF; Underdog Sports over $280K., [Gambling Insider, Aug 12, 2026](https://www.gamblinginsider.com/news/185823/gambling-prediction-markets-florida-elections-byron-donalds); [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
- Win for America PAC, funded largely by DraftKings and FanDuel, spends "tens of millions" nationally on state races to push favorable gambling legislation., [Bettors Insider, Aug 12, 2026](https://bettorsinsider.com/news/2026/08/12/gambling-industry-pours-12-2m-into-floridas-2026-midterms-as-seminole-tribe-leads-spending/)
- Jim Allen (Apr 2024): "every one of the companies would love to be in Florida"; Hard Rock has "never" ruled out working with Caesars, BetMGM, DraftKings, FanDuel., [Legal Sports Report, Apr 25, 2024](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/?p=178605)
- Analysts "do not expect meaningful change to Florida's sportsbook market before 2027, if at all.", [TribalGaming.com, June 12, 2026](https://tribalgaming.com/news/florida-hb-189-shadow-gambling-seminole-exclusivity-2026/)
- FanDuel and DraftKings' 2022 effort was described by The Lines and CardPlayer as the model for what "legalization could be a possible compromise to allow Seminole online casino gaming" (prediction markets legalized alongside Tribal iGaming)., [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)

##### Inferences
- Since the Legislature cannot authorize commercial sports betting under Amendment 3, the governor's only direct gift to FanDuel, DraftKings and Caesars is through the compact: an amendment under which the Tribe licenses or skins its exclusivity to them (the compact already requires the Tribe to partner with pari-mutuels at 60/40). That requires the Tribe's consent, so these donors are buying a seat at any future renegotiation table, not a path around the Tribe.
- The Sports Betting Alliance's and Caesars' giving to the same candidate as the Tribe, and Jim Allen's openness to partners, is consistent with a bargained outcome (Tribal iGaming in exchange for commercial skins) that only a governor can convene.
- DraftKings' $525K to Donalds now sits alongside the Tribe's Oct 8 lawsuit against it; the governor and FGCC will be asked, implicitly, which side the state is on.

##### Gaps
- No active 2026 or 2028 Florida sports betting or casino ballot initiative was found; a targeted search could not be run because the search budget was exhausted.
- Caesars' specific Florida objective (it has no Florida property found in sources) was not documented.

#### 4. Steve Wynn's Florida interests and history

##### Takeaway
Steve Wynn's documented Florida interest is the 2011 to 2012 push for destination resort casinos in Miami-Dade and Broward, when he called the Miami Beach Convention Center site "the greatest site for a destination resort in the United States"; that bill died in a House subcommittee Feb 3, 2012 under opposition from the Seminole Tribe, Disney and the Florida Chamber. No source found documents a current (2026) Wynn Florida project; his $1M to Donalds (reported in Q1 2026 fundraising coverage) is consistent with his long record of large GOP giving.

##### Cited Findings
- Nov 2011: Wynn met Miami Beach Mayor Matti Herrera Bower and City Manager Jorge Gonzalez; pointed to the roughly 50-acre city-owned Miami Beach Convention Center site; called Miami Beach "the greatest site for a destination resort in the United States.", [Meetings Today, Nov 11, 2011](https://www.meetingstoday.com/articles/124884/wynn-tips-his-hand-big-miami-plans)
- 2012 destination resort bill (Rep. Erik Fresen): three resorts in Broward and Miami-Dade, $2B pledge each; tabled Feb 3, 2012 after an 8-7 subcommittee split; opposed by a coalition including the Seminole Tribe, Disney, Florida Chamber, pari-mutuels and the Restaurant and Lodging Association. Genting (Resorts World Miami) was the main proponent; Wynn is not named in this account., [WFSU, Feb 3, 2012](https://news.wfsu.org/politics/2012-02-03/gaming-bill-dies-in-house)
- Q1 2026 fundraising coverage lists Steve Wynn, "a billionaire casino company owner," at $1M to Donalds, alongside the Seminole Tribe at $1M (at that point), Jeff Yass $2.5M and Club for Growth $2.5M; Donalds raised about $22.44M in Q1 2026., [Highlands News-Sun syndicated report](https://www.midfloridanewspapers.com/highlands_news-sun/news/donalds-dominates-fundraising-in-governor-race/article_70925d94-73c0-4f61-9737-e3805c0a1d70.html)
- Wynn's national GOP giving history: pro-Trump super PAC kept a $500,000 Wynn donation; reports of $7M in Wynn Resorts stock to the Trump inauguration; GOP continued accepting his money after 2018 misconduct allegations., [Casino.org](https://www.casino.org/news/pro-trump-super-pac-wont-return-500000-steve-wynn-donation); [WGBH, Oct 21, 2020](https://wgbh.org/news/politics/2020-10-21/gop-welcomes-steve-wynns-millions-despite-rape-and-harassment-allegations)

##### Inferences
- Wynn no longer controls Wynn Resorts (he resigned in 2018 and sold his stake), so his 2026 gift is best read as personal political giving by a Trump-aligned donor and possibly as positioning for a future South Florida destination resort license (Doral or Miami Beach) if Amendment 3 were ever overcome, rather than as a current corporate ask. This is inference; no 2026 source states a Wynn Florida project.

##### Gaps
- No 2026 reporting found tying Wynn's $1M to a specific Florida gaming objective; the Tampa Bay Times Oct 2 article body (which may discuss Wynn) could not be retrieved.
- Whether Wynn's gift went to Friends of Byron Donalds or another vehicle, and its date, were not confirmed in retrieved text.

#### 5. Poarch Band of Creek Indians: Florida holdings and goals

##### Takeaway
The Alabama-based Poarch Band, through PCI Gaming and Wind Creek Hospitality, owns a pari-mutuel and card room in Gretna (Gadsden County), a card room in Pensacola, two other North Florida properties, and acquired the Magic City Casino permit in Miami (deal announced Nov to Dec 2022, price undisclosed); its long-running goal is a Florida compact or a ballot route to casino gaming in North Florida, which the Seminole compact and Amendment 3 block.

##### Cited Findings
- Dec 2022: Poarch Band (Wind Creek Hospitality) agreed to acquire 100 percent of Magic City Casino's Miami gambling permit from West Flagler Associates, price undisclosed, pending FGCC approval; Poarch already held two North Florida properties, a Pensacola card room, and a Gretna barrel racing track and card room., [Casino.org, Dec 1, 2022](https://www.casino.org/news/poarch-creeks-to-buy-miamis-magic-city-casino/)
- March 2014: the tribe wrote Gov. Rick Scott requesting a gaming compact for a casino on its land outside Pensacola; offered to forgo blackjack if the state would allow a poker-style card room; offered a $250M budget bailout as part of a pact; "no progress was made"; Florida efforts "stalled.", [Florida Politics](https://floridapolitics.com/?p=215078)
- 2021: Poarch partnered with Las Vegas Sands on the "Florida Voters in Charge" initiative to allow North Florida card rooms 130 or more miles from Seminole land to become casinos., [Casino.org, Oct 20, 2021](https://www.casino.org/news/seminoles-strike-back-against-ballot-threats-from-lvs-draftkings/)
- 2026 cycle giving: Poarch Creek Indians $555,000 total in Florida, including $100,000 to Donalds., [Gambling Insider, Aug 12, 2026](https://www.gamblinginsider.com/news/185823/gambling-prediction-markets-florida-elections-byron-donalds)
- Miccosukee Tribe (for comparison): $10,000 to Friends of Byron Donalds, $25,000 to Conservatives for a Better Florida, $20,000 each to the Florida Republican Senatorial Campaign Committee and RPOF, at least $239,000 in 2026 state giving, nothing to Jolly; Chairman Talbert Cypress says the tribe wants leaders "committed to restoring the Everglades.", [WUSF/WLRN, Oct 2, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-10-02/despite-detention-center-lawsuit-the-miccosukee-tribe-donated-to-supporters-of-alligator-alcatraz)

##### Inferences
- Poarch's $555K is the second largest tribal outlay in Florida this cycle and spread beyond Donalds, consistent with a legislative and regulatory strategy (FGCC permits, slots at Gretna, a Magic City expansion) rather than a single ask of the governor. A Poarch compact would require the governor to negotiate and the Legislature to ratify, and would collide with Seminole exclusivity.

##### Gaps
- Status of the Magic City acquisition and any 2025 to 2026 Poarch Florida filings could not be searched (budget exhausted).

#### 6. The Seminole Tribe's broader 2025 to 2026 political giving and lobbyist network

##### Takeaway
The Tribe is the dominant political donor in Florida gaming, at roughly $8.75M to $9.1M by mid-August 2026 and $8.788M per Transparency USA by Oct 2, with $5.5M to Friends of Byron Donalds (the largest and latest tranche in Sept 2026), $1M to Wilton Simpson, $750K to Jim Boyd, $750K to RPOF, $200K to the Florida Democratic Party, $50K to AG James Uthmeier, $50K to Charlie Crist for St. Petersburg mayor, and nothing to David Jolly.

##### Cited Findings
- Seminole giving through July 2026: $9.1M total; Donalds $4M; Agriculture Commissioner Wilton Simpson $1M; Sen. Jim Boyd $750K; Republican Party of Florida $750K; Florida Democratic Party $200K; Charlie Crist (St. Petersburg mayor race) $50K., [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
- The Lines (Aug 15, 2026): Tribe at $8.75M total; $4M to Donalds; more than $7M to Florida Republicans; $200K to the Florida Democratic Party; $50K to AG Uthmeier; nothing to Jolly; "the Seminole Tribe appears to expect Republican Byron Donalds to protect its monopoly.", [The Lines](https://www.thelines.com/legal-betting/seminole-tribe-spending-to-protect-florida-gambling-interests/)
- By Oct 2, 2026: Tribe at least $8,788,000 to candidates and PACs (Transparency USA); $5.5M to Friends of Byron Donalds, with the latest and largest donation appearing in records in September 2026; federal giving since Jan 2025 of $544,600 with about two-fifths to Democrats., [WUSF/WLRN, Oct 2, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-10-02/despite-detention-center-lawsuit-the-miccosukee-tribe-donated-to-supporters-of-alligator-alcatraz)
- Tampa Bay Times (Michael Van Sickler, Aug 11, 2026): Hard Rock owners spent "nearly $9 million this election cycle"; gaming interests gave at least $12.2M through July, with "three out of every four dollars" from the Tribe; the Tribe is betting heavily on Crist's mayoral campaign and St. Petersburg officials wonder whether Derby Lane is part of the Tribe's plans. St. Petersburg Mayor Ken Welch called the Crist donation "a dangerous moment.", [Tampa Bay Times, Aug 11, 2026 (headline and caption)](https://www.tampabay.com/news/florida-politics/elections/2026/08/11/sports-betting-gambling-seminole-tribe-hard-rock-kalshi-campaign-money/); [CDC Gaming brief](https://cdcgaming.com/brief/gambling-money-goes-all-in-on-florida-in-this-election-cycle/); [CardPlayer](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
