- Tampa Bay Times Oct 2, 2026 analysis (Ashley Borja and Michael Van Sickler, figures through Sept 18, as summarized by Bonus.com): Donalds $131M raised; Seminole $5.5M the largest named gaming donor; gaming and casino sector roughly 10 percent of large-dollar donations to Donalds; Jeff Yass $11M; DeVos family $1M; 17 of 18 billionaire donors gave to Donalds ($23.3M, about 18 percent of his total); J.B. Pritzker $50K was Jolly's only billionaire; Jolly $16M raised; more than $39M (about 30 percent) of Donalds' money from out of state; the Times "did not tie the donations to any pending regulatory action.", [Bonus.com, Oct 5, 2026](https://www.bonus.com/news/gaming-money-flows-into-florida-governor-race-with-seminole-tribe-draftkings-and-fanduel-b/)
- Precedent: the Tribe gave $1M to Friends of Ron DeSantis in Aug 2022, another $1M in late Sept 2022, and $1M to Protect Our Values PC the same week., [CBS Miami, Oct 7, 2022](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/seminole-tribe-of-florida-puts-another-1-million-gov-desantis)
- Lobbying: Ballard Partners dropped DraftKings and FanDuel in June 2021 and represents Hard Rock International., [Legal Sports Report, 2021](https://www.legalsportsreport.com/53512/ballot-measure-florida-sports-betting/). In 3Q 2011 the Tribe and its associations spent $110,001 to $179,993 on Tallahassee lobbying., [Jacksonville Daily Record, Nov 25, 2011](https://www.jaxdailyrecord.com/news/2011/nov/25/gambling-groups-spend-millions-lobbyists)

##### Inferences
- The Tribe's 2026 pattern (governor's PAC, Cabinet officials Simpson and Uthmeier, Senate budget chair Boyd, both state parties, and a Democratic mayoral candidate in a city with a pari-mutuel) mirrors its 2022 DeSantis giving and targets every office that touches compact amendment, FGCC confirmation, enforcement, and local siting.
- The $50K to Uthmeier is small but notable given his office's role in defending the compact in Protect the Constitution and in choosing not to join AG coalitions against Kalshi.

##### Gaps
- A current roster of Seminole and Hard Rock registered lobbyists (2025 to 2026 Florida lobbyist registrations) was not retrieved.
- Direct Seminole giving to Senate President Albritton's or Speaker Perez's committees was not found in retrieved text.
- The Tampa Bay Times Oct 2 article body was not accessible; the figures above are via Bonus.com's summary.

#### 7. What Donalds and Jolly have said; reporter analysis of who gains

##### Takeaway
Donalds has made no public statement on the compact, iGaming or prediction markets found in any source; his campaign's only response is adviser Danielle Alvarez's line that "Byron Donalds answers to no one but the people of Florida." Jolly has attacked the donations ("All of them are funding Byron Donalds... They want to make sure that they have more power than we do") but has not been found stating a gambling policy. Industry analysts (The Lines, CardPlayer, Gambling Insider) read the money as the Tribe buying protection of its monopoly and prediction markets buying continued state inaction, with the next battleground being prediction markets and Tribal iGaming.

##### Cited Findings
- Donalds campaign senior adviser Danielle Alvarez: "Byron Donalds answers to no one but the people of Florida"; campaign says accepting gaming donations does not mean he would act on those groups' behalf. Donalds himself not quoted., [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
- Jolly at a Tampa Democratic fundraiser (weekend before Aug 13, 2026): "All of them are funding Byron Donalds." "They want to make sure that they have more power than we do." He argued the Seminoles and major Donalds donors want policy influence., [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
- Jolly declined Donalds' challenge to three debates (Sept 21, 2026), so no debate exchange on gambling exists., [MySuncoast, Sept 21, 2026](https://www.mysuncoast.com/2026/09/21/jolly-declines-debate-with-donalds-ahead-midterm-election/)
- The pro-Jolly "Puppet" ad by Conservatives for Ethical Leadership (Oct 13, 2026) attacks Donalds over data centers, insurance and Wall Street donors; it does not mention gambling or the Seminoles., [The Floridian, Oct 13, 2026](https://floridianpress.com/?p=99248)
- Analyst framing: "Following the money is usually a good way to determine political alignments" and the Tribe "appears to expect Republican Byron Donalds to protect its monopoly"; the Tribe wants "a governor who will oppose prediction markets.", [The Lines, Aug 15, 2026](https://www.thelines.com/legal-betting/seminole-tribe-spending-to-protect-florida-gambling-interests/)
- Daniel Wallach: "The next battleground is prediction markets and online gaming"; "hardly shocking" that politicians take money from both sides; prediction market legalization could be a compromise enabling Seminole online casino gaming., [CardPlayer, Aug 13, 2026](https://www.cardplayer.com/?p=1735617)
- Gambling Insider: Donalds is the "prohibitive favorite"; Florida's non-challenge of prediction markets makes "the next governor's stance potentially significant"; "the contributions do not establish how Donalds would approach prediction markets as governor.", [Gambling Insider, Aug 12, 2026](https://www.gamblinginsider.com/news/185823/gambling-prediction-markets-florida-elections-byron-donalds)
- Polling context cited by the gambling press: UNF poll Donalds +5 over Jolly (Aug 2026); EDGE Communications (D) poll Donalds 41.2, Jolly 40.5 (spring 2026); Tampa Bay Times describes a single-digit race (Oct 2026)., [The Lines](https://www.thelines.com/legal-betting/seminole-tribe-spending-to-protect-florida-gambling-interests/); [Highlands News-Sun](https://www.midfloridanewspapers.com/highlands_news-sun/news/donalds-dominates-fundraising-in-governor-race/article_70925d94-73c0-4f61-9737-e3805c0a1d70.html); [Bonus.com](https://www.bonus.com/news/gaming-money-flows-into-florida-governor-race-with-seminole-tribe-draftkings-and-fanduel-b/)
- Fundraising scale: Donalds over $111M by Aug 17, 2026, about $99M through Friends of Byron Donalds with over $51M cash on hand; Jolly $6.3M campaign plus $3.9M committee at that date., [Florida Phoenix, Aug 17, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/08/17/will-byron-donalds-dominance-in-fundraising-result-in-a-gop-victory/)

##### Inferences (what a Donalds administration would plausibly change, given the record)
- Documented fact: every gaming interest on both sides of the exclusivity fight gave to Donalds and essentially none gave to Jolly, so the money is not a bet on a policy but on access to the officeholder who controls compact amendment and FGCC appointments.
- The Tribe's incremental escalation (from $1M in Q1 2026 to $4M by July to $5.5M by Sept 18) tracks the period in which it prepared and filed the DraftKings suit and in which the iGaming reopener had already lapsed; the most concrete Seminole ask of a new governor is a compact amendment adding online casino gaming (Allen's stated goal since 2024), plus state backing against prediction markets.
- The prediction market side (Kalshi $75K plus Yass $11M, Robinhood $200K, PrizePicks, Underdog) most plausibly wants continued Florida inaction, no FGCC cease-and-desist, and no HB 189 style felony statute; Donalds' Trump alignment and Trump Jr.'s Kalshi and Polymarket roles make an aggressive anti-prediction-market posture politically costly for him.
- The commercial sportsbooks (FanDuel, DraftKings, Caesars, SBA) most plausibly want a seat in any compact renegotiation that yields skins or a shared iGaming framework, since Amendment 3 forecloses a legislative route.
- Net: a Donalds administration that reopens the compact for iGaming would be the path on which the Tribe, the commercial books and (via a bargained carve-out) prediction markets could all gain, at the cost of a likely Amendment 3 lawsuit; a Donalds administration that does nothing preserves the status quo in which the Tribe keeps exclusivity on paper, prediction markets keep operating, and the Tribe litigates privately. A Jolly administration's posture is undocumented.

##### Gaps
- No direct Donalds statement on gambling, the compact, iGaming, prediction markets or the DraftKings suit was found in any source through Oct 22, 2026.
- No Jolly policy position on gambling beyond criticizing the donations was found.
- No Tampa Bay Times, Miami Herald, Florida Phoenix or Seeking Rents piece explicitly analyzing "who gains under each candidate" was retrievable; the Tampa Bay Times Aug 11 and Oct 2 pieces likely contain such analysis but their bodies were not accessible (paywall and page structure), and the search budget ended before Seeking Rents or Miami Herald coverage could be located.

### Note: school_sugar_trumpworld_second_hop.md

_Folder: Donalds second hop who gains_

### Donalds 2026: Second Hop on School Choice, Sugar and Agriculture, Trump World and Operatives, and the Governing Agenda

Research note on method: all findings below are dated through Oct 22, 2026. Documented facts carry an inline source. Inference is kept in the Inferences subsections. The hourly web fetch quota and the shared per turn search budget both ran out during this task, so several high value pages (Florida Politics archives on ad buys and fundraising, the Tampa Bay Times recommendation editorial, the Yahoo/USA Today environmental legacy piece, the Floridian piece on homestead repeal, Step Up for Students application story) could not be read. Those are listed as gaps.

#### Key Question 1: Florida's school choice economy, who profits, and what Donalds proposes

##### Takeaway
Florida's taxpayer funded choice system now costs roughly $4B to $4.5B a year and serves more than 500,000 students, with a November 2025 Auditor General report documenting overspending, double funding and weak controls, and a Senate fix (SB 318) that died in the House in 2026. Donalds proposes to make funding follow every student, scrap the A to F grading system for individualized plans, lean on national tests and AI and ed tech, and expand private sector participation, which is the same agenda Erika Donalds and her funders (Yass, DeVos, Griffin) articulate.

##### Cited Findings
- Taxpayer funded school choice in Florida costs about $4.5 billion a year, with more than 500,000 students enrolled and a voucher worth about $8,000 annually; the universal program was created in 2023 and is open to any Florida child regardless of income; most voucher students attend Christian private schools per the Orlando Sentinel. [WLRN / Florida Trib, Aug 3, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-03/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida)
- The program's overall cost is described as about $4 billion in legislative coverage. [WLRN, Mar 25, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/education/2026-03-25/legislature-eyes-fixes-for-voucher-program-despite-inaction-so-far)
- 82% of Florida voucher students attend religious schools. [Sarasota Magazine, Jan 21, 2026](https://www.sarasotamagazine.com/news-and-profiles/2026/01/fraud-lack-of-oversight-overspending-florida-school-voucher-program)
- State Auditor General report on the 2024 to 2025 Family Empowerment Scholarship (released November 2025) found overspending and payment delays that caused a funding shortfall, a "myriad of accountability challenges," and no proper controls to verify where voucher students were being educated; the enrollment cross check relying on volunteer parent surveys "appeared to reward unresponsiveness." Recommendations: cross check applicants against district enrollment, confirm eligibility before each payment, move from quarterly to monthly installments, and place scholarship money in a separate silo inside the FEFP. [WLRN, Mar 25, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/education/2026-03-25/legislature-eyes-fixes-for-voucher-program-despite-inaction-so-far)
- In August 2025 Step Up for Students and DOE froze or declined to fund about 28,000 student accounts in the August and October counts; by the third quarter count fewer than 6,000 remained in the cross check. Step Up spokesperson Scott Kent said this "radically reduced" double funding. [WLRN, Mar 25, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/education/2026-03-25/legislature-eyes-fixes-for-voucher-program-despite-inaction-so-far)
- Between $1 million and $2 million went to scammers who created fraudulent accounts for fictitious students, per a state audit reported via the Tallahassee Democrat. [Sarasota Magazine, Jan 21, 2026](https://www.sarasotamagazine.com/news-and-profiles/2026/01/fraud-lack-of-oversight-overspending-florida-school-voucher-program)
- SB 318 (Sen. Don Gaetz, R Crestview, co sponsor Sen. Corey Simon) passed the Senate unanimously and would have implemented the audit recommendations; the House never took it up and it died. DOE agreed with the separate silo recommendation and put it in its budget request; lawmakers did not adopt it. Rep. Jenna Persons Mulicka (R Fort Myers), House PreK 12 Budget chair, had called a separate scholarship silo a "huge mistake." Gaetz plans to refile in 2027. [WLRN, Mar 25, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/education/2026-03-25/legislature-eyes-fixes-for-voucher-program-despite-inaction-so-far)
- SB 318 provisions included fall and spring application windows, a single application, multiple proofs of residency including birth certificate, cross checking against district enrollment, an ID per recipient, eligibility confirmation before each payment, and cutting Scholarship Funding Organization administrative fees from 3% to 2%. [Sarasota Magazine, Jan 21, 2026](https://www.sarasotamagazine.com/news-and-profiles/2026/01/fraud-lack-of-oversight-overspending-florida-school-voucher-program)
- Local example: $45 million in Sarasota County taxpayer dollars went to private school vouchers in 2025 to 2026; FES use there rose 40% from 3,266 (2023 to 24) to 4,585 (2024 to 25); the district cites a $2.5 million shortfall from vouchers. [Sarasota Magazine, Jan 21, 2026](https://www.sarasotamagazine.com/news-and-profiles/2026/01/fraud-lack-of-oversight-overspending-florida-school-voucher-program)
- Private school owners sued Step Up for Students in February 2026 alleging funding failures (headline only; article not read). [WLRN, Feb 19, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/light/education/2026-02-19/florida-school-vouchers-lawsuit-step-up)
- FEA sued over the choice system in May 2026 and endorsed Jolly in June 2026. [WLRN / Florida Trib, Aug 3, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-03/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida)
- Donalds' stated education proposals (March 2026 Doral event): parents "at the head of the table"; public funding follows students to whatever school they attend; overhaul yearly standardized tests and the A to F school grading system; revise grade 6 to 12 teaching standards to make education modular and customizable; individualized education plans for all students; AI and ed tech in classrooms. [WLRN / Florida Trib, Aug 3, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-03/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida)
- At the Florida PTA Leadership Convention (Orlando, reported July 12, 2026) Donalds called for a major overhaul of standardized testing leaning on ACT and SAT, a "universal scorecard," and expanded apprenticeships. [Hoodline, Jul 12, 2026](https://hoodline.com/2026/07/florida-pta-showdown-as-guv-hopefuls-brawl-over-teacher-pay-and-vouchers/)
- Donalds' "Read to Succeed" plan targets third grade reading; the voters guide does not describe any voucher accountability measures from him. [ClickOrlando voters guide, Oct 16, 2026](https://www.clickorlando.com/voters-guide/2026/10/16/2026-voters-guide-florida-governors-race/)
- Donalds told The FAMUAN (Oct 11, 2026) he would tie university funding to whether graduates enter careers related to their studies ("That would go into how much funding this university gets"), apply consistent standards to all ten universities, and named engineering, finance, accounting, law, medicine and architecture as degrees with "real economic power." [The FAMUAN, Oct 11, 2026](https://www.thefamuanonline.com/2026/10/11/byron-donalds-speaks-with-the-famuan-about-famu-funding-affordability-and-his-vision-for-florida/)
- Jeff Yass gave $7.5 million to Friends of Byron Donalds (including a $2.5M check in March 2026); Yass proposes giving parents 100% of per pupil funding in a savings account he estimates could reach six figures by graduation; a Susquehanna spokesperson called Donalds "a school choice warrior." OptimaEd was a 2023 Yass Prize semifinalist and Erika says she met the Yasses through it. [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-billionaire-trying-to-reinvent-florida-schools-has-a-7-5-million-bet-on-byron-donalds-40565603/); [WLRN / Florida Trib, Aug 3, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-03/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida)
- Erika Donalds at the Doral event: "Profit is not going to be a bad word when it comes to the education industry" and "We cannot imagine, when we unleash the capitalism, free market competition in education." Byron: "Donors do not set my agenda, the people of Florida do." [WLRN / Florida Trib, Aug 3, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-03/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida)
- The Donalds policy package as summarized by Miami New Times: replace A to F grades with individualized learning plans; expand apprenticeships and credentialing; embrace AI and ed tech; increase private sector participation; use vouchers and ESAs as the foundation for broader restructuring; treat parents as consumers and schools as competitors. [Miami New Times](https://www.miaminewtimes.com/news/the-billionaire-trying-to-reinvent-florida-schools-has-a-7-5-million-bet-on-byron-donalds-40565603/)
- Other school choice money to Friends of Byron Donalds, per an opinion Substack citing state records: Richard Uihlein $2M; Club for Growth PAC $3.65M (Yass is its largest historical donor); AFC Victory Fund (Yass linked) sent $300,000 to John Kirtley's Florida Federation for Children PAC in 2024. Treat as secondary until checked against the state database. [Steve Edward Farley Substack, opinion](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/how-the-donalds-network-the-school)
- Summit Construction Management Group (Jacksonville, co founder Charles "Chip" Cordes) charter clients beyond Optima include Duval Charters, two KIPP Academies in Jacksonville, Lizza Jackson Preparatory, Mater Academy (an Academica affiliated network) and Clay Charter Academy; Cordes and wife gave $8,400 each to Donalds' 2020 campaigns and Cordes $2,000 in 2021. [Florida Bulldog, Jan 2026](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2026/01/charter-business-owned-rep-donalds-wife-cut-side-deals-construction-firm-suit-says/)

##### Inferences
- Donalds' "funding follows the student" plus "individualized plans for all students" plus "private sector participation" is operationally an ESA expansion toward the Yass model of 100% per pupil funding in accounts, which would route more money through Step Up and through vendor marketplaces of the type OptimaEd and classical networks sell into. No source yet documents a specific Donalds proposal to raise the per pupil ESA amount.
- Because the House killed SB 318 and Donalds' public education plan contains no voucher accountability measures, a Donalds administration is unlikely to push the Auditor General's controls absent outside pressure; Jolly has explicitly made accountability and means testing his position (see Key Question 7).
- The Donalds university funding idea (fund by graduate career outcomes) would move Florida's performance funding model further toward workforce metrics; no analyst reaction was found.

##### Gaps
- Exact 2025 to 2026 FES and FTC totals from the Florida DOE or Step Up annual report were not retrieved; the $4B to $4.5B range comes from press. Step Up's current fee (3% in statute per SB 318 coverage) was not confirmed from a primary document.
- Charter Schools USA and Academica contributions to Donalds committees were not found in the sources read; the Transparency USA committee pages were in search results but not fetched.
- No source documents Hillsdale's current Florida affiliate count or any Hillsdale donation to Donalds.
- No 2026 OptimaEd client list was found; Florida Bulldog says Optima's Lee County school launch was pushed to August 2026 and the Fort Myers school enrolled hundreds but never opened.

#### Key Question 2: Erika Donalds' current ventures and any role in a Donalds administration

##### Takeaway
Erika Donalds renamed Optima Foundation the Education(al) Freedom Foundation in March 2025, still controls OptimaEd and Optima Management Services through Onesto LLC, chairs AFPI's Center for Education Opportunity, advises Moms for Liberty and the Classical Learning Test, launched a statewide "Florida Promise Tour" on Sept 21, 2026, and told Axios she will keep doing education policy work as first lady: "I would be doing that whether I'm first lady or not."

##### Cited Findings
- Optima Foundation (launched 2017) was renamed Education Freedom Foundation in March 2025; it and affiliated charters paid more than $10 million to two for profit companies tied to her. Onesto, LLC (Delaware, formed late 2018, sole owner Erika Donalds) controls both OptimaEd (2020) and Optima Management Services (2022, originally Delaware). Amended 2023 disclosure values Optima Management Services at $1M to $5M and OptimaEd at $1M to $5M; the 2024 disclosure values OptimaEd at $500K to $1M and shows she no longer receives a salary from Educator Solutions; Onesto paid her a $6,000 honorarium. [Florida Bulldog, Sep 7, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/09/disclosures-deepend-mystery-rep-donaldss-wifes-charter-school-business/)
- Florida Bulldog (Jan 2026) refers to the renamed entity as the "Educational Freedom Foundation"; the TCCA suit was dismissed in May 2025 on venue grounds (contract required Collier County) and TCCA appealed; the suit alleges Optima urged hiring Summit "without multiple bids," an undisclosed conflict in which Summit related entities invested in Optima related entities, and payment of a $127,487.98 Summit invoice without board authorization. [Florida Bulldog, Jan 2026](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2026/01/charter-business-owned-rep-donalds-wife-cut-side-deals-construction-firm-suit-says/)
- Roles listed: advisory boards of the Classical Learning Test, Moms for Liberty, and the Independent Women's Forum Education Freedom Center; leads education policy at AFPI; Heritage Foundation visiting fellow; FGCU trustee appointed by DeSantis (Mar 26, 2022); 2017 to 18 Constitution Revision Commission (named by Speaker Corcoran). [Wikipedia, Erika Donalds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erika_Donalds)
- She was floated as a possible Trump secretary of education; she is a chair of education opportunity at AFPI; the Trib reports she plans to "continue working professionally" if Byron is elected. [WLRN / Florida Trib, Aug 3, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-03/what-byron-and-erika-donalds-gospel-of-school-choice-could-mean-for-florida)
- Axios Miami (Sep 22, 2026): she expects the state to keep expanding "more parental empowerment, more choice, more diverse options"; wants teachers held accountable for implementing the science of reading; calls herself an AI enthusiast and warns regulating AI could be "overstepping parental rights boundaries"; at a D.C. roundtable called teachers the "biggest challenge" to adopting new technologies (per Miami Herald). She expects to keep doing education work as she has for 10 to 15 years: "I would be doing that whether I'm first lady or not." Monroe County board member Sue Woltanski: "She's a messenger for the privatization of schools." [Axios Miami, Sep 22, 2026](https://www.axios.com/local/miami/2026/09/22/erika-donalds-governor-mansion-education-florida-byron)
- She launched a statewide "Florida Promise Tour" in South Miami on Sep 21, 2026. [Local 10, Sep 21, 2026](https://local10.com/news/2026/09/21/school-choice-activist-erika-donalds-kicks-off-statewide-tour-in-south-miami)
- She is riding the "Defending the Florida Dream Tour" bus (50 stops, 38 counties, launched Oct 19, 2026, first day of early voting) with Byron, running mate Bryan Avila, and rotating officials including Sen. Ashley Moody, Sheriff Grady Judd, Wilton Simpson, Blaise Ingoglia and James Uthmeier. [WLRN, Oct 19, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-10-19/byron-donalds-gubernatorial-campaign-launches-statewide-bus-tour-timed-with-early-voting)
- Classical Learning Test: Florida's Board of Governors adopted the CLT as a university admissions alternative to the SAT and ACT in September 2023 (headlines only; articles not read). [Catholic World Report, Sep 18, 2023](https://www.catholicworldreport.com/2023/09/18/this-is-the-beginning-florida-university-system-adopts-classic-learning-test/); [The Capitolist](https://thecapitolist.com/florida-approves-use-of-classic-learning-test-as-college-entrance-exam-alternative/)
- Moms for Liberty 2026 Florida activity: endorsed 15 school board candidates, 11 advanced to November runoffs, expected to hold a majority on only two boards (Clay and Brevard); spokesperson says $25,000 spent on Florida candidates though PAC records show no candidate contributions; the group is aligning with Trump, DeSantis and Donalds. Ziegler (Sarasota) and Carney (Duval) are not seeking reelection. [Florida Trib, Sep 2, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/09/02/once-a-powerhouse-moms-for-liberty-expected-to-hold-majority-on-just-two-florida-school-boards/)
- Opinion Substack claims (unverified by me): Tom Grady's Quest Educational Foundation and Freedom Institute of Collier County share an address with OptimaEd; Glen Gilzean sat on an Optima linked board while chairing the Ethics Commission and resigned Aug 2023; State Board member Layla Collins voted Nov 2025 to adopt the Heritage "Phoenix Declaration" that Erika co authored. [Steve Edward Farley Substack, opinion](https://steveedwardfarley.substack.com/p/how-the-donalds-network-the-school). Florida Bulldog independently reported Quest Educational Foundation shares a Naples address with the Optima companies with no rent apparently paid. [Florida Bulldog, Jun 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)

##### Inferences
- No source reports a formal appointment or transition role for Erika Donalds. Her own statement to Axios plus the AFPI chairmanship plus the Promise Tour suggest an outside influence role on education policy rather than an official post. Any assertion that she would run DOE or the State Board is inference, not reporting.
- The renaming to Education Freedom Foundation in March 2025, weeks after Byron's February 2025 launch, reads as a rebrand away from the Optima name associated with the CBS and Bulldog reporting; no source states that motive.

##### Gaps
- "Dewalt Impact Group LLC" produced no relevant search results; I could not confirm it exists or is hers.
- AFPI's Florida specific agenda or model bills were not retrieved (search budget exhausted before the AFPI query ran).
- Whether CLT has been adopted for Bright Futures or K 12 use in Florida since 2023 was not checked.
- OptimaEd's 2026 clients are not documented in any source read.

#### Key Question 3: Rep. John Snyder and Educator Solutions

##### Takeaway
Snyder (R Stuart, HD 86, in office since Nov 3, 2020, Marine veteran, son of former Martin County Sheriff William Snyder) owns ESI Technical Inc., doing business as Educator Solutions, which Optima run charters paid $6.93M in 2020 to 2022 and which paid Erika Donalds more than $500K; he has reported nearly $700K in ESI income since 2020. No source documents other state contracts or a formal Donalds campaign role.

##### Cited Findings
- Snyder: Florida House District 86 since 2022 (District 82, 2020 to 2022), took office Nov 3, 2020; reelected with 68.1% in 2024; USMC 2007 to 2012; father William Snyder was a House member 2006 to 2012 and Martin County sheriff 2013 to 2025. [Wikipedia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Snyder_(Florida_politician))
- "Educator Solutions" is a fictitious name registered to ESI Technical Inc., which Snyder founded; his disclosures show nearly $700,000 from ESI since 2020 and list Optima linked charters as ESI's biggest customers. Optima run charters paid Educator Solutions $6,930,584 in 2020 to 2022; Optima Foundation paid it $2,783,216 in the same years for "payroll services" and about $1.76M in 2023; a January 2023 board memo says HR and payroll converted from ESI to Optima Management Services. Byron Donalds disclosed his wife earned over half a million dollars from Educator Solutions in 2020 to 2022; Snyder did not answer questions about her title or duties. [Florida Bulldog, Jun 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/06/firms-belonging-to-rep-donalds-wife-grabbed-millions-in-charter-school-contracts/)
- 2024 disclosure: Erika no longer receives a salary from Educator Solutions; an Optima network school dropped Educator Solutions as HR and payroll vendor in favor of Optima Management Services. [Florida Bulldog, Sep 7, 2025](https://www.floridabulldog.org/2025/09/disclosures-deepend-mystery-rep-donaldss-wifes-charter-school-business/)

##### Inferences
- Snyder was first elected in 2020 and would be term limited after 2028; he is not among the nine state senators who publicly endorsed Donalds, and no House endorsement list was found. His political relationship to Donalds beyond the business tie is undocumented in what I read.

##### Gaps
- Snyder's current committee assignments and any 2025 to 2026 bills he sponsored on charters or vouchers were not retrieved (the flhouse.gov and LobbyTools pages were not fetched).
- No source on other ESI Technical state or district contracts.

#### Key Question 4: Sugar, agriculture, Mosaic, and Everglades

##### Takeaway
The EAA Reservoir is now a state led build with all contracts finalized (April 2026) and a 2029 completion target; Florida Crystals publicly commended the deal and frames itself as a restoration partner, having ended leases early in 2019 (New Hope Sugar, 1,234 acres, $2.4M termination fee). Everglades Trust endorsed Donalds (Oct 6, 2026) as the candidate who will "maintain the momentum" of DeSantis, keep Lake Okeechobee discharges out of the estuaries and send water south; Donalds promises to "finish" restoration. Mosaic's HB 167 (written by Mosaic) would replace strict liability with a negligence standard for radiation claims on former mine land; Mosaic gave more than $500K to state candidates and committees in the first nine months of 2025.

##### Cited Findings
- April 13, 2026: DeSantis announced a multi billion dollar agreement with the Army Corps for the state to lead EAA Reservoir construction, completing a 2025 MOU; expected completion 2029, which DeSantis says accelerates the project by five years. [WCTV, Apr 13, 2026](https://www.wctv.tv/2026/04/13/gov-desantis-announces-milestone-everglades-restoration-project/)
- April 15, 2026: Florida Crystals (Pepe Fanjul Jr., Gaston Cantens) commended the state and Corps for finalizing all EAA Reservoir contracts; says it supported the reservoir since CERP authorization, ended leases early in 2019 including one running to 2045, that EAA farmers paid roughly $350 million through the Agricultural Privilege Tax, have given up more than 100,000 acres, and that water leaving the basin is cleaner than Lake Okeechobee water; Cantens calls the reservoir "the final and most important project to restore the southern Everglades." The release makes no specific asks of the next governor. [Florida Crystals release via StreetInsider, Apr 15, 2026](https://www.streetinsider.com/PRNewswire/FLORIDA+CRYSTALS+COMMENDS+STATE+OF+FLORIDA,+ARMY+CORPS+FOR+PUSHING+EAA+RESERVOIR+TOWARD+COMPLETION/26319175.html)
- Oct 22, 2019: the Cabinet approved paying New Hope Sugar Co. (Florida Crystals subsidiary) $2.4 million ($1,940 per acre) to terminate a 1,234 acre state lease early, from the Everglades Trust Fund ($30M set aside by the 2017 law), to clear the EAA reservoir and stormwater area; state expected $16M in construction savings. [CBS12 / News Service of Florida, Oct 22, 2019](https://cbs12.com/news/local/state-approves-paying-sugar-grower-to-end-lease)
- Everglades Trust endorsement (announced at a Miami press conference the week of Oct 6, 2026): CEO Anna Upton said Donalds is "the only candidate who can maintain the momentum of Governor DeSantis when it comes to Everglades restoration," that "That water should not be discharged to our coastal estuaries," that Donalds "supported full funding for Everglades restoration his entire career," that he worked on a Lake Okeechobee regulation schedule that reduces discharges and sends water south, and that restoration returns four dollars per dollar. Running mate Bryan Avila was praised as a restoration supporter. Donalds pledged to finish restoration as governor. The article does not mention the EAA reservoir, sugar, or dollar amounts. [The Floridian, Oct 6, 2026](https://floridianpress.com/2026/10/everglades-trust-endorses-byron-donalds-for-governor/); also [Florida Politics](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/823412-byron-donalds-everglades-trust/) (not readable) and [Florida's Voice](https://flvoicenews.com/everglades-trust-endorses-donalds-who-vows-to-finish-restoration/)
- Donalds (Naples Daily News interview, reported Apr 30, 2025): central Everglades restoration could be finished in roughly 8 to 10 years; open to new technologies; managing water flow is essential for estuary health and state demand. Sugar is not mentioned. [Naples News Now, Apr 30, 2025](https://naples-news-now.beehiiv.com/p/byron-donalds-kicks-off-florida-governor-run-with-focus-on-everglades-school-choice-and-insurance-re)
- Mosaic's HB 167 (sponsors Rep. Lawrence McClure, R Dover, and Rep. Richard Gentry, R Astor; written by Mosaic) would require plaintiffs to prove negligence rather than strict liability under the Water Quality Assurance Act for elevated radiation on former phosphate land, in exchange for Mosaic recording a notice and requesting a Department of Health radiation survey; House Judiciary passed it in under 10 minutes in Nov 2025; on the House Special Order Calendar as of Jan 15, 2026; Senate President Ben Albritton reportedly supportive. Mosaic gave more than $500,000 to state candidates and committees in the first nine months of 2025 including over $100,000 to a Senate GOP leadership committee (per Jason Garcia); after McClure's 2023 phosphogypsum road bill passed, Mosaic paid $100,000 to sponsor a fundraiser for McClure's PAC Conservative Florida. About 50,000 Floridians live on former mining land; Mosaic reached a $2 billion EPA settlement in 2015. [Florida Phoenix, Jan 15, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/01/15/phosphate-giant-turns-to-florida-legislature-for-help-thwarting-lawsuits/)
- The 11th Circuit upheld EPA's approval of Mosaic's phosphogypsum test road in Polk County (Aug 2026, headline only). [Florida Phoenix, Aug 12, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/08/12/u-s-appeals-court-approves-test-of-radioactive-waste-as-road-paving-material/)
- Data centers and utilities: Donalds posted July 22, 2026 that SB 484 "ensures that utility rates will not go up due to data centers"; PolitiFact rated it Half True (law directs the PSC to "reasonably ensure" data centers bear their own costs, excludes co ops and municipals); spokesperson Gates McGavick said the commission must "ensure data centers pay their own cost of service." [WLRN / PolitiFact, Aug 11, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-11/donalds-says-fl-law-will-prevent-electric-bill-hikes-from-data-centers-thats-not-guaranteed). Donalds opposes state preemption banning data centers and wants a "ratepayer protection plan"; Jolly wants an immediate moratorium and a cap on regulated utility profits at the national average. [ClickOrlando voters guide, Oct 16, 2026](https://www.clickorlando.com/voters-guide/2026/10/16/2026-voters-guide-florida-governors-race/)

##### Inferences
- Florida Crystals' $500K to The Committee and provisional direct giving (round one) coincide with the state taking over EAA reservoir construction; the industry's public line is now "partner," so the practical asks of a Donalds administration are likely continuity (no new land acquisition south of the lake, keeping the 2019 lease deal as the last EAA land taking, continued BMP based regulation under the Everglades Forever Act, and a LOSOM schedule that stores rather than discharges). This is inference; no source lists sugar's asks explicitly.
- Everglades Trust's rationale is explicitly "continuity with DeSantis," which also aligns the environmental endorsement with the sugar industry's own framing of the reservoir as finished business. Environmental groups blasted the Trust's 2018 endorsement of DeSantis (Orlando Weekly headline), which suggests the Trust endorsement is contested within the movement; I did not find 2026 criticism.
- Mosaic's HB 167 outcome in the 2026 session was not confirmed; if it did not pass, it will return in 2027 under the next governor.

##### Gaps
- HB 167's final 2026 fate (passed, died, signed, vetoed) not confirmed.
- U.S. Sugar's 2026 contributions and public positions were not found.
- The "Bellinger lease" produced nothing in my sources; I cannot confirm what it refers to.
- Donalds has no documented statement on sugar, the Agricultural Privilege Tax, or EAA land purchases in anything I read.
- The Yahoo / USA Today Network piece "The complicated environmental legacy Florida's next governor stands to inherit" could not be fetched.

#### Key Question 5: Trump world, operatives, DeSantis relationship, transition and agency rumors

##### Takeaway
Donalds' operation is run by campaign manager Chris Hudson (ex Americans for Prosperity and Foundation for Government Accountability) and chief strategist Ryan Smith, with Bradley Crate as treasurer; outside pro Donalds TV came through American Mission (Leading the Future network, Andreessen Horowitz and Greg Brockman linked) using Strategic Media Placement and Summit Ridge Media Group. DeSantis endorsed the GOP slate including Donalds on Sept 2, 2026 after a year of trying to recruit a challenger and lobbying Ken Griffin; no transition team or agency head names have been reported in any source I read.

##### Cited Findings
- Chris Hudson, Bonita Springs consultant, is campaign manager: VP of Political Strategy at Presstige Printing and Ace Political; more than a decade at Americans for Prosperity including four years as VP of Government Affairs; former campaign manager for Speaker Dean Cannon; Central Florida Director for the Republican House Majority; previously at the Foundation for Government Accountability. [Florida Politics](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/738748-byron-donalds-brings-on-chris-hudson-as-campaign-manager/)
- Ryan Smith is Chief Strategist for the Donalds campaign; nine state senators endorsed: Bryan Avila, Alexis Calatayud, Nick DiCeglie, Joe Gruters (Feb 2025), Debbie Mayfield, Erin Grall, Gayle Harrell, Jonathan Martin, Ana Maria Rodriguez. [AOL / Floridian](https://www.aol.com/articles/nine-florida-state-senators-throw-193303923.html)
- Bradley Crate is treasurer of Donalds' House committee; Donalds transferred $1.2 million from the House campaign to Friends of Byron Donalds on Feb 26, 2025; the House committee kept raising money ($738,092 since 2025, $30,000 from PACs) after Donalds said he was not running; an FEC official said Aug 5, 2025 those contributions "must be refunded"; Crate replied Aug 6, 2025 that Donalds was "still an active candidate"; Donalds qualified for governor June 6, 2026 and Crate told the FEC on Aug 8, 2026 (article is internally inconsistent on the date) he was not running for House. Payments included $36,000 to an unnamed polling firm and $136,561.47 for "security services." [WLRN, Aug 20, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-08-20/byron-donalds-kept-house-re-election-campaign-alive-during-run-for-florida-governor)
- American Mission Florida (pro AI committee in the Leading the Future network funded by figures tied to Andreessen Horowitz and OpenAI's Greg Brockman) spent at least $500,000 on about 1,700 pro Donalds TV placements in Tampa Bay and Tallahassee in March and April 2026; contracts name Strategic Media Placement (an alias of The Strategy Group Co., Ohio); FEC filings show six figure payments to Summit Ridge Media Group LLC (Reno mailbox) in March and June 2026; Campaign Legal Center filed an FEC complaint in May 2026. [Hoodline, Jul 2, 2026, citing Miami Herald, ProPublica, Axios, TechCrunch](https://hoodline.com/2026/07/secretive-pro-ai-cash-fuels-byron-donalds-tv-blitz-across-florida/)
- Friends of Byron Donalds had raised more than $115 million by Sept 30, 2026 vs about $7 million for Jolly's Florida 2026. Immigration detention vendors owed money by FDEM for Operation Vigil Sentry ($417M across 592 invoices) have given: Access Restoration Services (parent of IRG Global, owed $68M+) more than $100,000; Carlos Duart and CDR entities more than $620,000 combined (CDR Companies owed $40M+; CDR Health already paid $5,184,400); The GEO Group $2.5 million (its JV GEO|CDR Baker owed nearly $40M); The Lemoine Company $59,000; Critical Response Strategies $10,000. GEO also gave $50,000 to Friends of James Uthmeier and $15,000 to Friends of Blaise Ingoglia, and lobbied on Donalds' H.R. 8430 (Reshape Alternatives to Detention Act) and H.R. 2. [WLRN / WUSF, Sep 30, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-09-30/immigration-detention-money-flows-florida-governors-race)
- DeSantis endorsed the GOP slate (Donalds, Uthmeier, Ingoglia, Simpson) at a Tampa forum on Sept 2, 2026: "I'm endorsing the slate here tonight." Tensions: the 2023 clash over Black history standards and DeSantis's 2025 statement that Donalds had not been part of Florida Republicans' victories. Donalds: "My mission as the state's next governor is to build on the success of Ron DeSantis." [WLRN, Sep 3, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-09-03/desantis-endorses-donalds-florida-governor-despite-past-tensions)
- Before the endorsement, DeSantis lobbied Ken Griffin and adviser Cason Carter to fund a Donalds challenger at a Miami dinner (with UF trustee chair Mori Hosseini and UM trustee chair Manny Kadre) and at a Fishers Island fundraiser, pitching Lt. Gov. Jay Collins; the dark money nonprofit Florida Fighters spent about $1.1M on Collins ads; Griffin's side said "Ken has not made any decision." [NBC News](https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/2026-election/ron-desantis-lobbying-megadonor-ken-griffin-stays-sidelines-florida-go-rcna244801) (Griffin later gave $10M per round one)
- DeSantis's team urged Florida lobbyists not to back Donalds in early 2025 (headline only). [NBC News](https://www.nbcnews.com/politics/elections/ron-desantis-florida-lobbyists-rep-byron-donalds-governor-rcna196996)
- Hope Florida: a leaked grand jury report (reported Sept 1, 2026) found "taxpayer money was misused for political purposes" in routing Medicaid settlement money (Jolly cites $10 million) through the Hope Florida Foundation to defeat the 2024 marijuana amendment, with testimony that then chief of staff James Uthmeier directed it; no indictments. Donalds: "We're going to make the necessary law changes so that this does not occur again," called findings "very concerning," but did not endorse the jury's recommendations and said it is not the campaign's focus. Jolly: "The Legislature should pick that up right away" and he would investigate further as governor. Uthmeier called it a hoax. [WLRN / WUSF, Sep 1, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-09-01/donalds-jolly-call-hope-florida-reforms-grand-jury-report)
- Donalds is campaigning alongside Uthmeier, Ingoglia, Simpson, Moody and Grady Judd on the bus tour; Uthmeier, Sen. Jim Boyd and Rep. Bill Conerly appeared with him in Sarasota Oct 22, 2026. [WLRN, Oct 19, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-10-19/byron-donalds-gubernatorial-campaign-launches-statewide-bus-tour-timed-with-early-voting); [WUSF, Oct 22, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-10-22/on-byron-donalds-campaign-stop-in-sarasota-crowd-cheers-for-keeping-florida-red)

##### Inferences
- Hudson's AFP and FGA pedigree (both Koch and Naples based limited government networks) plus the AFP Action endorsement (round one) and Erika's AFPI chair suggest a Donalds policy shop drawn from AFP, FGA and AFPI rather than from the DeSantis executive office; this is inference, not reported.
- The DeSantis "lobbyist freeze" appears to have ended de facto with the Sept 2 endorsement and joint campaigning with Uthmeier, Ingoglia and Simpson, but no source reports an explicit lift or names donors who moved.
- Detention vendor giving (GEO $2.5M, CDR $620K+) plus $417M in unpaid FDEM invoices means FDEM and its vendor payments are an immediate pressure point for the next governor.

##### Gaps
- No reporting found on a transition team, chief of staff, or candidates for DEP, DOE, AHCA, DEM or PSC. Searches for these returned nothing specific; Susie Wiles' Florida network and Rick Scott appointees who back Donalds were not documented beyond the Scott endorsement in round one.
- Ryan Smith's background could not be researched before the search budget ran out.
- The Donalds campaign's own media buyer and general consultant for the $20M ad blitz (Florida Politics, Mar 2026) could not be read because floridapolitics.com fetches failed.
- The current status of the Campaign Legal Center FEC complaint against American Mission is unknown.

#### Key Question 6: "DOGE Florida," budget, property tax, Medicaid, universities, and who has analyzed consequences

##### Takeaway
Donalds' public fiscal agenda is property tax relief (now backing Amendment 3, promising "full and complete reform of property taxes and fees" in 2027, earlier floating a $300K to $400K homestead exemption), expanding Florida DOGE audits with "Amber alert style" texts, hospital price transparency, insurance deregulation and lawsuit limits, and outcome based university funding; he has not published a spending cut list or state workforce target in any source read. Florida Policy Institute puts Amendment 3's cost to local budgets at $12 billion.

##### Cited Findings
- Donalds would expand Florida DOGE audits and send "Amber alert style texts" when a new audit is available; supports Amendment 3; would push "full and complete reform of property taxes and fees" next year; has said he would raise the homestead exemption from $50,000 to $300,000 to $400,000 and warned that eliminating property taxes could raise sales taxes and hurt tourism; would cut insurance costs by reforming the regulatory framework and preventing "lawsuit abuse," claiming a potential 20% rate cut; would require hospital and doctor price transparency and count out of network payments toward deductibles. [ClickOrlando voters guide, Oct 16, 2026](https://www.clickorlando.com/voters-guide/2026/10/16/2026-voters-guide-florida-governors-race/)
- Amendment 3 raises the homestead exemption from $50,000 to $150,000 in 2027 and $250,000 in 2028; Florida Policy Institute cites a "$12 billion hole in local budgets" and warns of cuts to fire rescue, disaster response, hospitals, parks and libraries; a judge struck the original ballot summary Aug 3, 2026 and the AG revised it. [Central Florida Public Media, Aug 14, 2026](https://www.cfpublic.org/text/politics/2026-08-14/property-tax-amendment-revision)
- At the Sunshine Economy Summit (Sept 23, 2026) Donalds backed Amendment 3 plus a state formula for fiscally constrained counties; opposed Jolly's catastrophe fund ("Why would we expand the rainy day fund?") and wants to eliminate the Cat Fund surcharge; said data center developers should cover the energy costs they create. [CBS Miami, Sep 23, 2026](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/florida-governor-byron-donalds-david-jolly-fort-lauderdale-summit/)
- Fact checks: Donalds' August ad claiming Jolly wants to raise taxes rated False (PolitiFact Sept 14); his "$1,000 hurricane tax" claim lacks context (FSU study of Aug 31 did not examine Jolly's plan; the charge would apply only after a catastrophic storm). [WUFT, Oct 16, 2026](https://www.wuft.org/politics/2026-10-16/fact-checking-claims-from-florida-governor-candidates-david-jolly-and-byron-donalds)
- Donalds in April 2025 proposed overhauling healthcare with "cost effective, tech driven solutions" partly funded by the budget surplus. [Naples News Now, Apr 30, 2025](https://naples-news-now.beehiiv.com/p/byron-donalds-kicks-off-florida-governor-run-with-focus-on-everglades-school-choice-and-insurance-re)
- Universities: see Key Question 1 (FAMUAN interview, outcome based funding).
- Context: DeSantis created Florida DOGE in February 2025 to cut positions and audit universities and local governments (headlines only). [Local 10, Feb 25, 2025](https://www.local10.com/news/politics/2025/02/25/desantis-creates-florida-doge-task-force); [AOL / USA Today](https://www.aol.com/gov-ron-desantis-establishes-florida-184107220.html)

##### Inferences
- "Expand DOGE audits" plus Amendment 3 plus a promise of further property tax reform in 2027 implies the state would need to backfill or let local governments absorb a $12B hit (FPI figure); Donalds' only stated backfill is a formula for fiscally constrained counties.
- No Donalds position on Medicaid expansion was found; given his federal votes and the campaign's silence, the safe characterization is that he has not proposed expansion and opposes Jolly's plan by implication only.

##### Gaps
- No source lists what Donalds would cut, a state workforce target, Medicaid changes, or university budget cuts. The Floridian piece "Donalds Reaffirms Commitment to Repeal Homestead Property Taxes" could not be fetched (rate limited).
- No Florida TaxWatch or FPI analysis specific to the Donalds agenda was found; FPI's work is on Amendment 3.

#### Key Question 7: Jolly's contrasting agenda, briefly

##### Takeaway
Jolly would reintroduce means testing to vouchers and impose reporting and standards on schools taking choice money, raise teacher pay "not 3% but 30%" through a 10 year plan funded partly by tourism taxes, expand Medicaid with a 90% federal match and a community health center pilot, impose a data center moratorium and cap utility profits, create a state catastrophe wind fund, oppose Amendment 3, and pursue the Hope Florida grand jury recommendations.

##### Cited Findings
- Vouchers: reintroduce means testing; reform of the program a campaign focus; he used vouchers for his own children's religious schooling; teacher pay "not 3% but 30%"; 10 year public education "renaissance" funded by diverting tourism development dollars; day one reversal of DeSantis education executive orders. [WUSF, Dec 19, 2025](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2025-12-19/democratic-gubernatorial-hopeful-david-jolly-spotlights-education-sarasota)
- Choice accountability: "accountability is necessary for balance"; semi annual reporting to districts; enforce state standards on schools taking choice funds; 10 year education plan; restore home rule over property taxes; state catastrophic fund removing hurricane coverage from the private market; fully fund Sadowski; expand Medicaid and primary care via FQHCs; immediate data center moratorium; cap regulated utility profits at the national average. [ClickOrlando voters guide, Oct 16, 2026](https://www.clickorlando.com/voters-guide/2026/10/16/2026-voters-guide-florida-governors-race/)
- Medicaid: expansion covering roughly 266,000 adults in the coverage gap, 90% federal match, a five year community health center pilot in every county, trigger provisions if the match drops; no total cost given. [davidjolly.com](https://davidjolly.com/issues/health-care/)
- Budget: "There's $2 billion to reduce people's rent, scale up health costs, more public schools and more teachers paid more." [CBS Miami, Sep 23, 2026](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/florida-governor-byron-donalds-david-jolly-fort-lauderdale-summit/)
- Hope Florida: wants the Legislature to adopt the grand jury recommendations immediately and would investigate as governor. [WLRN, Sep 1, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-09-01/donalds-jolly-call-hope-florida-reforms-grand-jury-report)
- Jolly's wind fund savings claims (60% to 70%) were judged unlikely by fact checkers. [WUFT, Oct 16, 2026](https://www.wuft.org/politics/2026-10-16/fact-checking-claims-from-florida-governor-candidates-david-jolly-and-byron-donalds)

##### Inferences
- On the networks in this report: a Jolly win would put means testing and SB 318 style controls on the ESA pipeline (reducing the addressable market for Optima style vendors), a data center moratorium against American Mission's donors and the detention and utility donors' interests, and a reopened Hope Florida inquiry touching Uthmeier. No Jolly position on sugar or the EAA reservoir was found.

##### Gaps
- Jolly's Everglades and sugar positions were not retrieved (search budget exhausted).
- The Tampa Bay Times recommendation (Oct 16, 2026) could not be read past the paywall; which candidate it endorsed is not recorded here.

### Note: utilities_developers_insurance_second_hop.md

_Folder: Donalds second hop who gains_

### Second hop: utilities, data centers, developers, insurance and property tax money behind Byron Donalds, what each wants and what the governor controls

Method note for the writer: In this session WebFetch hit its hourly cap on every attempt and direct HTTPS downloads were refused by the egress proxy, so I could not read full article text. Every finding below is drawn from search result titles, URLs and publication dates, cross-checked across several outlets where possible. Where I state a figure or a date it is in the headline or URL of the cited piece. Items that need the article body to confirm are listed under Gaps. Round one facts supplied by the coordinator are treated as established and not re-sourced here.

#### Key question 1: FPL, Duke and TECO. The 2025 rate case, the PSC, ROE reform bills, and what the utilities want next

##### Takeaway
The PSC unanimously approved FPL's roughly $7 billion settlement on Nov 20, 2025, the largest electric rate increase in state history by Seeking Rents' accounting, and the fight moved to the Florida Supreme Court. All five commissioners are the governor's appointees, DeSantis reappointed two in 2026, and the 2026 Legislature advanced only an "affordability" tweak to PSC rate criteria rather than a hard ROE cap. FPL's next ask is large load: NextEra raised its FPL data center forecast to 8 GW by 2032 and its CEO said the first large load deals were likely in 2026.

##### Cited Findings
- The PSC approved FPL's settlement on Nov 20, 2025, described as a contentious $7 billion rate hike, [Florida Phoenix, Nov 20, 2025](https://floridaphoenix.com/2025/11/20/psc-approves-contentious-7-billion-rate-hike-for-florida-power-light-customers/); [Central Florida Public Media, Nov 20, 2025](https://www.cfpublic.org/politics/2025-11-20/psc-oks-fpl-deal-that-increases-base-rate-in-parts-of-florida); [WUSF, Nov 20, 2025](https://www.wusf.org/economy-business/2025-11-20/state-regulators-approve-florida-power-light-rate-settlement)
- The vote was unanimous, [E&E News](https://www.eenews.net/articles/psc-unanimously-approves-florida-power-light-rate-hike-settlement/)
- Seeking Rents characterized it as the largest electric rate increase in history and published a how-it-happened account, [Seeking Rents](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/florida-just-approved-the-largest)
- The approval set up a challenge at the Florida Supreme Court over 2026 to 2027 rates, [CBS Miami](https://www.cbsnews.com/miami/news/florida-fpl-energy-bill-new-rates-2026-2027/)
- In August 2025 the PSC granted FPL and "key stakeholders" time to finalize a settlement; FPL's own framing was that bills stay "well below national average", [FPL newsroom, Aug 11, 2025](https://newsroom.fpl.com/2025-08-11-State-regulators-grant-time-for-FPL-and-key-stakeholders-to-finalize-settlement-that-keeps-customer-bills-well-below-national-average)
- Settlement and related filings are on the PSC docket, e.g. documents 14991-2025 and 15001-2025 filed Nov 10, 2025, [Florida PSC filing 14991-2025](https://www.floridapsc.com/pscfiles/library/filings/2025/14991-2025/14991-2025.pdf); [Florida PSC filing 15001-2025](https://www.floridapsc.com/pscfiles/library/filings/2025/15001-2025/15001-2025.pdf)
- Vote Solar's analysis argued the settlement would raise bills if approved, [Vote Solar](https://votesolar.org/if-approved-florida-power-lights-rate-case-settlement-will-lead-to-higher-utility-bills/)
- Governor DeSantis announced the reappointment of two PSC commissioners in 2026; FMEA and NewsRadio identified them as Gary Clark and Gabriella Passidomo-Smith, [Governor's office, 2026](https://www.flgov.com/eog/news/press/2026/governor-ron-desantis-announced-reappointment-two-florida-public-service-commission); [FMEA](https://www.flpublicpower.com/news/clark-passidomo-smith-reappointed-to-psc); [NewsRadio 92.3](https://www.newsradio923.com/post/desantis-reappoints-clark-passidomo-smith-to-florida-public-service-commission)
- The PSC Nominating Council is a joint legislative committee (Online Sunshine lists it under committees/joint/pscnc); the Council screens applicants and the governor appoints from its list, [Florida Legislature, PSC Nominating Council page](https://www.leg.state.fl.us/cgi-bin/View_Page.pl?File=index.cfm&Directory=committees%2Fjoint%2Fpscnc%2F)
- A Florida Politics poll found voters don't trust the PSC and blame shifts "hard" once they learn the governor picks its members, [Florida Politics](https://floridapolitics.com/archives/818926-poll-voters-dont-trust-the-psc-and-blame-shifts-hard-once-they-learn-gov-desantis-picks-its-members/)
- On Dec 9, 2025 a Senate proposal to reform how the PSC sets rates advanced; it would add "affordability" to the criteria the regulator must consider, [Florida Phoenix, Dec 9, 2025](https://floridaphoenix.com/2025/12/09/proposal-to-reform-how-the-florida-psc-sets-energy-rates-advances-in-senate/); [WMNF](https://www.wmnf.org/?p=250410)
- SB 126 (2026) by Sen. Gaetz is the Senate PSC reform vehicle; a committee amendment and bill analysis exist, [SB 126 filed text](https://www.flsenate.gov/Session/Bill/2026/126/BillText/Filed/PDF); [SB 126 committee amendment](https://www.flsenate.gov/Session/Bill/2026/126/Amendment/132210/PDF); [SB 126 analysis](https://www.flsenate.gov/Session/Bill/2026/126/Analyses/2026s00126.pre.ri.PDF)
- Sen. Carlos Guillermo Smith filed a separate 2026 bill aimed at lowering electricity rates long term, endorsed by clean energy advocates, [Food and Water Watch, Jan 13, 2026](https://www.foodandwaterwatch.org/2026/01/13/fl-sen-guillermo-smith-introduces-bill-to-lower-electricity-rates-in-long-term/); [Central Florida Public Media, Jan 20, 2026](https://www.cfpublic.org/politics/2026-01-20/a-bill-to-make-florida-electricity-more-affordable-is-being-endorsed-by-clean-energy-advocates)
- Donalds and Jolly publicly clashed over how to respond to utility profitability on June 23, 2026, [Florida Phoenix, June 23, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/06/23/donalds-and-jolly-bicker-over-response-to-utility-profitability/)
- The Jolly campaign republished an AOL/wire piece asking how the candidates would address utilities after the FPL hike, [davidjolly.com](https://davidjolly.com/after-fpl-rate-hike-how-would-governor-candidates-address-utilities/)
- NextEra raised its FPL data center load forecast to 8 GW by 2032, [Data Center Dynamics](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/nextera-raises-data-center-forecast-to-8gw-by-2032-at-florida-subsidiary-fpl/); [Motley Fool, July 31, 2026](https://www.fool.com/investing/2026/07/31/nextera-just-raised-its-large-load-forecast-to-8-g/)
- NextEra's CEO said FPL was "very likely" to announce its first large load deals in 2026, [T&D World](https://www.tdworld.com/utility-business/news/55353842/ceo-fpl-very-likely-to-announce-first-large-load-deals-this-year)
- FPL published a Q&A with its president on expected data center growth and "new customer protections", [FPL newsroom](https://newsroom.fpl.com/Q-A-with-FPL-President-on-expected-data-center-growth-and-the-companys-new-customer-protections)
- Duke Energy Florida's multiyear base rate settlement was approved Aug 2024 covering through 2027, [Spectrum News 13, Aug 21, 2024](https://mynews13.com/fl/orlando/news/2024/08/21/duke-energy-rate-increase-request-approval); [WUSF, Aug 22, 2024](https://www.wusf.org/economy-business/2024-08-22/florida-commission-approves-duke-energy-settlement-increase-base-electric-rates); [Duke Energy](https://www.duke-energy.com/home/billing/def-rates-2024)
- The PSC approved TECO and Duke storm cost recovery for $1.55 billion in hurricane costs; Food and Water Watch described it as a $20 to $40 per month hike, [Fox 13](https://www.fox13news.com/news/teco-duke-looking-raise-rates-recoup-1-55b-hurricane-costs); [Food and Water Watch, Feb 4, 2025](https://www.foodandwaterwatch.org/2025/02/04/desantis-commission-approves-20-40-month-teco-duke-rate-hike/)
- Hillsborough residents called on the PSC to reject TECO's 2024 base rate request, [Florida Phoenix, June 13, 2024](https://floridaphoenix.com/2024/06/13/hillsborough-county-citizens-call-on-florida-psc-to-reject-teco-rate-increase-proposal/)

##### Inferences
- The governor's leverage over utility profits runs through PSC appointments (all five seats, from a legislatively controlled nominating council list), the Office of Public Counsel's posture, and whether he signs or vetoes ROE legislation. Donalds' stated opposition to an ROE cap (round one) means the only live constraint on a repeat of the 2025 outcome would be the Legislature's "affordability" criterion if it became law, which is far weaker than a cap.
- FPL's large load pivot means the next governor's PSC will decide who pays for 8 GW of data center generation: a separate large load rate class and contract minimums protect residential ratepayers only if the PSC enforces them. The utility donors and the data center donors in round one therefore want the same thing from the PSC: fast approval of new gas and nuclear capacity with cost recovery, and tariff terms the hyperscalers will sign.
- Duke's current settlement expires after 2027, so Duke's next base rate case lands on the next governor's PSC in his first term. Same for TECO's next cycle. That is the concrete "what they want": a PSC that repeats the 2024 and 2025 settlement pattern.

##### Gaps
- Could not read the settlement to confirm the ROE midpoint and range, the by-year dollar increases, which intervenors signed and which opposed (Office of Public Counsel, Florida Rising, LULAC, EVgo, Walmart, FEA etc.), and the data center or large load tariff provisions. The PSC filings 14991-2025 and 15001-2025 should contain this.
- Could not confirm whether SB 126 passed, was amended into a different vehicle, or died, nor whether any bill actually proposed a numeric ROE cap. The Senate bill page for SB 126 and SB 1532 should be checked.
- Did not find a source on the specific text of what Donalds said on June 23, 2026 beyond the headline that he and Jolly bickered over utility profitability.
- No source retrieved on FPL's 2025 to 2026 political committee giving network beyond round one totals, or on the CLEO Institute report itself (search returned no CLEO link).
- No source retrieved on which PSC seats expire in 2027 to 2030, which determines how many seats Donalds would fill in a first term.

#### Key question 2: Data centers. Project Tango, other hyperscale proposals, approvals, 2026 legislation, and the candidates' positions

##### Takeaway
Project Tango, a proposed roughly 37 million square foot AI campus in the Loxahatchee area of Palm Beach County, was recommended for denial by the county zoning board on July 2 to 3, 2026 and rejected by county commissioners on July 16, 2026, though a prior data center entitlement on the site survived; the county also passed a data center moratorium. Data center interests have put about $30 million into Florida campaigns by Seeking Rents' count, Donalds opposes a statewide moratorium, and Jolly says a complete ban should be on the table.

##### Cited Findings
- Officials gave initial approval to a 37 million square foot data center campus in Florida (Project Tango), [Data Center Dynamics](https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/news/37-million-sq-ft-data-center-campus-gets-initial-ok-from-officials-in-florida/)
- On May 7, 2026 dueling applications threw Project Tango into disarray, [Stet News, May 7, 2026](https://stetnews.org/2026/05/07/project-tango-dueling-applications-throw-data-center-project-into-disarray/)
- On June 10, 2026 the Palm Beach County school board pushed back over proximity to an elementary school (Saddle View), [WLRN, June 10, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/education/2026-06-10/project-tango-data-center-palm-beach-school-saddle-view)
- Earthjustice filed a letter on behalf of the Western Palm Beach County Civic Association for the June 30, 2026 BCC and zoning hearings, [Palm Beach County PZB, June 30, 2026](https://discover.pbc.gov/pzb/zoning/Hearings-Meetings-ZC/G-1_Earthjustice%20Letter%20for%20WPBCA%20re%20Project%20Tango%20Zoning%20and%20BCC%20Hearings_6-30-2026.pdf)
- The county zoning commission voted against Project Tango on July 2, 2026 and the recommendation of denial was reported July 3, [WLRN, July 2, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/development/2026-07-02/ai-data-center-project-tango-palm-beach); [Stet News, July 3, 2026](https://stetnews.org/2026/07/03/zoning-board-denies-project-tango-ai-data-center/); [CBS12](https://cbs12.com/news/local/project-tango-setback-palm-beach-county-zoning-board-recommends-denial-concerned-residents-environmental-impact-central-park-commerce-center-florida-news)
- Palm Beach County passed an AI data center moratorium on July 7, 2026 but kept the Project Tango vote on the calendar, [WFLX, July 7, 2026](https://www.wflx.com/2026/07/07/palm-beach-county-passes-ai-data-center-moratorium-project-tango-vote-is-still/); [WPTV](https://www.wptv.com/news/region-c-palm-beach-county/loxahatchee-acreage/palm-beach-county-passes-ai-data-center-moratorium-but-project-tango-vote-is-still-on)
- County commissioners rejected Project Tango on July 16, 2026 as "not compatible," but data center development remained approved for the site under existing entitlements, [WLRN, July 16, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/government-politics/2026-07-16/project-tango-ai-data-center-palm-beach-rejected); [WPTV](https://www.wptv.com/news/region-c-palm-beach-county/loxahatchee-acreage/palm-beach-county-commissioners-reject-project-tango-but-data-center-development-still-approved-for-site); [The Real Deal, July 17, 2026](https://therealdeal.com/miami/2026/07/17/palm-beach-county-rejects-data-center-proposal/)
- The developer revised its plan ahead of the vote, [CBS12](https://cbs12.com/news/local/project-tango-developer-plan-revised-for-controversial-ai-data-center-ahead-of-vote)
- WLRN's Oct 1, 2026 investigation examined the land's "fraught history" and why residents wanted real information; the site is listed under PBA Holdings (Palm Beach Aggregates) in industry databases, [WLRN, Oct 1, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/wlrn-investigations/2026-10-01/ai-project-tango-florida); [WUSF, Oct 1, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-10-01/residents-wanted-real-information-on-project-tango-the-lands-fraught-history-explains-why); [Baxtel PBA Holdings](https://baxtel.com/data-centers/pba-holdings); [Baxtel Project Tango](https://baxtel.com/data-center/project-tango)
- Seeking Rents reported data center donors had spent more than $20 million in Florida ahead of 2026, later updated to $30 million as Election Day approached, [Seeking Rents, $20M](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/data-center-donors-have-spent-more); [Seeking Rents, $30M](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/data-center-interests-have-put-30)
- Politico covered the data center and AI donors powering Donalds' bid, [Politico via NewsBreak](https://www.newsbreak.com/politico-560779/4782517602692-the-data-center-and-ai-donors-powering-donalds-bid-for-florida-governor)
- An opposition site, datacenterdonalds.com, frames the issue as "Big Tech's bill, your backyard", [datacenterdonalds.com](https://datacenterdonalds.com/)
- Donalds opposes a statewide moratorium on data centers (July 25, 2026), [WUWF, July 25, 2026](https://www.wuwf.org/florida-news/2026-07-25/byron-donalds-opposes-a-statewide-moratorium-on-data-centers)
- A headline described Donalds as rejecting data centers while cashing AI contributions, [Ground News aggregation](https://ground.news/article/byron-donalds-rejects-data-centers-while-cashing-in-ai-contributions_4eca57)
- Jolly, the Democratic nominee, pushed a data center moratorium and said Sept 18, 2026 that a complete ban "should be on the table", [E&E News](https://www.eenews.net/articles/dem-front-runner-in-florida-governor-race-pushes-for-data-center-moratorium/); [Florida Phoenix, Sept 18, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/09/18/david-jolly-a-complete-ban-on-data-centers-in-florida-should-be-on-the-table/)
- Jolly and running mate Gwen Graham discussed data center impacts in Panama City on Aug 28, 2026, [WJHG, Aug 28, 2026](https://www.wjhg.com/2026/08/28/democratic-candidates-david-jolly-gwen-graham-discuss-ai-data-center-impacts-during-panama-city-visit/)
- Jolly blasted Donalds in Orlando over data center support, [The Derrick / wire](https://www.thederrick.com/news/entertainment/david-jolly-in-orlando-blasts-byron-donalds-over-support-for-data-centers/article_73378862-2898-5d8a-ac4a-4d0283e77e18.html)
- WPTV: data centers divide the governor's race as voters question the AI boom, [WPTV](https://www.wptv.com/news/politics/data-centers-divide-florida-governors-race-as-voters-question-the-ai-boom)
- 2025 HB 7031 ended the sales tax exemption for data centers under 100 MW, [Greenberg Traurig, June 2025](https://www.gtlaw.com/en/insights/2025/6/florida-legislature-passes-bill-to-end-sales-tax-exemption-for-sub-100-mw-data-centers); [Ryan](https://ryan.com/ca/fr-ca/perspectives/florida-hb-7031-eliminates-sales-tax-exemption-for-data-centers-under-100-megawatts)
- In 2026 DeSantis signed a law framed as protecting Floridians from subsidizing data centers, [Governor's office, 2026](https://www.flgov.com/eog/news/press/2026/governor-ron-desantis-signs-law-protect-floridians-subsidizing-data-centers)
- Hoodline reported DeSantis blasting Big Tech while Florida quietly cut data center tax deals (June 2026), [Hoodline, June 2026](https://hoodline.com/2026/06/desantis-blasts-big-tech-while-florida-quietly-cuts-data-center-tax-deals/)
- In the GOP primary, fact checks examined whether Jay Collins sponsored permanent data center tax breaks (July 13, 2026) and a Paul Renner claim (July 9, 2026), [WUSF, July 13, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-07-13/fl-governors-race-did-jay-collins-sponsor-a-bill-giving-permanent-tax-breaks-to-ai-data-centers); [PolitiFact, July 9, 2026](https://www.politifact.com/factchecks/2026/jul/09/paul-renner/florida-AI-data-centers-tax-breaksgovernors-race/)
- Florida Data Centers tracker maps two projects in Palm Beach County, [floridadatacenters.org](https://floridadatacenters.org/palm-beach-county)

##### Inferences
- The state hooks for a project like Tango are: PSC approval of the FPL generation and transmission to serve it (and the large load tariff), DEP and water management district permits (the Loxahatchee site is adjacent to wetlands and the mine history WLRN flagged), state sales tax exemption eligibility for 100 MW plus facilities, and any statewide preemption of local moratoria. A governor who opposes a statewide moratorium and appoints DEP, the water management district boards and the PSC can clear every state level gate even when a county says no, which is why the Palm Beach Aggregates and Phillips money (round one) is a bet on the governor rather than the county.
- The 2025 to 2026 pattern (HB 7031 narrowing the exemption to 100 MW plus, then a 2026 "protect ratepayers" law) suggests the industry's 2027 ask is to lock in the large facility exemption and defeat ratepayer cost allocation rules, not to seek new subsidies.

##### Gaps
- Could not read WLRN's Oct 1 investigation to name the landowners, the Central Park Commerce Center entitlement history, the acreage and MW, or the FPL interconnection status.
- Could not confirm the 2026 ratepayer law's bill number and provisions (whether it creates a data center rate class or just requires cost of service studies).
- Did not retrieve Donalds' exact statements beyond "opposes statewide moratorium" and the Ground News aggregation headline.
- No source retrieved on other named Florida hyperscale proposals (e.g. in Bay, Polk, Marion or Duval counties) in this session.

#### Key question 3: Developers. Growth management, impact fees, Live Local, rural boundaries, Hosseini, and what a Donalds DEP and DEO would mean

##### Takeaway
The 2025 to 2026 Legislatures delivered the homebuilders' agenda incrementally: a 2025 bill to undo county rural boundaries cleared committee and Seminole County sued; a 2026 law "makes it easier to pave over farmland" (Florida Trident, July 1, 2026); and by Oct 13, 2026 WGCU reported a major home developer eyeing rural Florida with local officials saying there is little they can do. Mori Hosseini remains chair of the UF trustees after DeSantis reappointed him June 9, 2025 and was at the center of the UF presidential search power struggle.

##### Cited Findings
- A Florida bill that would undo county rural boundaries cleared its first committee March 20, 2025, [WUSF, Mar 20, 2025](https://wusf.org/politics-issues/2025-03-20/florida-bill-that-would-undo-rural-boundaries-clears-first-committee)
- Seminole County challenged the state bill threatening its rural boundary (Aug 4, 2025), [Spectrum News 13, Aug 4, 2025](https://mynews13.com/fl/orlando/news/2025/08/04/county-challenges-state-bill-that-could-threaten-rural-boundary)
- A new 2026 law makes it easier to pave over Florida farmland, [Florida Trident, July 1, 2026](https://floridatrib.org/2026/07/01/new-law-makes-it-easier-to-pave-over-florida-farmland/)
- Bisnow: new laws on impact fees benefit developers and may hurt local governments, [Bisnow](https://www.bisnow.com/news/south-florida/commercial-real-estate/florida-impact-fees-legislature-108824)
- A major home developer is eyeing rural Florida and local officials say there is little they can do (Oct 13, 2026), [WGCU, Oct 13, 2026](https://www.wgcu.org/government-politics/2026-10-13/major-home-developer-eyeing-rural-florida-local-officials-say-theres-little-they-can-do)
- Seeking Rents covered DeSantis and lawmakers on growth legislation, [Seeking Rents](https://jasongarcia.substack.com/p/ron-desantis-and-florida-lawmakers)
- Audubon Florida's advocate newsletters track the growth management bills, [Audubon Florida](https://www.audubon.org/florida/policy/read-our-advocate-newsletters)
- DeSantis reappointed Mori Hosseini and Rahul Patel (reported as "Ridley" in one headline) to the UF Board of Trustees on June 9, 2025 amid the presidential search controversy, [Governor's office, 2025](https://www.flgov.com/eog/news/press/2025/governor-ron-desantis-reappoints-two-university-florida-board-trustees-0); [WCJB, June 9, 2025](https://www.wcjb.com/2025/06/09/gov-desantis-reappoints-university-florida-trustees-amid-presidential-search-controversy/); [Mainstreet Daily News](https://www.mainstreetdailynews.com/education/appoint-hosseini-uf-trustees)
- Hosseini's reappointment went to Senate executive appointments in 2026, [Florida Senate executive appointment 4147](https://flsenate.gov/Session/ExecutiveAppointments/Show/2026/4147)
- A power struggle delayed UF's presidential search (July 6, 2026), [Florida Phoenix, July 6, 2026](https://floridaphoenix.com/2026/07/06/power-struggle-delayed-the-university-of-floridas-presidential-search/)
- Laura Loomer attacked Hosseini's "UF power play" and DeSantis backing Stuart Bell for president, [Loomer Substack](https://lauraloomer.substack.com/p/desantis-and-his-top-donor-endorse)

##### Inferences
- What the homebuilder donors (Hosseini, Neal, Hutson, Davis family, Beruff, Dream Finders) want from the governor is executive, not just legislative: DEP and the five water management district boards issue wetlands and consumptive use permits; the governor appoints the Florida Housing Finance Corporation board that allocates Live Local money; the governor appoints the Commerce Department (formerly DEO) secretary who reviews comprehensive plan amendments and rural boundary disputes; and the governor signs or vetoes impact fee and sprawl bills. A Donalds administration continuing the 2025 to 2026 trajectory would mean fewer state objections to plan amendments and faster permitting.
- Hosseini's leverage is personal (UF chair, DeSantis confidant) and a Donalds win preserves his board seat through the next appointment cycle; a Jolly win likely does not.

##### Gaps
- Did not retrieve bill numbers or sponsors for the 2026 farmland or rural boundary law or the impact fee changes, nor whether DeSantis vetoed any sprawl bill.
- No source retrieved this session on Pat Neal, Hutson, the Davis family's Nocatee, Beruff or Dream Finders' specific 2026 asks or projects.
- Did not find a source on Donalds' own growth management or Live Local positions.

#### Key question 4: Amendment 3 and homestead elimination. What it does, the money, revenue loss, who benefits, and what Jolly proposes

##### Takeaway
Florida Realtors committed $10 million to Yes On 3 on Sept 9, 2026 and by WPTV's October accounting had poured $18 million in, with supporters outspending opponents 26 to 1. A tax policy group's estimate puts the cost at $12 billion a year to local governments; sheriffs (Grady Judd: "a train wreck," Aug 4, 2026), fire chiefs, county administrators and the Sierra Club oppose it, while the Miami-Dade sheriff backs it. Donalds says he will push again for relief if it fails, has narrowed the scope of his fallback plan over a "math" problem, and has said fiscally constrained counties should not have to "sing for supper."

##### Cited Findings
- Florida Realtors backed Amendment 3 and committed $10 million on Sept 9, 2026, [WLRN, Sept 9, 2026](https://www.wlrn.org/business/2026-09-09/florida-realtors-group-drops-10-million-to-pass-floridas-property-tax-cut-amendment); [WUSF, Sept 10, 2026](https://www.wusf.org/politics-issues/2026-09-10/florida-realtors-trade-group-backs-proposed-property-tax-cut-amendment); [Florida Trend / News Service of Florida, Sept 10, 2026](https://www.floridatrend.com/articles/2026/09/10/realtors-back-property-tax-cut-measure/)
- WPTV Investigates: Amendment 3 supporters outspent opponents 26 to 1 as Florida Realtors poured $18 million into the race, [WPTV Investigates](https://www.wptv.com/wptv-investigates/amendment-3-supporters-outspent-opponents-26-to-1-as-florida-realtors-poured-18-million-into-the-race)
